*Source Module*: `sep_lcc_lc_state_w1s_prod_test`, `sep_lcc_lc_state_w1s_prod_demote_test`, `sep_lcc_lc_state_w1s_rma_sip_test`, `sep_lcc_lc_state_w1s_rma_chiplet_test`, `sep_lcc_lc_state_w1s_prod_end_test`, `sep_lcc_lc_state_w1s_transient_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, LCC golden

*Leaves*: `sep_lcc_lc_state_w1s_prod_test`, `sep_lcc_lc_state_w1s_prod_demote_test`, `sep_lcc_lc_state_w1s_rma_sip_test`, `sep_lcc_lc_state_w1s_rma_chiplet_test`, `sep_lcc_lc_state_w1s_prod_end_test`, `sep_lcc_lc_state_w1s_transient_test` — one module, one sensed starting state per leaf, selected by `+lc_start` and staged at t=0 by `cocotb/dv_sim_prestage.py`.

*Objective*: Prove the next lifecycle state computed on the frontdoor shadow write. This is the second writer of the lifecycle state and the stitch walk does not reach it: that walk programs a one-time-programmable bit and re-senses, which never evaluates this logic. The contract is the specification's three rules — write-one-to-set, a don't-care low bit inside each return-material-authorisation state, and the two hardware-ordered token gates — from which the reachable destinations follow.

*Test Coverage*: Frontdoor `LC_STATE` write-one-to-set, token gates, and the six starting-state leaves.

*Why one leaf per starting state*: the one-time-programmable image is deposited at time zero and a fuse holds its state across every reset, so a test cannot re-sense its way to a different starting state; from one image only a write-one-to-set superset is reachable. Each starting state is therefore its own leaf, with the sensed value read off the device and required to match what the leaf asked for.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Sense the leaf's starting state from a real fuse image; require the sensed lifecycle value to be the one requested, and the security-disable override to be inactive so no feature-control check can pass by being forced to all-ones.
. Present the return-material-authorisation tokens the cell needs, and require each to reach a match code before relying on it — an unmatched token would let a gate check pass for the wrong reason.
. Write the lifecycle word write-one-to-set, and require both the differentially encoded lifecycle word and the controller's decoded feature-control output to match the specification-derived golden for the expected next state.
. Re-check the observed step against the transition rules in value form, using the code the device returned rather than the one the test wrote.
. On the demoted leaf, assert both demotion registers first and prove the demotion is visible in feature control before requiring the transition to succeed.
. On the production leaf, present a valid SIP token with the comparator collapsed; the bit must not set. Release the fault and the same write then advances.
. On the transient leaf, require the state to hold with the enable armed and no token, then present the token and take no bus write at all; the same rules must apply.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-W1S-NO-CLEAR
|A write of zero to the lifecycle word clears nothing. Write-one-to-set means a bit never returns to 0, so a controller that treated the write as an assignment fails here.
|CHK-GATE-SIP
|The system-in-package bit does not set without a matching token. The write is otherwise identical to the one that succeeds later in the same leaf, so the gate is the only difference between the two outcomes.
|CHK-GATE-SIP-FAULT
|A valid SIP token presented with the comparator collapsed must not set the bit. The status is the fault code `6'b111111`, which a gate written as "not a mismatch" would accept. The cell that follows presents the same token with the fault removed and does advance.
|CHK-GATE-CHIP-ORD
|The chiplet bit does not set from the production state even with the chiplet token *already matched*, because the system-in-package state is not yet established. This isolates the ordering requirement from the token requirement — with the token gate open, only the ordering gate can hold.
|CHK-GATE-CHIP-TOK
|The complementary half: from the system-in-package state, the chiplet bit does not set without a chiplet token match.
|CHK-SIP-ADVANCE
|With a matching token the production part advances to the system-in-package state, and feature control decodes the new state. Together with CHK-GATE-SIP this is an allow and a block observed under one image.
|CHK-DEMOTE-OPEN
|Both demotion registers read back asserted AND the decoded feature-control word matches the golden *with demotion applied*. This is what stops CHK-DEMOTE-NO-BLOCK from passing vacuously: a leaf that silently failed to demote would fail this check first.
|CHK-DEMOTE-NO-BLOCK
|The same authorized advance succeeds while both demotion groups are asserted. Demotion is volatile debug state and not a lifecycle term, so a demoted part must transition exactly as a non-demoted one does.
|CHK-RMA-BIT0
|The low bit sets inside the system-in-package state and, on its own leaf, inside the chiplet state. The specification writes these encodings with a don't-care low bit, so this is a move within one state rather than a transition out of it.
|CHK-CHIP-ADVANCE
|With the chiplet token matched and the system-in-package state established, the chiplet bit sets and feature control decodes the chiplet profile.
|CHK-INVALID-REACH
|A write whose result falls outside the named encodings lands there, reached from a state the same leaf advanced into legitimately. Feature control must then read exactly 0 — the fail-closed default. Under the pinned disable vectors no named state decodes to 0, so this discriminates rather than coinciding.
|CHK-INVALID-TERM
|From outside the named set, a write of every lifecycle bit with BOTH tokens matched changes nothing. This is the strongest available form of the end-of-life property: every gate that could open is open, and the state still does not move.
|CHK-PROD-END-INVALID
|From the terminal product state, the reachable destination is outside the named set — the specification's "the only permitted transition is to INVALID", proven rather than assumed.
|CHK-TRANSIENT-NO-TOKEN
|With the transient enable armed and no token presented, the sensed terminal product state holds. The transient path applies the token gates; it does not move on the enable bit alone.
|CHK-TRANSIENT-INVALID
|The transient return-material-authorisation path applies the same write-one-to-set and token rules with no bus request at all, including when the result falls outside the named set.
|CHK-UPPER-MERGE
|The upper three bytes of the lifecycle word merge as ordinary set-only shadow bytes and do not disturb the lifecycle nibble, and the nibble's special handling does not leak into them. The pattern comes from the run seed, so this is not a fixed-constant check.
|CHK-LEAF
|Per-leaf closing line naming which walk ran, the sensed starting state, and the seed the tokens and upper-byte pattern came from.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

[NOTE]
====
The expected next state comes from `lc_state_next` in `cocotb/env/sep_lcc_golden.py`,
which is derived from `hw/sys/sep/doc/lifecycle_controller.adoc` and not transcribed
from the register logic. The chapter gives three rules and no destination table, and
the golden states them the same way — a destination table would be a second, weaker
statement of the same rule and would have to be re-derived every time the reachable
set changed. See <<lcc-golden-from-spec>>.

Randomization: the tokens and the upper-byte pattern come from the run seed
(`cocotb/env/sep_lc_transition.py`, which reuses `SepRmaTokenCfg` so this walk and
the token randomized-configuration test cannot drift). The cells within a leaf are
fixed, so no contract is seed-selected and every checker runs every seed.
====

[[sep_dma_cpu_contention_test]]
===== `sep_dma_cpu_contention_test`

*Phase*: 1

*Run mode*: `cpu`

*Source Module*: `sep_dma_cpu_contention_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, CPU FW

*Objective*: The only test where the CPU and the DMA engine are genuinely competing masters on the SRAM at the same instant.

*Test Coverage*: CPU and DMA as concurrent SRAM masters.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Inherit the global side-effect region marking from startup code; this test performs no per-test region write of its own.
. Seed the source pattern; clear the destination and the CPU's own region.
. Start the DMA copy and do not wait.
. Immediately run the CPU store loop.
. Sample DMA status at store-loop exit, then poll to completion.
. Check both regions and clear the status bits.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-OVERLAP
|A status sample at store-loop exit shows the DMA busy and not done, which means it was in flight for the whole loop — the two masters genuinely overlapped.
|CHK-NOERR
|The DMA completes with no error.
|CHK-RW1C
|The done status bit clears on write-one-to-clear. Chunk-done is never raised in this single-chunk configuration, so its clear is not exercised.
|CHK-DMA-DATA
|The DMA destination matches its source.
|CHK-CPU-DATA
|The CPU's own region holds exactly what the CPU wrote — its traffic was not corrupted by the competing master.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

'''''

[[sep_clock_uvm_wdt_rst_input_reset_path_test]]
===== `sep_clock_uvm_wdt_rst_input_reset_path_test`

*Phase*: 1

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`target = "lsu_stub_all_live"`, `+skip_fuse_sense`)

*Source Module*: `sep_clock_uvm_wdt_rst_input_reset_path_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Objective*: Drives the watchdog reset input pin and proves it takes the SEP CPU-reset net low and releases it again, without disturbing the main SEP reset.

*Test Coverage*: `wdt_rst_ni_i` drops and releases the CPU-reset net only.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Release the watchdog reset input; confirm the CPU reset is released and the main reset is high.
. Assert the watchdog reset input; after a few clocks confirm the CPU reset asserted.
. In the same window, confirm the main SEP reset is unaffected.
. Release the input again; confirm the CPU reset returns high.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-BASELINE
|Before any stimulus, both resets are released.
|CHK-ASSERT
|Asserting the watchdog reset input drives the CPU reset low.
|CHK-ISOLATION
|While the CPU reset is asserted, the main SEP reset stays high — and that observable is proven capable of reading 0, by asserting the main reset at the end of the test. Without that anchor a stuck-high read would have satisfied this row identically.
|CHK-RELEASE
|Releasing the SEP input `wdt_rst_ni_i` returns `sep_cpu_reset_n` high. This is the pin path only. Closed-loop bite stickiness is outside this DUT.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

'''''

[[sep_mailbox_plic_test]]
===== `sep_mailbox_plic_test`

*Phase*: 1

*Run mode*: `cpu`

*Source Module*: `sep_mailbox_plic_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, CPU FW

*Objective*: Completes the interrupt path all the way to the CPU.

*Test Coverage*: Mailbox push to PIC to a CPU interrupt service routine.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Initialise the fabric filter. The firmware also writes the clock-gate control register, but the only field the generated map defines there is the PKA gate, so that write ungates nothing in this design and is not on the proof path.
. Register a service routine on the mailbox interrupt source and enable it.
. Arm the mailbox: clear pending status, set the watermark to fire on one word, enable the interrupt.
. Write one word into the mailbox queue.
. Wait for interrupt; the routine claims it, raises the watermark, clears the status bits, confirms they read back clear while the enable is still set, then masks the enable.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-DELIVERY
|The service routine actually runs — the interrupt reached the CPU rather than stopping at the aggregator.
|CHK-RW1C
|The interrupt status register clears on write-one-to-clear, and the read-only pending register — derived as status AND enable — reads back zero while the enable is still fully set, so that zero is not a masking artefact.
|CHK-NOSTORM
|The routine runs exactly once — the count is compared against one, not merely printed — and the interrupt does not re-fire afterwards.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

'''''

[[sep_hmac_kmac_cpu_crypto_smoke_test]]
===== `sep_hmac_kmac_cpu_crypto_smoke_test`

*Phase*: 1

*Run mode*: `cpu`

*Source Module*: `sep_hmac_kmac_cpu_crypto_smoke_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, CPU FW

*Objective*: Brings the HMAC and KMAC engines up over the real CPU-to-fabric path, as a complement to the sideload tests which drive them from the Key Manager.

*Test Coverage*: HMAC and KMAC over the CPU fabric path.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. HMAC: configure for SHA-256; start; feed the message with queue backpressure; process; poll done; read and byte-swap the digest. Repeat for three messages.
. KMAC: poll idle; configure for the customisable mode with software-supplied entropy; set the entropy-ready bit (it is set, never read back); load the seed and prefix; start; feed the message with its length encoding; process; read the unmasked digest.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-HMAC-EMPTY
|The empty-message digest equals the independent software value.
|CHK-HMAC-SHORT
|A short message digest equals the software value.
|CHK-HMAC-MULTI
|A longer message digest equals the software value.
|CHK-RW1C
|Both engines' done events clear on write-one-to-clear.
|CHK-KMAC-LIVE
|KMAC completes with clean error status and a non-zero digest. Golden-checked KMAC is `sep_kmac_mode_strength_rand_test`.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

'''''

[[sep_rom_sanity_test]]
===== `sep_rom_sanity_test`

*Phase*: 1

*Run mode*: `cpu`

*Source Module*: `sep_rom_sanity_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, CPU FW

*Objective*: Proves the CPU fetch unit executes real code out of the boot ROM across the main RISC-V instruction formats.

*Test Coverage*: Boot-ROM fetch across the main RV32 instruction formats.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Initialise the fabric filter.
. Call seven ROM functions through pointers at known offsets: immediate arithmetic, a multi-instruction chain, argument passthrough, an upper-immediate load, a jump, a no-operation sled, and a two-register add.
. Compare each return value with its expected value.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-IFU-TYPES
|All seven functions fetch and execute correctly, each returning its distinct expected value across the immediate, upper-immediate, register and jump formats.
|CHK-ALL
|The test passes only if all seven match; a partial result is a failure. The host also counts the seven per-function lines itself, so a firmware that stopped calling into the ROM partway cannot pass on the completion magic alone.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

'''''

[[sep_km_aes_sideload_kat_test]]
===== `sep_km_aes_sideload_kat_test`

*Phase*: 1

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, real fuse sense

*Source Module*: `sep_km_aes_sideload_kat_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, KM path / golden

*Objective*: The AES member of the sideload family.

*Test Coverage*: Known-key Key Manager to AES sideload known-answer.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Real-sense a production fuse image; park OTBN, KMAC and HMAC; leave AES released.
. Bring up real entropy; release the Key Manager and wait for firmware ready.
. Load the known 256-bit key for AES; require a non-zero handle.
. Encrypt the plaintext with a decoy software key; check against golden.
. Transfer the key to AES; read back the reset register for the isolation record.
. Read the public key registers and require zero, with a readable register in the same window as a positive control.
. Encrypt in sideload mode; decrypt the result in sideload mode.
. Load the known key through software and encrypt again, proving the engine reaches the same golden independently of the sideload route.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-A
|The key is staged and a non-zero handle returned.
|CHK-B
|The transfer to AES succeeds.
|CHK-ISO
|Only the Key Manager and AES are released during the transfer.The reset-control readback that records this is `SW_RESET_N=0x25`: the TRNG bit stays released, and the check is a mask over the engine bits rather than an equality.
|CHK-NEG
|The decoy-key ciphertext matches its own golden, so the comparison baseline is sound.
|CHK-F
|The sideload ciphertext equals the independent golden for the known key.
|CHK-H
|The software-key path reaches the same golden ciphertext, so the engine is correct independently of the sideload route. `CHK-F` proves the delivered key bit-exactly. Guards that only relate two results already pinned to the same golden are not claimed here — they hold with the simulator off.
|CHK-RT
|Decrypting the sideload ciphertext recovers the original plaintext.
|CHK0
|Key Manager firmware boots on real entropy and reports ready over the mailbox.
|CHK-PUB
|After the sideload transfer, frontdoor reads of the AES public `KEY_SHARE0`/`KEY_SHARE1` registers return zero — the software-unreadability property of a sideloaded key. A readable status register in the same window is the positive control that the frontdoor read path is alive, so an all-zero read from a dead bus cannot pass.
|CHK1..CHK3
|The entropy stack matches its golden bit-exactly at the decorrelator, compress and seed stages.
|CHK5
|Both the Key Manager and AES draw real entropy beats. Alive-mode beat count; bit-exact per-sink compare is the small KM ROM tests, not `rom_main`.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

'''''

[[sep_km_hmac_sideload_kat_test]]
===== `sep_km_hmac_sideload_kat_test`

*Phase*: 1

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, real fuse sense

*Source Module*: `sep_km_hmac_sideload_kat_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, KM path / golden

*Objective*: The HMAC member of the sideload family.

*Test Coverage*: Known-key Key Manager to HMAC sideload known-answer.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Bring up real entropy; boot the Key Manager; load the known key for HMAC.
. Transfer the key; read back the reset register for the isolation record.
. Read the public key registers and require zero.
. Configure keyed SHA-256; start; feed the message; process; read the digest.
. Compare against the golden under the pinned convention, and against the decoy.
. Clear the done event and confirm it reads back clear.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-A
|The key is staged with a non-zero handle.
|CHK-B
|The transfer to HMAC succeeds.
|CHK-ISO
|Only the Key Manager and HMAC are released during the transfer.
|CHK-MAC
|The digest equals the independent HMAC-SHA256 golden for the known key.
|CHK-RW1C
|The done event clears on write-one-to-clear.
|CHK0
|Key Manager firmware boots on real entropy and reports ready over the mailbox.
|CHK-PUB
|After the sideload transfer, frontdoor reads of the HMAC public `KEY` registers return zero — the software-unreadability property of a sideloaded key. A readable status register in the same window is the positive control that the frontdoor read path is alive.
|CHK1..CHK3
|The entropy stack matches its golden bit-exactly at the decorrelator, compress and seed stages.
|CHK5
|The Key Manager draws real entropy beats from the shared bus while the key is staged. Alive-mode beat count; bit-exact per-sink compare is the small KM ROM tests, not `rom_main`.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

'''''

[[sep_km_kmac_sideload_kat_test]]
===== `sep_km_kmac_sideload_kat_test`

*Phase*: 1

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, real fuse sense

*Source Module*: `sep_km_kmac_sideload_kat_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, KM path / golden

*Objective*: The KMAC member of the sideload family.

*Test Coverage*: Known-key Key Manager to KMAC sideload known-answer.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Real-sense a production image; park the engines; bring up real entropy.
. Boot the Key Manager; load the known key for KMAC.
. Release KMAC and run a decoy-key operation for the reference digest.
. Read back the reset register; transfer the key.
. Read the public key registers and require zero.
. Run the operation in sideload mode, then again with the known key in software.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-A
|The key is staged with a non-zero handle.
|CHK-B
|The transfer to KMAC succeeds.
|CHK-ISO
|Only the Key Manager and KMAC are released during the transfer.
|CHK-SIDE
|The sideload digest differs from the decoy-key digest.
|CHK-MAC
|The sideload digest equals the software-key digest for the same known key.
|CHK-ENT
|The engine draws real masking entropy. Alive-mode beat count. Bit-exact per-sink compare is the small KM ROM tests, not `rom_main`. The scoreboard reports it as `CHK5_km` / `CHK5_kmac`.
|CHK0
|Key Manager firmware boots on real entropy and reports ready over the mailbox.
|CHK-PUB
|After the sideload transfer, frontdoor reads of the KMAC public `KEY_SHARE0`/`KEY_SHARE1` registers return zero — the software-unreadability property of a sideloaded key. A readable status register in the same window is the positive control that the frontdoor read path is alive.
|CHK1..CHK3
|The entropy stack matches its golden bit-exactly at the decorrelator, compress and seed stages.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Not claimed*: fail-closed `CFG.sideload=1` START with no valid Key Manager key
(`ErrKeyNotValid`), and a later software-key digest after `CMD.err_processed`
recovery. Those sit on the Phase 3 holding note <<phase-3>>.

'''''

[[sep_irq_ip_to_aggregator_test]]
===== `sep_irq_ip_to_aggregator_test`

*Phase*: 1

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`

*Source Module*: `sep_irq_ip_to_aggregator_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Objective*: Proves that interrupts owned by the entropy blocks reach the SEP interrupt aggregator on the correct bit, using each block's real interrupt-test register rather than a forced wire. After that walk, one in-window unmapped read through the Secure DMA adapter and one through each HMAC, KMAC and OTBN adapter must complete SLVERR, latch the matching `DMA_BUS_ERR_STATUS` / `PERIPH_BUS_ERR_STATUS` bit, raise aggregator bits 40 and 42, and clear on the matching CLEAR write.

*Test Coverage*: Entropy `INTR_TEST` bits plus adapter SLVERR into the aggregator.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. For each covered interrupt: clear the test register and the pending state.
. Assert the aggregator bit is low.
. Enable the interrupt and write the real test bit.
. Wait for the matching aggregator bit to assert; confirm the other covered bits stay low.
. Clear the test bit and the pending state; wait for deassertion.
. Confirm both bus-error STATUS registers and aggregator bits 40 and 42 read 0.
. Read the unused word between the last DMA `INTR_SRC_ADDR` and the first `INTR_SRC_WR_VAL`; require SLVERR, exclusive `reg_path_err`, and bit 40.
. Write `DMA_BUS_ERR_CLEAR`; STATUS and bit 40 read 0.
. Read the unused word between the HMAC CSRs and the message FIFO; require SLVERR, exclusive `hmac`, and bit 42. Write `PERIPH_BUS_ERR_CLEAR.hmac`; STATUS and bit 42 read 0.
. Repeat for the KMAC CSR/STATE gap (`kmac`) and the OTBN CSR/IMEM gap (`otbn`).

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-AGG
|All six interrupts propagate to their expected aggregator bit and clear again — baseline-low, assert, isolation and clear all covered. "One-hot" here means one-hot among the six covered bits. Bits outside that set are not compared — and the logged vector shows some of them are legitimately high, so this is a real scope limit rather than a theoretical one.
|CHK-NONVAC
|A non-zero number of AXI transactions was observed, so the run cannot pass having done nothing. This is a transaction floor, not a per-source minimum: no assertion requires that all six sources ran. The six per-source lines are what evidence that.
|CHK-BUSERR-BASE
|`DMA_BUS_ERR_STATUS` and `PERIPH_BUS_ERR_STATUS` read 0, and aggregator bits 40 and 42 read 0, before either fault. A stuck-high latch fails here.
|CHK-BUSERR-DMA
|The DMA hole read completes SLVERR, not DECERR. `DMA_BUS_ERR_STATUS` is exactly `reg_path_err`. Aggregator bit 40 is 1; bits 41 and 42 stay 0.
|CHK-BUSERR-PERIPH
|Each HMAC, KMAC and OTBN hole read completes SLVERR, not DECERR. `PERIPH_BUS_ERR_STATUS` is exactly that block's bit. Aggregator bit 42 is 1; bit 40 stays 0.
|CHK-BUSERR-CLR
|Each matching CLEAR write returns its STATUS word and its aggregator bit to 0. The CLEAR registers are write-only single-pulse fields that always read 0, so their read value is not graded.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Not claimed*: DMA host-path integrity (`host_path_err` / bit 41) — this leaf does not start a DMA transfer, so the host-adapter checker never sees `a_valid`. AES, CSRNG, EDN and WDT register-bridge bits — those adapter windows are packed to the last register. Lockstep punch-through. Per-block clear isolation: each hole is raised and cleared on its own, so no run holds two peripheral bits at once. The aggregator OR is likewise never driven by more than one source at a time. Persistence across a CPU-only reset, and the rule that a fault arriving in the clear cycle still latches, are unproven. The misaligned-offset and access-control terms of `reg_path_err` are unproven.

'''''

[[sep_drbg_real_sink_multi_km_aes_test]]
===== `sep_drbg_real_sink_multi_km_aes_test`

*Phase*: 1

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, real fuse sense, `+esrc_noise_force`

*Source Module*: `sep_drbg_real_sink_multi_km_aes_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, entropy golden

*Objective*: One entropy source feeding two real consumers at once.

*Test Coverage*: One entropy source with Key Manager and AES consuming together.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Real-sense a production image; park OTBN, KMAC and HMAC; leave AES released.
. Bring up entropy with strict golden checking and per-consumer scoring; wait for generated bits; start draining the queue; boot the Key Manager.
. Fork: one key-generate on the Key Manager, concurrently with reseed-and-encrypt cycles on AES.
. Confirm both consumers' beat counts advanced; compare the AES first-block ciphertext against golden.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-CONCUR
|Both consumers' beat counts advance within the forked window. This is an in-window advance, not same-cycle arbiter contention: the check would hold equally if one sink drained completely before the other began.
|CHK-AESKAT
|The AES first-block ciphertext equals the independent golden.
|CHK-ROUTING
|Each AES beat equals the next word on its own feed, in order; both consumers' words are members of the golden generated-bits set. This row is the per-sink entropy checker; the scoreboard reports it as `CHK5_aes` / `CHK5_km` / `CHK5_axis1`.
|CHK-NONVAC
|A non-zero number of AXI transactions was observed, so the run cannot pass having done nothing. This is a transaction floor, not a comparison count — the cited line reports zero value-verified reads, because the value comparisons for this test live in the entropy scoreboard rather than the AXI scoreboard.
|CHK1..CHK3
|The shared entropy stack matches its golden bit-exactly at the decorrelator, compress and seed stages.
|CHK4
|Generated bits match the golden. Generate segmentation is `sep_drbg_gen_segmentation_test`, not this `glen=32` budget.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

'''''

[[sep_cpu_ifu_lsu_alias_remap_matrix_test]]
===== `sep_cpu_ifu_lsu_alias_remap_matrix_test`

*Phase*: 1

*Run mode*: `cpu`

*Source Module*: `sep_cpu_ifu_lsu_alias_remap_matrix_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, CPU FW

*Objective*: Proves the CPU-side address alias window remaps both data and instruction accesses to physical SRAM.

*Test Coverage*: Alias window remaps LSU and IFU accesses to physical SRAM.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Probe the alias base CSR with a value distinct from its reset value, restore the operating base, and read back. The region size is a fixed localparam and is not programmed.
. Write through the alias; read the physical address.
. Write the physical address; read through the alias.
. Write a small function into SRAM, fence, and call it through its alias address.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-CSR
|The alias base register is writable and reads back. It is probed with a value distinct from its reset value first — writing only the operating base would be unfalsifiable, since that is also the field's reset value and a read-only register would pass. `CHK-BASE-LIVE` below then proves the programmed base is actually consumed.The operating base is `0xd0000000` over a fixed 768 MiB window onto physical `0x10000000`; the probe value is `0xe0000000`.
|CHK-BASE-LIVE
|A write through the alias window with a NON-reset base programmed lands at the physical address that base selects. A remapper hardwired at the operating base (`0xD000_0000`) would map `0xE000_0318` to `0x2000_0318`, not `0x1000_0318`, and fails here. This is what makes CHK-CSR's probe more than a register readback.
|CHK-LSU-WR
|A data write through the alias lands at the physical address.
|CHK-LSU-RD
|A physical write is readable through the alias.
|CHK-IFU
|A call through the alias is fetched, remapped, executed, and returns its value.
|===
*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Anti-vacuity*: the instruction-fetch checker cannot pass vacuously — the fetch
demultiplexer runs on the post-remap address, so an alias address that was not
remapped routes to the error slave and never executes.

'''''

[[sep_efuse_jtag_axil_el2_cpu_mux_test]]
===== `sep_efuse_jtag_axil_el2_cpu_mux_test`

*Phase*: 1

*Run mode*: `cpu`, real fuse sense

*Source Module*: `sep_efuse_jtag_axil_el2_cpu_mux_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, ocah_jtag_vip, CPU FW

*Objective*: The debug-port counterpart to the two-processor coexistence test.

*Test Coverage*: JTAG AXI-Lite and the CPU coexist on eFuse.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Real-sense a production image; wait for sense-done; both OTP JTAG2AXIL `dbg_disable` bits read 0; publish CPU-ready.
. CPU: a free-running loop reading and value-checking a token register, publishing a counter to cold scratch. The CPU issues no eFuse writes, so only the read channel is contended.
. Host, concurrently: 16 debug-port register rounds (52 AXI-Lite operations), all expected to succeed.
. Host: attempt a debug-port read of the protected shadow window; expect a decode error.
. Host: repeat a register read after the denial; expect success.
. Read the CPU's counter to confirm it advanced during the burst.
. Host: read `SEC_DISABLE_TOKEN_I[0]`, `TOKEN_EOP`, and `SEC_DISABLE_TOKEN_MATCH`; expect OKAY. A sample of the token-block allow class in PROD.
. Host: read `EFUSE_PROGRAM_CTRL`; expect DECERR and `0xbadcab1e`.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-JTAG-MMR
|All 16 debug-port register rounds complete successfully through the multiplexer.
|CHK-JTAG-DENY
|The protected shadow window is denied with a specific decode error and marker value, not a timeout.
|CHK-JTAG-ALLOW
|The register window still works after the denial — the port is gated per window, not disabled wholesale.
|CHK-CPU-MMR
|The CPU's readback of an untouched token register stays at its reset value throughout, with zero errors — so no non-zero debug-port data leaks into the CPU's read stream. The expected value is zero, which is also what two of the three debug-port read types return, so this catches a leak of the distinctive payload but not a zeroed CPU read path.
|CHK-COEXIST
|The CPU made real progress during the debug-port burst — the overlap was genuine.
|CHK-JTAG-TOKEN-BLOCK
|In PROD, JTAG reads of `SEC_DISABLE_TOKEN_I[0]`, `TOKEN_EOP`, and `SEC_DISABLE_TOKEN_MATCH` return OKAY. A sample of the `hw/sys/sep/doc/periphs.adoc` token-block allow class, not every `TOKEN_I` word and MATCH register. `CHK-JTAG-MMR` already walks RMA `TOKEN_I`.
|CHK-JTAG-IFACE-DENY
|In PROD, a JTAG read of `EFUSE_PROGRAM_CTRL` completes DECERR with `0xbadcab1e`. Shadow deny is CHK-JTAG-DENY; this is the program/read interface.
|CHK-OTP-JTAG2AXI-UNGATED
|After sense-done, `dbg_disable_o.smc_otp_jtag2axi` and `sep_otp_jtag2axi` both read 0. The common post-sense helper emits this on every leaf that waits for fuse-sense-done. Fuse-interface allow/deny stays on the checkers above.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

'''''

[[sep_nmi_sanity_test]]
===== `sep_nmi_sanity_test`

*Phase*: 1

*Run mode*: `cpu`

*Source Module*: `sep_nmi_sanity_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, CPU FW

*Objective*: Covers the non-maskable interrupt mechanism end to end: the vector register has the right default, accepts a new value, and refuses further writes once locked — and then a real watchdog bark delivers an actual non-maskable interrupt to that handler, which clears the condition and returns. The lock check is a negative test: the write after locking must be ignored and the vector must still hold its previous value.

*Test Coverage*: NMI vector read/write/lock plus a real watchdog bark to the handler.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Register the handler.
. Check the vector register default; write a new vector; read it back.
. Set the lock; attempt another write; confirm the vector is unchanged.
. Configure the watchdog with a small bark threshold and enable it; wait.
. On the interrupt, the handler clears the bark and disables the watchdog, then returns.
. Confirm the bark state is clear and the watchdog is disabled.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-VEC-DEFAULT
|The vector register holds its specified reset value.
|CHK-VEC-WRITE
|A new vector value is accepted and reads back.
|CHK-LOCK
|After locking, a further write is ignored and the vector keeps its value.
|CHK-WDT-NMI
|A real watchdog bark delivers the interrupt and the handler runs.
|CHK-WDT-CLEAR
|The bark status bit was observed *set* inside the handler — which is what attributes the NMI to the watchdog rather than to any other source vectoring to the same handler — and after the handler it reads clear with the watchdog disabled. The pre-clear sample matters: without it the bit is only ever seen as zero, and "cleared" cannot be distinguished from "never set".
|===
*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Sim scaling*: the watchdog bark threshold is reduced for simulation — the
watchdog clock runs roughly a thousand times slower than the core, so the
production threshold would dominate run time. The mechanism exercised is
identical: the counter still increments through the bark path and firmware still
reaches its wait before the interrupt fires.

'''''

[[sep_reset_wdt_sanity_test]]
===== `sep_reset_wdt_sanity_test`

*Phase*: 1

*Run mode*: `cpu`

*Source Module*: `sep_reset_wdt_sanity_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, CPU FW

*Objective*: Two related mechanisms in one boot.

*Test Coverage*: Watchdog bite to CPU reset in one firmware boot.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Check the software reset register default, which is `0x3E`: the Key Manager is held and OTBN, AES, HMAC, KMAC and the TRNG come up released. JTAG-holds OTBN/AES/HMAC/KMAC across `rst_ni` release and fuse sense so they never raise crypto `edn_req` while the fabric is opening, then releases them. The TRNG is not parked, because it supplies entropy rather than requesting it. The firmware then probes each per-IP reset wire, including the shared TRNG bit: assert the bit, confirm the register goes to its reset value, release, confirm it is writable again.
. Perform a bad write and a bad read of the unmapped gap, with settling in between; expect two bus-error interrupts.
. Configure the watchdog with small bark and bite thresholds; enable.
. On the first bark the handler clears and disables; confirm the count is frozen non-zero, pet returns it to zero, and it stays zero while disabled.
. Re-enable; confirm a second bark, then let it run on to bite.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-BADADDR
|Accesses to the unmapped gap raise exactly two bus-error interrupts.
|CHK-WDT-NMI
|The first bark delivers an interrupt and the handler runs.
|CHK-WDT-CLEAR
|The handler clears the bark state and disables the watchdog.
|CHK-WDT-PET
|The count is frozen non-zero, petting returns it to zero, and it stays zero while disabled.
|CHK-WDT-REBARK
|Re-enabling fires a second bark — the mechanism is re-armable, not one-shot.
|CHK-BITE
|The bite reset request reaches the real output pin.
|CHK-SWRST-DEFAULT
|The software reset register holds its specified default of `0x3E`.
|CHK-SWRST-WIRE
|Pulsing each per-IP bit, the shared TRNG bit included, returns that domain's probe register to its reset value and leaves the neighbouring domains untouched.
|===
*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Sim scaling and timing requirement*: watchdog thresholds are reduced for
simulation, as in the non-maskable-interrupt test. A settling delay is required
between the bad store and the bad load; without it the core's imprecise
store-error reporting coalesces the two bus-error interrupts into one, and the
entry requires exactly two.

'''''

[[sep_lcc_uvm_inbound_filter_gating_test]]
===== `sep_lcc_uvm_inbound_filter_gating_test`

*Phase*: 1

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, real fuse sense

*Source Module*: `sep_lcc_uvm_inbound_filter_gating_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Objective*: Proves that the lifecycle security state actually gates the external inbound port — the security-boundary property, not just the register that describes it.

*Test Coverage*: Lifecycle state gates the SMN-inbound port.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Real-sense a production image with pinned disable overrides.
. Read the feature-control register over the control bus and compare all 64 bits against golden; confirm the debug bit is clear.
. Attempt the same read from the external port; require a decode error.
. Write and read back the demote register over the control bus.
. Re-read feature-control; compare against golden; confirm the debug bit is now set.
. Repeat the external read; require success and the expected value.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-PROD-FEAT
|In the production state the full feature-control value matches golden and the debug bit is clear.
|CHK-PROD-BLOCK
|The external read is refused with a specific decode error, not a timeout.
|CHK-DEMOTE-INDEP
|`DEMOTE_2` alone opens DBG_2 `[47:24]` and leaves DBG_1 `[23:0]` closed, so the two demote registers act per-group rather than sharing a priority. `sep_debug` is bit 0, inside DBG_1, so it must still read 0 — and the external port must still be blocked, which is a second, independent consequence of the same property. `DEMOTE_2` is driven first deliberately: the demote field is write-once-set and cannot be cleared, so this is the only order in which this DUT can show one group opening without the other. Before this row existed, `DEMOTE_2` was never written by any test here — it appeared only in the golden, so the specification's DEMOTE_2 column rested on the golden's import-time KAT comparing the model against itself.
|CHK-DEMOTE
|The demote register accepts a write and reads back.
|CHK-DBG-FEAT
|After demotion the full feature-control value matches the spec-derived golden decode and the debug bit is set. See <<lcc-golden-from-spec>>.
|CHK-DBG-ALLOW
|The external read now succeeds and returns the distinctive value, proving it reached the lifecycle controller.
|CHK-IDENTITY
|An external inbound access tracks the debug bit — refused when clear, served when set. This is a behavioural differential on the external port; the filter's internal bypass signal is not sampled.
|CHK-NONVAC
|Both the blocked and allowed cases were actually observed in this run.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

'''''

[[sep_esrc_e2e_smoke_test]]
===== `sep_esrc_e2e_smoke_test`

*Phase*: 1

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`target = "lsu_stub_all_live"`, `+skip_fuse_sense`, `+esrc_noise_force`)

*Source Module*: `sep_esrc_e2e_smoke_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, entropy golden

*Objective*: Proves the entropy stack is alive end to end on the open-source build and that its output reaches a real consumer.

*Test Coverage*: Entropy stack seed-out to a real consumer.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Bring up in `no_cpu` mode with fuse sense skipped.
. Release the Key Manager with a small entropy-consuming ROM image.
. Configure the source, generator and distribution blocks in order and enable them.
. Start the golden scoreboard; drain the source queue through the front door.
. Wait for generated bits, for the Key Manager to take a word, and for a non-zero word to appear in its memory. The stored word is checked for presence, not value.
. Check the generator and distribution error and alert status.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-ALERTS
|Generator and distribution error and alert status are clear.The four status registers read are the generator's `0x10915054` / `0x10915050` and the distribution block's `0x1091583c` / `0x10915838`, each required to be zero.
|CHK1
|Decorrelated output matches the golden model.
|CHK2
|Compressed output, observed at the queue front door, matches golden.
|CHK3
|The seed matches golden.
|CHK4
|Generated bits match golden. Generate segmentation is `sep_drbg_gen_segmentation_test`, not this `glen=32` budget.
|CHK5
|The Key Manager consumes an entropy word and it matches golden.
|CHK5_pool
|Mux endpoint [2] (entropy-pool adapter): each post-adapter pool beat equals the next AXIS2 word, and `pool_edn_fips_o=1` on every completed beat, so the adapter forwarded the DRBG FIPS bit. Does not drain `0x1095_0000`.
|===
*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

[[esrc-noise-exception]]
*What `+esrc_noise_force` is, and what it costs*: a ring oscillator is a
free-running combinational loop — `entropy_ring_oscillator` is a NAND-plus-buffer
delay chain closed on itself — and no event-driven simulator produces physical
randomness from it. Injection is therefore the only way to exercise the stack,
and it is applied at the decorrelator noise input, upstream of every stage this
plan checks. This is the same node and the same default mode the entropy IP's own
testbench uses, so it is not a weaker substitute for that verification.

*Not claimed*: the injection point sits downstream of the entire noise source —
the ring oscillator, the metastable sampling flop, and the sample-clock-to-core-clock
synchroniser — and the oscillator tune/detune path drives that same stage. All of
that is real logic, and no test in this plan exercises it: no testlist entry runs
this stack with the injection off. Entropy quality, oscillator tuning and health
tests over real noise belong to the entropy IP's own testbench. Every stage after
the injection point is real logic and is golden-checked.

'''''

==== Phase 2 procedures

[[sep_aes_mode_keysize_rand_test]]
===== `sep_aes_mode_keysize_rand_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: Crypto and entropy

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), `+skip_fuse_sense +esrc_noise_force`

*Source Module*: `sep_aes_mode_keysize_rand_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Objective*: Walks the nine-cell product of {ECB, CBC, CTR} x {128, 192, 256} in one invocation, with key, IV and plaintext randomised per cell.

*Test Coverage*: Nine AES cells: {ECB, CBC, CTR} × {128, 192, 256}.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Bring up in `no_cpu` with `+skip_fuse_sense` and park non-AES EDN clients.
. Walk all nine {ECB, CBC, CTR} × {128, 192, 256} cells against `sep_aes_golden`.
. Score CHK1..CHK4 bit-exact and CHK5_aes routing.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-CELL
|Per cell: the ciphertext equals the golden, the round trip returns the plaintext, and no alert fired. For ECB and CBC the round trip programs a real DECRYPT operation; for CTR it re-runs ENCRYPT, which is a keystream-determinism check rather than a decrypt.
|CHK-RAND-REP
|Every mode x key-size cell ran in one invocation and produced a *distinct* ciphertext, so the stimulus stream is live rather than repeating one configuration. Note the key, IV and plaintext are re-drawn per cell, so this does not by itself prove the cells programmed different modes — the per-cell golden compare is what proves that.
|CHK1..CHK4
|Strict entropy scoreboard: the decorrelator, compress, seed and genbits stages each match the chained golden bit-exactly. OTBN, KMAC and HMAC are parked, so AES is the only crypto EDN client.
|CHK5_aes
|Each post-adapter AES beat equals the next AXIS1 word — the single-sink routing golden. The Key Manager is unused on this path. The minimum-evidence floor is raised to 32 scored beats, so the per-beat claim cannot be discharged by a single beat.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

'''''

[[sep_hmac_sha_variant_rand_test]]
===== `sep_hmac_sha_variant_rand_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: Crypto and entropy

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), `+skip_fuse_sense`

*Source Module*: `sep_hmac_sha_variant_rand_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Objective*: Walks 17 cells: keyed HMAC over every legal key length for each of SHA-256/384/512, plus plain SHA for each variant.

*Test Coverage*: Seventeen HMAC/SHA cells across SHA-256/384/512 and legal key lengths.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Bring up in `no_cpu` with `+skip_fuse_sense`.
. Walk the 17 HMAC/SHA cells against `sep_hmac_golden`.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-CONV
|The engine honours the *specified* software-key register convention. `hmac.hjson:446-447` defines `key[1023:0] = {KEY0, ... , KEY31}`, so `KEY_0` is the most-significant word and no word reversal is needed; `hmac.sv:219-221` implements it. The convention is pinned from the specification, not discovered by asking the engine which one it agrees with, and not transcribed from the RTL it checks — see <<audit>>. On mismatch the failure reports whether the reversed order would have matched, which separates a key word-order regression from a general digest fault.
|CHK-CELL
|Per cell: the digest equals the golden, the done interrupt sets and clears on write-one-to-clear, and the error status is clean.
|CHK-RAND-REP
|Every legal cell ran, each producing a distinct digest. The matrix is derived from the generated digest-size and key-length banks minus `HMAC_ILLEGAL_KEYED`, which is the single source of truth for legality, so the sequence and this walk cannot drift apart. As on the AES sweep, the key and message are re-drawn per cell, so distinctness alone does not prove the cells programmed different variants — the per-cell golden compare is what proves that.
|SKIP-ILLEGAL-KEYED
|Each digest-size / key-length pair the engine rejects as `invalid_config` is named in the log with its reason, rather than being dropped silently from the matrix. A declaration, not a checker: no negative cell is driven, so nothing here provokes `ERR_CODE`.
|CHK-ERR
|`ERR_CODE` and the error interrupt bit read zero across the walk. This is a passive observation: nothing in this entry induces an error, so there is no positive control that the register can ever be non-zero on this path.
|===
*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Scope limit*: `CHK-ERR` is a passive observation — `ERR_CODE` and the error
interrupt bit are read and required to be zero, but nothing in this entry induces
an error, so there is no positive control that the register can ever be non-zero
on this path.

'''''

[[sep_kmac_mode_strength_rand_test]]
===== `sep_kmac_mode_strength_rand_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: Crypto and entropy

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), `+skip_fuse_sense +esrc_noise_force`

*Source Module*: `sep_kmac_mode_strength_rand_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Objective*: Thirteen cells across SHA3-224/256/384/512, SHAKE-128/256, cSHAKE-128/256 and KMAC-128/256 spanning all five key lengths.

*Test Coverage*: Thirteen KMAC/SHA3/SHAKE/cSHAKE cells across the legal key lengths.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Bring up in `no_cpu` with `+skip_fuse_sense`.
. Walk the thirteen SHA3/SHAKE/cSHAKE/KMAC cells against `sep_kmac_golden`.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-CELL
|Per cell: the digest equals the golden, and the error status is clean.
|CHK-DONE-RW1C
|The done interrupt is observed *set*, written to clear, and re-read as zero — a real three-step contract rather than a single sample.
|CHK-RAND-REP
|Every cell ran, each producing a distinct digest. The walk covers the complete legal SHA3 strength set (224/256/384/512) and all five KMAC key lengths. As on the AES sweep, the key and message are re-drawn per cell, so distinctness alone does not prove the cells programmed different modes — the per-cell golden compare is what proves that.
|CHK-ERR
|`ERR_CODE` and the error interrupt bit read zero across the walk. Passive observation only: nothing in this entry induces an error, so there is no positive control that the register can ever be non-zero on this path. None of the twelve error codes `kmac_pkg.sv` defines is provoked.
|CHK1..CHK4
|Strict entropy scoreboard: the decorrelator, compress, seed and genbits stages each match the chained golden bit-exactly. OTBN, AES and HMAC are parked, so KMAC is the only crypto EDN client.
|CHK5_kmac
|Each post-adapter KMAC beat equals the next AXIS1 word — the single-sink routing golden. The Key Manager is unused on this path. The minimum-evidence floor is 3 scored beats, a 2x margin under an observed run of six: KMAC draws entropy once per operation rather than per block, so this walk scores about six routed beats where the AES sweep scores over a hundred.
|===
*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Shared-code limit*: the SP800-185 encoders (`right_encode`, `encode_string`) are
shared between the stimulus and the golden, so an encoder error would program the
DUT and build the expectation identically. This is mitigated rather than
eliminated: the golden's import-time KATs exercise those same encoders against
NIST samples.

'''''

[[sep_crypto_edn_multisink_arbitration_test]]
===== `sep_crypto_edn_multisink_arbitration_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: Crypto and entropy

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), `+skip_fuse_sense +esrc_noise_force`, 3 seeds

*Source Module*: `sep_crypto_edn_multisink_arbitration_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, entropy golden

*Objective*: Two crypto sinks draw EDN entropy concurrently and both complete correctly: each AES fork block matches its golden bit-exact and the KMAC digest matches its own, while both are shown to have taken real post-adapter EDN beats inside the contention window. An AES-alone baseline in which KMAC takes zero beats is the control that keeps the both-beats check from passing vacuously.

*Test Coverage*: AES and KMAC draw EDN together, plus an AES-alone control.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Bring up in `no_cpu` with `+skip_fuse_sense` and `+esrc_noise_force`.
. Run the entropy scoreboard in strict mode with `report()`.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-NONVAC
|An AES-alone baseline reproduces its known-answer ciphertext while the KMAC sink takes *zero* beats — a real control showing the both-beats check below is not always true.
|CHK-BOTH-COMPLETE
|Under contention both clients complete correctly: every AES fork block matches its golden bit-exact, and the KMAC digest matches its own golden.
|CHK-BOTH-BEATS
|Both crypto sinks took real post-adapter EDN beats during the contention window.
|CHK-OVERLAP
|The two sinks' beat windows overlap in time, so one DRBG time-multiplexed two live clients. This is interval-span containment, *not* interleaving: it is satisfied when one client's entire burst falls in a gap between two of the other's beats, which is what the kept runs actually show. Same-cycle dual `edn_req` and alternating grants are <<sep_crypto_edn_round_robin_grant_test>>, not this entry.
|CHK-ROUTING
|Each AES post-adapter beat and each KMAC post-adapter beat equals the AXIS1 word the adapter granted that cycle. Unscored clients (OTBN wipe) still consume AXIS1 words; those grants are popped so AES/KMAC stay aligned.
|CHK-MEMBERSHIP
|Every word either sink received is a member of the CHK4 genbits golden set (AXIS1 is chained to genbits in `report()`).
|CHK1..CHK4
|The entropy stack matches its golden bit-exactly at each stage, in strict mode, with `report()` called. Generate segmentation is `sep_drbg_gen_segmentation_test`, not this budget. CHK1's golden initial state is seeded from the live RTL shift register after reset, so CHK1 verifies the *recurrence* rather than the reset phase, and CHK2..CHK4 take the previous verified stage's DUT output as their input.
|===
*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Chain of custody*: CHK1's golden initial state is seeded from the live RTL
shift-register after reset, so CHK1 verifies the *recurrence* rather than the
reset phase, and CHK2..CHK4 take the previous verified stage's DUT output as
their input. That is the standard chained-stage methodology, recorded here so the
custody chain is explicit.

'''''

[[sep_crypto_edn_round_robin_grant_test]]
===== `sep_crypto_edn_round_robin_grant_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: Crypto and entropy

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), `+skip_fuse_sense +esrc_noise_force`

*Source Module*: `sep_crypto_edn_round_robin_grant_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, entropy golden

*Objective*: `hw/sys/sep/doc/crypto.adoc`: `u_axis_edn_crypto_s3c_scan` (`drbg_axis_edn_adapter`) is a small round-robin native-protocol EDN fan-out to AES, KMAC, OTBN RND, and OTBN URND.

*Test Coverage*: Round-robin EDN fan-out to AES, KMAC, OTBN RND, and OTBN URND.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Bring up in `no_cpu` mode with fuse sense skipped.
. Hold AES PRNG reseed and OTBN URND `edn_req` together before EDN is enabled.
. Bring up the entropy stack (noise force, seed, then enable EDN).
. Sample `crypto_edn_ack_o` for a fixed grant budget, then let the checkers decide. Breaking the collection loop on the alternation condition would make the checkers restatements of that guard.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-DUAL-REQ
|At least one cycle both selected clients have `edn_req` high. Without this, CHK-GRANT-ALT is vacuous.
|CHK-GRANT-ALT
|After that dual request, a prefix of consecutive post-adapter grants strictly alternates between the two clients. A same-client repeat is allowed only after that prefix has already granted both. A grant stream that is one client then the other, with no alternating prefix, fails. Same-cycle dual-`edn_req` grants are not scored: AES reseed pulses, so an ack-time dual sample can be AES-only. This is sharing plus an alternating prefix, not a lockstep proof of `prim_arbiter_ppc` under simultaneous request.
|CHK-NO-STARVE
|Each requesting client receives at least one `edn_ack` in the window.
|CHK-ROUTING
|Each AES post-adapter beat and each OTBN URND post-adapter beat equals the AXIS1 word the adapter granted that cycle.
|CHK1..CHK4
|Strict entropy scoreboard on the same bring-up that feeds the dual-request window: FIFO drain after the seed, then `report()`.
|CHK5_aes / CHK5_otbn_urnd
|Both live sinks match the AXIS1 grant-order stream with zero mismatches. This entry drives two of the four crypto sinks (AES and OTBN URND); KMAC and OTBN RND sharing is not claimed here, and OTBN RND is scored by no vehicle in the tree. The testlist pins one seed, so the per-sink word counts are fixed rather than varying per seed; the zero-mismatch requirement is what is claimed.
|===
*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Standing rule*: any test that moves data across the DRBG runs CHK1..CHK4
(strict scoreboard, FIFO drain, `report()`) and enables CHK5 on every live sink.
CHK5 mode is the strongest the stimulus allows: golden routing when the adapter
grant order is observed from `edn_ack` (one or more crypto sinks); membership
when the pull order is firmware-driven; observe when even membership is not
scored (`rom_main` KM). This test uses golden routing.

'''''

[[sep_drbg_gen_segmentation_test]]
===== `sep_drbg_gen_segmentation_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: Crypto and entropy

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), `+skip_fuse_sense +esrc_noise_force +km_rom_hex=km_rom_entropy.parhex`

*Source Module*: `sep_drbg_gen_segmentation_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, entropy golden

*Objective*: Phase 2 DRBG iconic feature: a CSRNG Generate command completes at the commanded length, `gen_last` asserts, and the trailing CTR_DRBG Update runs.

*Test Coverage*: CSRNG Generate at the commanded length, `gen_last`, and the trailing Update.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Bring up in `no_cpu` mode with fuse sense skipped.
. Release the Key Manager with the small entropy-consuming ROM image.
. Program the boot Generate and the auto Generate to `glen=4` from the same config object that feeds the golden.
. Bring up the entropy stack, drain the source queue through the front door, and wait for generated bits and a Key Manager consume.
. Value-compare the word the KM firmware stored to KM SRAM against the word the DUT delivered on the AXIS endpoint.
. Require at least one completed Generate of exactly four blocks, then report the strict CHK1..CHK5 scoreboard.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-SEGMENTATION
|At least one Generate command completed, and every completed command carried exactly the commanded `glen=4` blocks — so `gen_last` was observed, the trailing Update ran, and a segment of any other length would fail.
|CHK1..CHK4
|The entropy stack matches its golden bit-exactly at each stage, including across the Update boundary this entry forces, in strict mode, with `report()` called.
|CHK5_km
|The Key Manager consumes an entropy word on the post-mux AXIS endpoint and it matches golden bit-exactly — the same consumer close as the end-to-end smoke. It says nothing about Key Manager *memory*; `CHK5_km_sram` is the row that covers the store.
|CHK5_km_sram
|The word the Key Manager firmware stored to KM SRAM equals the word the DUT delivered on the AXIS endpoint. `km_rom_entropy.S` reads one DRBG-sampler DATA word and stores exactly that word to KM SRAM base + 0, so a wrong-word store, a dropped store, or a store to the wrong offset fails here — where a non-zero-only liveness poll would pass. The stored value is a genbits word and so differs per run; only the invariant is claimed.
|===
*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Scope limit*: `km_rom_entropy.S` reads one DRBG-sampler DATA word and stores
exactly that word to KM SRAM base + 0, so `CHK5_km_sram` compares that one
delivered word against KM SRAM word 0; it does not claim a sequence of stores. A
second AUTO Generate after the Update is not claimed — the Key Manager ROM reads
one word and parks, which is enough for the legality check to execute.

'''''

[[sep_crypto_per_ip_reset_isolation_test]]
===== `sep_crypto_per_ip_reset_isolation_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: Crypto and entropy

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), `+skip_fuse_sense +esrc_noise_force`

*Source Module*: `sep_crypto_per_ip_reset_isolation_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Objective*: Proves the per-IP software reset domains are isolated, using live crypto results rather than a poked status bit: each engine computes a golden-checked value, then one engine is reset and the other's held result must survive bit-exact.

*Test Coverage*: Per-IP software reset; neighbour crypto result survives bit-exact.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Compute a golden-checked result on two engines.
. Reset one engine; the other's held result must survive bit-exact. Both directions.
. Hold HMAC in software reset and prove the same `DIGEST_0` read and a `CFG` write complete SLVERR, while the sibling AES port stays OKAY.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-NONVAC
|Both held results are golden-matched and survive a *second* independent read, so the survives/cleared checks below are observations rather than restatements of one sample.
|CHK-SELF-RESET
|The AES reset pulse perturbs its own output. This is an inequality, not a pinned value — the masking-presented `DATA_OUT` after reset is a secure-wipe PRNG pattern rather than a fixed constant. The compensating evidence is that the entry re-derives the exact golden ciphertext afterwards, so the domain provably reset *and* recovered.
|CHK-NEIGHBOR-SURVIVES
|The HMAC digest is bit-exact intact across the AES reset. The single most valuable check in the entry.
|CHK-REVERSE
|The reverse direction: the HMAC reset clears its own digest to the exact reset value, and the AES output is bit-exact intact.
|CHK-KMAC-SELF
|KMAC `SW_RESET_N` restores `STATUS` to its register-map reset and STATE (share0^share1) reads 0, while HMAC DIGEST stays bit-exact.
|CHK-KMAC-NEIGHBOR
|HMAC `SW_RESET_N` leaves KMAC STATE bit-exact intact.
|CHK-OTBN
|OTBN `LOAD_CHECKSUM` (rst_ni CSR) survives an AES reset and clears to its register-map reset on OTBN's own pulse. DMEM holds the staged mark across the AES neighbour pulse (cross-domain leak). OTBN's own reset runs a secure wipe, so DMEM retention across that pulse is not claimed.
|CHK-ISOLATE-PRE
|HMAC `DIGEST_0` answers OKAY with the live golden digest before the isolate request, so the later SLVERR is at a live mapped address.
|CHK-ISOLATE-LANDED
|`SW_RESET_N` readback shows the HMAC bit held, so a refusal cannot be a missed write.
|CHK-ISOLATE-SLVERR
|During the window a read of the same `DIGEST_0` completes SLVERR, not OKAY, DECERR, or hang, and carries `axi_lite_isolate`'s own error-data payload, scraped from that module at import. The payload is what attributes the termination to the isolate rather than to any responder that happens to slave-error.
|CHK-ISOLATE-WR
|During the window a write to HMAC `CFG` completes SLVERR. The write target is the RW `CFG` register, not the RO digest.
|CHK-ISOLATE-SIBLING
|AES `DATA_OUT_0` answers OKAY with the held ciphertext in the same HMAC window (port-scoped, not a global bus kill).
|CHK-ISOLATE-REOPEN
|After release the same `DIGEST_0` answers OKAY again.
|CHK-ISOLATE-RESET-DEFAULT
|After release `DIGEST_0` reads its reset value 0.
|CHK-TRNG-NEIGHBORS
|Parking the shared TRNG reset leaves the four accelerator domains released and their held results readable, so the TRNG domain is reset-independent of them.
|===
*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Scope limit*: scope walks AES, HMAC, KMAC and OTBN. The Key Manager bit stays at
its reset default (a different mechanism) and is not claimed.

*Not claimed*: drain-before-reset for these four domains. The `CHK-ISOLATE-*`
rows cover in-window SLVERR and sibling OKAY only. The shared TRNG domain
grades drain-before-reset in <<sep_trng_reset_recovery_test>>. Key Manager
isolate termination and SMC CPU-MMIO isolate are not claimed.

'''''

[[sep_trng_reset_recovery_test]]
===== `sep_trng_reset_recovery_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: Crypto and entropy

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), `+skip_fuse_sense +esrc_noise_force`

*Source Module*: `sep_trng_reset_recovery_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, entropy golden

*Objective*: Proves the one `SW_RESET_N` bit that resets ESRC, CSRNG and EDN
together behaves as a single coordinated domain: the reset waits for all three
AXI ports to isolate, the entropy pool scrubs whatever it held, the external
source path stays outside the domain, and recovery admits only fresh entropy.

*Test Coverage*: Shared TRNG `SW_RESET_N` drain-before-reset and ordered recovery.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Bring the internal chain up and fill the fabric entropy pool through
  ESRC → CSRNG → EDN, recording the pool level and that `FIPS_LOCK` is set.
. Select every external-source mux leg and sample the external TRNG CSR
  response, establishing the pre-reset baseline for the paths that must survive.
. Hold every entropy consumer (KM, OTBN, AES, KMAC), then request the TRNG reset
  while concurrent reads are in flight on all three internal CSR ports.
. Through the reset, observe the isolate probe, the fate of the in-flight
  accesses, and the pool's packer depth.
. With the domain in reset, read the three internal ports, the external CSR and
  the source-select register; then exercise the JTAG reset pair to hold and
  release the same coordinated reset.
. Reinitialize ESRC, CSRNG and EDN with consumers still held, require a fresh
  seed and fresh pool progress, and only then restore the consumer resets.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-TRNG-FILL
|The initial DRBG produces genbits and `FIPS_LOCK` reads set, so the pool level the later checks compare against is real entropy rather than a reset default.
|CHK-TRNG-ISOLATE-ALL
|While the reset is requested the isolate probe reads all three ports isolated. This is the drain-before-reset evidence: the reset does not assert until ESRC, CSRNG and EDN have all reported isolated.
|CHK-TRNG-DRAIN
|Every in-flight pre-reset access completes either OKAY or SLVERR — never DECERR and never a hang — and at least one completes OKAY, so accepted traffic provably drained rather than being dropped.
|CHK-TRNG-PACKER
|The entropy pool's packer depth is zero, so a half-packed word does not survive the reset.
|CHK-TRNG-SLVERR
|All three internal CSR ports answer SLVERR while isolated.
|CHK-TRNG-EXTERNAL
|The source-select register still reads all three legs selected after the internal reset, so the source-select CSR is outside the TRNG reset domain. The external TRNG aperture itself is terminated by a permanent DECERR error slave in this build (`hw/top/sep_ip_integration.sv`, `u_ext_trng_axil_err_slv`) and its passthrough leg carries no isolate unit (`hw/sys/sep/rtl/sep_crypto_axi_interconnect.sv`), so the before/after DECERR pair is a build invariant kept as the liveness baseline for that read, not as reset-domain evidence. Domain membership for the aperture needs a real responder behind it and is not claimed.
|CHK-TRNG-JTAG
|The JTAG override holds the coordinated reset asserted and releases it again, the same pair used for the other domains.
|CHK-TRNG-CONSUMERS-HELD
|The `SW_RESET_N` readback shows the four consumers still held and the TRNG bit released, so the recovery ordering is observed rather than assumed.
|CHK-TRNG-CSR-RESET
|After the reset `FIPS_LOCK` is clear, the CSRNG and EDN interrupt enables return to zero from the non-zero masks written before the reset, and reserved ESRC `CTRL[0]` reads zero and ignores writes — the domain returned to its register-map reset rather than partially. The interrupt enables are driven off reset first, so this is a 1->0 traversal rather than a read of two registers that were never written.
|CHK-TRNG-STALE
|A pop from the empty pool answers SLVERR with zero data, so pre-reset entropy cannot be read back. `sep_entropy_fifo.sv` drives SLVERR on both the empty-pop and the pending-read-during-clear paths; a DECERR here would mean the aperture had fallen into the reset domain, which is the opposite of what this proves, so the response is pinned rather than tested as any non-OKAY.
|CHK-TRNG-FRESH
|ESRC produces a fresh seed, `FIPS_LOCK` sets again, and a pool read succeeds, all before the consumer resets are restored to their saved value.
|CHK-TRNG-RECOVERY
|Closing summary line over the eleven rows above: the pool went full, emptied and refilled, the stale read was rejected, fresh data was delivered, and the consumer resets were restored to their saved value. It carries no contract of its own.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Why the pool is checked from outside the domain*: the entropy pool's AXI
responder is not in the TRNG reset domain, which is what makes the scrub
observable at all — the port stays live while its contents are invalidated.

*The RTL clear and isolate assertions are VCS-only*: the six
`ClearDropsRequest_A` / `ClearScrubs*` / `ClearResetsStall_A` /
`NoEntropyAcceptedDuringClear_A` / `ClearScrubsPendingRead_A` assertions in
`sep_entropy_fifo.sv`, the two `AxisEdnNo*DuringClear_A` in
`drbg_axis_edn_adapter.sv`, and `ResetOnlyWhenIsolated_A` /
`IsolateHeldThroughReset_A` in `sep_isolate_rst_seq.sv` all expand to nothing
under Verilator, because `prim_assert.sv` and `ocah_assert.svh` both gate on
`ifndef VERILATOR`. Since a kept Verilator PASS is the sign-off gate, those ten
contribute no evidence on it. CHK-TRNG-ISOLATE-ALL and CHK-TRNG-PACKER are the
Verilator-live stand-ins for the drain-order and packer-scrub contracts, which
is why they are asserted from Python probes rather than left to the SVA.

'''''

[[sep_abr_mldsa_keygen_kat_test]]
===== `sep_abr_mldsa_keygen_kat_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: Crypto and entropy

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`

*Source Module*: `sep_abr_mldsa_keygen_kat_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Objective*: Proves the ML-DSA-87 key-generation path through the ABR register aperture: identity and reset status, an ACVP keyGen known-answer public key, completion status, the PIC error and notification legs, `ZEROIZE`, and single-bit seed sensitivity. Randomization is RANDCFG — the masking entropy words, and the seed word and bit flipped in the sensitivity leg, come from the run seed (`SepAbrKeygenCfg`).

*Test Coverage*: ML-DSA-87 keyGen known-answer, PIC legs, `ZEROIZE`, and seed sensitivity.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. The aperture is a `sep_crypto` demux rule at `ABR_REG_MAP_BASE_ADDR` (`hw/sys/sep/rtl/sep_crypto_pkg.sv`), reachable from the CPU-LSU master the way the OTBN tests already reach their block.
. It is *not* behind an `axi_lite_isolate` unit: unlike the ESRC, CSRNG and EDN legs, the external TRNG passthrough in `hw/sys/sep/rtl/sep_crypto_axi_interconnect.sv` carries none.
. Accesses are 32-bit beats (`AxSIZE=2`) because this path has no width converter.
. The Key-Manager-side key CSR is the generated PeakRDL `abr_wrapper_key_reg`. The ABR register block behind this aperture is real RTL.
. This leaf writes the seed window over the bus. The key-vault seed path is not claimed.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-NAME
|Identity registers read `MLDSA-87`.
|CHK-RESET-STATUS
|Reset STATUS is READY, not VALID, not ERROR.
|CHK-NIST-PK
|648-word public key matches the vendored ACVP keyGen vector.
|CHK-STATUS-VALID
|Completion STATUS is VALID with ERROR=0.
|CHK-PIC-ERROR
|Aggregate `[34]` traverses `0 -> 1` via `error_intr_trig`, then `W1C` readback 0, before the KAT. A stuck-low probe fails this row; the later `[34]=0` at completion is then a live observation.
|CHK-PIC-NOTIF
|Aggregate `[35]` high and `[34]` low at completion.
|CHK-PIC-NOTIF-W1C
|`notif_internal_sts` W1C clears `[35]`.
|CHK-ZEROIZE
|`ZEROIZE` drops VALID and every word of the pubkey window then reads zero. `MLDSA_STATUS.READY` is low through the `ABR_ZEROIZE` walk (`abr_ready = (abr_prog_cntr == ABR_RESET)`), so a READY-high wait would hang rather than pass vacuously. Scope: the pubkey read port is gated on `mldsa_valid_reg`, so a zero window proves the key is no longer readable, *not* that the RAM was wiped. The memory walk is not claimed; proving it needs a probe on the pubkey RAM or on `zeroize_mem_done`.
|CHK-SENSITIVITY
|Flipping one seed bit yields a different public key, and that key is not an all-zero window — an inequality against the vector alone would be satisfied by a cleared window.
|===
*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Anti-vacuity*: the public key is a cryptographic function of the seed words
written over the bus. The sensitivity leg flips one seed bit after the NIST
match, so a cached or constant engine passes the first leg and fails the second.
`[34]` is driven high through `error_intr_trig` and cleared before the KAT, so a
stuck-low aggregator cannot satisfy both `CHK-PIC-ERROR` and `CHK-PIC-NOTIF`.

*Not claimed*: ML-KEM-1024, ML-DSA sign/verify, and the key-vault seed path — a
green keyGen closes none of them. This leaf writes the seed window over the bus.

'''''

[[sep_entropy_pool_aperture_test]]
===== `sep_entropy_pool_aperture_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: Crypto and entropy

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`, `+esrc_noise_force`

*Source Module*: `sep_entropy_pool_aperture_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, entropy golden

*Randomization*: RANDCFG — extra pops past the watermark come from the run seed (`SepEntropyPoolCfg`). The alias set and all three unique-dead offsets are walked every seed, so the decode coverage is seed-independent.

*Objective*: The fabric entropy pool answers only its three live registers. Occupancy, low-watermark and fill-stall status reach software and the interrupt aggregator, a pop drains one word, an empty pop is refused, and every write and every unmapped offset inside the 64 KB window is refused with zero data.

*Test Coverage*: Three live pool registers; pop, empty-refuse, and unmapped refuse.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Every seed walks the high-bit mirrors of the three live registers, plus all three unique-dead offsets, so a truncated decode cannot hide behind a seeded pick.
. The block is a 64 KB crossbar target at `entropy_fifo.main` (`0x1095_0000`).
  Depth 32, `LowWatermark` 8, and `StallThresh` 4096 are independent goldens
  in the sequence (the `sep_entropy_fifo` defaults). They are not scraped
  from RTL.
. It fills from the native EDN endpoint after the shared entropy bring-up.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-POOL-LOW-HIGH
|Aggregate `[36]` high on empty (level 0).
|CHK-IRQ-CAUSE-LOW
|`0x08=0x1` at empty (`pool_low`), not a copy of status `0x40`.
|CHK-POOL-FILL
|`fifo_level` at or above the watermark. `pool_err` reads 0, but the pool FIFO is instantiated with `Secure=0` (`sep_entropy_fifo.sv`), and `prim_fifo_sync` then ties `err_o` low, so `status[8]`, irq-cause[2] and aggregate `[38]` are tied off in this build. The zero is not a fault-free claim and no pool integrity fault is claimed.
|CHK-POOL-LOW-LOW
|Aggregate `[36]` low after fill.
|CHK-IRQ-CAUSE-IDLE
|`0x08` traverses `0x1 -> 0x0` after fill, not a copy of status.
|CHK-WRITE-SLVERR
|Status write returns SLVERR and does not change `fifo_level` while the pool is not full (a full FIFO cannot show a refused fill).
|CHK-UNMAPPED
|Every high-bit mirror of a live register, plus all three unique-dead offsets, returns SLVERR with RDATA=0. All three are walked every seed: `0x18` is the unused `[4:3]=11` code and catches a decode class the other two do not, so a seeded pick of one could miss it.
|CHK-STALL-ASSERT
|`[37]` high after `StallThresh` with the pool not full.
|CHK-IRQ-CAUSE-STALL
|`0x08=0x2` at stall from level vs `LowWatermark` (`fill_stall`, not `pool_low`), not `{status[7], status[6]}`.
|CHK-STALL-CLEAR
|`[37]` low after EDN and ESRC re-enable.
|CHK-POP-DATA
|Accepted pops OKAY, level minus one, nonzero distinct words.
|CHK-POOL-LOW-REHIGH
|`[36]` high again after drain (high-low-high).
|CHK-POOL-LOW-EDGE
|On every status read the test makes, `status[6]` agrees with `level <
LowWatermark` and the `[36]` aggregate agrees with `status[6]`. The fill lands on
the watermark and the drain steps through the level below it, so the comparator
boundary itself is sampled -- an off-by-one there survives a test that only looks
at a filled pool and an empty one.
|CHK-EMPTY-SLVERR
|Pop at `0x10` on empty returns SLVERR, after an accepted pop.
|CHK5_pool
|Native EDN beats reached the pool adapter (observe-mode floor). Bit-exact per-beat routing stays with `sep_esrc_e2e_smoke_test`, not this row.
|CHK-ALERTS-ZERO
|CSRNG/EDN error and recoverable-alert status are clear after the aperture walk.
|===
*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Anti-vacuity*: fill is observed at or above the watermark before any refusal is
scored. An accepted pop happens before the empty-pop SLVERR. `[36]` is seen
high, then low, then high. `[37]` is the first fill-stall after the fill path is
starved for `StallThresh` with the pool not full. At least one popped word is
nonzero and two popped words differ.

*RTL fact that arms `[37]`*: ESRC CTRL `MODULE_ENABLE=0` does not drop AUTO-mode
EDN acks while CSRNG still has a seed, and after that disable the pool runs to
full (level 32). `fill_stall` needs `req_pending && !edn_ack` for `StallThresh`,
and a full pool holds `req_pending` low. The stall wait therefore also writes
EDN_CTRL `EDN_ENABLE=False` (legal mubi4, other fields unchanged) and pops
leftover words until the pool has room and acks have stopped.

*Not claimed*: full PIC delivery (claim / mask / ISR). Drain-under-fill and
stall-duration / recovery stress are not exercised here.

'''''

[[sep_fabric_inbound_filter_rule_matrix_test]]
===== `sep_fabric_inbound_filter_rule_matrix_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: Fabric and remap

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), *real PROD fuse sense* — no `+skip_fuse_sense`

*Source Module*: `sep_fabric_inbound_filter_rule_matrix_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Randomization*: RAND-REP — entries 0 and 7 × two windows × `{rw, r, w}` at match-all, plus entry 0 / window 0 source-ID match and mismatch, run every seed. Seed varies the window data, the entry-15 granule window, and the bound-probe registers.

*Objective*: Brings the lifecycle controller up in PROD so the inbound filter is genuinely active (`sep_debug=0`, value-checked against the lifecycle golden), then walks entries 0 and 7, two address windows, and `{rw, read-only, write-only}` at `src_id=0` (match-all), plus entry 0 / window 0 x `{rw, r, w}` at `src_id=0x5` with a matching AXI `user[3:0]` and one src-mismatch cell — 16 cells in one invocation — then the same-page `allow_burst=1` granule, page-bound, and `FILTER_CONFIG.locked` on entry 15.

*Test Coverage*: Sixteen inbound-filter cells plus burst granule, page-bound, and lock.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Bring the lifecycle controller up in PROD so the inbound filter is active.
. Walk entries 0 and 7, two windows, `{rw, r, w}`, and the source-ID match / mismatch cells — 16 cells.
. Walk `allow_burst` deny then allow on AR and AW of the scratch window (2-beat INCR, AxLEN=1). The allow window spans two 4 KB pages. The allowed burst becomes two Lite singles; the denied burst reaches none.
. Program an 8-byte `allow_burst=1` window inside one 4 KB page on entry 15. Prove the page grant on the bus, both page edges, then freeze `allow_burst` with `FILTER_CONFIG.locked`. The lock is sticky, so this step is last.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-ALLOW-RULE
|An allowed external read returns OKAY *and* the exact staged value. A denied read returns the error-slave sentinel, so allow and deny are distinguished by data as well as by response code.
|CHK-BLOCK-DEFAULT
|A non-allowed external read in the *same CSR page* returns DECERR — so region reachability is proven by the allow case and cannot explain the deny.
|CHK-READ-ALLOWED
|The read-allowed bit gates the matched read in both directions.
|CHK-WRITE-ALLOWED
