|The write-allowed bit gates the matched write in both directions, and the allowed write lands (confirmed by a CPU-side readback).
|CHK-OWNERSHIP
|The CPU-LSU reads inbound CFG, outbound CFG[0], alias/AP/STEE remap bases, `SEP_GLOBAL_BASE_ADDR`, and `SEP_REGION_SIZE`. The external master completes DECERR on read and write of each address, and the denied write does not land. Those CSRs stay outside every allow window (firmware must not allow-list them). `SEP_GLOBAL_BASE_ADDR` and `SEP_REGION_SIZE` share the window-0 (`SEP_SW_DEBUG`) 4 KB page, so they also prove the live window is START/END, not the page.
|CHK-NONVAC
|Allow and block were both observed under one lifecycle state.
|CHK-SRC-ID
|A non-zero `FILTER_CONFIG.src_id` matches only the external master's `user[3:0]`. The match cells keep r/w-allow gating; the mismatch cell DECERRs with both allow bits set.
|CHK-RAND-REP
|Entries 0 and 7, two address windows, `{rw, r, w}` at match-all, plus entry 0 / window 0 source-ID match and mismatch — 16 cells in one invocation. Entry 15 carries the granule, bound, and lock cells.
|CHK-BURST-DENY
|With `allow_burst=0`, a single-beat external read of the scratch window is OKAY (anti-vacuous) and a 2-beat INCR (AxLEN=1) to the same address is DECERR. `traffic_filter.sv` `pass_burst` is not vacuously true.
|CHK-BURST-ALLOW
|With `allow_burst=1` and a programmed window that already spans two 4 KB pages, the same 2-beat INCR is OKAY and returns the staged scratch value. START/END read back as programmed (the same-page 4 KB granule widen does not fire). The granule widen itself is `CHK-PAGE-WIDEN`.
|CHK-BURST-WRITE-DENY
|The write-side `traffic_filter` (`aw.len`) with `allow_burst=0`: a single-beat external write is OKAY and lands; a 2-beat INCR write is DECERR and does not land.
|CHK-BURST-WRITE-ALLOW
|With `allow_burst=1` and the same two-page window, a 2-beat INCR write is OKAY and the poke lands on a CPU-LSU readback. START/END stay the programmed range.
|CHK-BURST-TO-SINGLE
|A filter-allowed 2-beat INCR (AxLEN=1) becomes two AXI-Lite singles on the system-CSR bridge (`u_system_csr_a2l_1`): AR/AW at start and start+4. A denied AxLEN=1 produces zero Lite handshakes (filter before the converter). `hw/sys/sep/doc/fabric.adoc`: convert burst to single.
|CHK-PAGE-WIDEN
|A same-page `allow_burst=1` window grants the whole 4 KB page (`hw/common/axi/axi_filter/doc/index.adoc`: `START_ADDR` rounds down, `END_ADDR` rounds up, `allow_burst` selects 4 KB). Measured on the bus rather than in a register mirror: an external access to an address inside the granted page but outside the programmed `START..END` answers OKAY for read and write and carries the staged value. The hardware-adjusted `START_ADDR`/`END_ADDR` readback is the setup step showing the rewrite took effect; a CSR mirror is not evidence of what the filter passes.
|CHK-PAGE-BOUND
|The 4 KB grant stops at both page edges. Under a scratch-page grant an access one page below is refused; with that page granted instead it answers OKAY while the scratch address turns DECERR. Both probes are proven reachable first, so neither refusal can be a decode hole rather than a filter decision. This is the security contract: 4 KB is the block pitch for the DMA CSR, watchdog and scratch-bank blocks, so a page-crossing grant would reach a neighbouring block.
|CHK-CONFIG-LOCK
|`FILTER_CONFIG.locked` freezes `allow_burst`. Once locked, clearing `allow_burst` and clearing `locked` both answer SLVERR, the field keeps its value, and the frozen granule still grants the widened page. The freeze is not in the register block -- `locked` is a W1S bit with no write-enable over other fields -- but in `sep_system_csr.sv`, where a per-entry demux routes a locked entry's writes to an error slave while reads pass through. Asserting the response alone would not prove the field held, so the readback and the still-granted access are checked with it.
|===
*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Scope*: the walk is inbound-filter entries 0 and 7, two address
windows (`SEP_SW_DEBUG` and scratch-cold[0]), `{rw, read-only, write-only}` at
`src_id=0`, plus the source-ID product on entry 0 / window 0, then the
`allow_burst` deny/allow pair on AR and AW of scratch and the burst-to-single
Lite split, then the same-page 4 KB granule, page-bound, and `FILTER_CONFIG.locked`
on entry 15. `CHK-OWNERSHIP` keeps inbound CFG, outbound CFG, the remap banks, and `SEP_GLOBAL_BASE_ADDR` / `SEP_REGION_SIZE` outside every allow window. WRAP, FIXED and
`AxLEN>1` are not walked: the Lite bridges convert those bursts to singles, so the
filter only ever sees `tx_len==0`.

'''''

[[sep_fabric_remap_filter_csr_bank_test]]
===== `sep_fabric_remap_filter_csr_bank_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: Fabric and remap

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), `+skip_fuse_sense`, 3 seeds

*Source Module*: `sep_fabric_remap_filter_csr_bank_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Objective*: R/W, 64-bit, RO, valid, and write-once-set across the alias/AP/STEE remap and inbound/outbound filter CSR banks.

*Test Coverage*: Remap and filter CSR banks: R/W, 64-bit, RO, valid, write-once-set.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Write each bank and require the readback to differ from the observed pre-write.
. Prove RO `data_bus_width` ignores writes on both filters.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-NONVAC
|A written register's readback differs from its *observed* pre-write value, and the neighbouring field is undisturbed. The pre-write sample is required: asserting only that the written pattern is non-zero is a property of the stimulus constructor, not of storage.
|CHK-ALIAS-RW
|Region start, end and attribute registers are read/write, including the 64-bit upper word.
|CHK-AP-STEE-RW
|The output-remap CSRs are read/write across both banks.
|CHK-INFILT-CFG / CHK-OUTFILT-CFG
|Filter-configuration read/write fields (`rd`, `wr`, `ns`, `burst`, `src_id`, `group_id`) read back exactly on both the inbound and the outbound bank.
|CHK-RO
|The data-bus-width field reads its fixed value and ignores writes of the inverse pattern, on both filters.
|CHK-VALID-RW
|The region valid bit is read/write in both directions.
|CHK-WOSET
|The write-once-set lock *actively rejects* a clear attempt with SLVERR rather than silently ignoring it — the strongest check in this entry.
|===
*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Scope limit*: one region or entry per bank per seed. Cross-region isolation —
that writing region *k* leaves region *j* untouched — is not checked beyond the
immediate neighbour, and nothing records which indices a regression covered.

'''''

[[sep_axil_mailbox_iface_rand_test]]
===== `sep_axil_mailbox_iface_rand_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: Mailbox, IRQ, PIC

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), `+skip_fuse_sense`, 3 seeds

*Source Module*: `sep_axil_mailbox_iface_rand_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Objective*: 64-bit TX FIFO, status, threshold IRQ, empty/full errors, and flush on the outbound mailbox aperture.

*Test Coverage*: Outbound mailbox TX FIFO, status, threshold IRQ, errors, and flush.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Confirm empty FIFOs in STATUS, then push native 64-bit WRITE_DATA beats.
. Overfill and empty-read must SLVERR with the documented error flags; flush drains TX.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-NONVAC
|The empty transmit FIFO is reflected in STATUS, compared field-by-field against the mailbox golden. Receive-FIFO bits stay at their empty-reset values because this aperture never fills RX.
|CHK-64B
|Native 64-bit writes each occupy exactly one FIFO entry, so `depth` pushes reach exactly full. Occupancy is compared against the golden after every push, which is what makes the one-entry-per-beat claim real — a 32-bit split would trip `full` early. The transmit FIFO is write-only on this aperture, so payload survival is not claimed.
|CHK-WIRQT
|The write-threshold interrupt state matches the golden prediction, the pending register is gated by the enable (IRQP drops when IRQEN is cleared, IRQS stays), and the interrupt re-asserts after write-one-to-clear while still above threshold.
|CHK-ERR-RD
|A read of the empty FIFO returns SLVERR, the vendor block's `0xFEEDDEAD` sentinel (PULP `vendor/pulp-platform/axi/upstream/src/axi_lite_mailbox.sv` line 377), and a sticky read-error flag with read-clear semantics. The `read_error` flag is the documented contract (`hw/ip/axi_lite_mailbox_unit/doc/interface.adoc`); the sentinel value is a vendor constant with no TT-side specification, so the golden traces it to that source. Deny is distinguished three ways.
|CHK-ERR-IRQ / CHK-ERR-WR / CHK-ERR-WR-IRQ
|The error interrupt sets and clears on write-one-to-clear; a write to the full FIFO returns SLVERR and sets the write-error flag, with its own interrupt.
|CHK-FLUSH
|The control flush drains the transmit FIFO below the write threshold: not-full afterwards, and the threshold interrupt clears. Occupancy to empty is not observable on this aperture.
|CHK-SUBWORD-PUSH
|Write-side contract: a 4-byte `WRITE_DATA` beat is a whole push. Two 4-byte beats, at `+0x00` and then `+0x04`, are each answered OKAY and each occupy their own FIFO entry, so occupancy advances by two. `+0x04` is the same 8-byte register, not a high half. STATUS carries no exact depth, so occupancy is read through the write threshold programmed to 1: `write_level_above` must be clear after the first beat and set after the second. This does not grade the spec's two-word read of an incoming 64-bit message — see <<known-limitations>>.
|CHK-WDATA-RD
|The push register is write-only: a read of it returns the vendor block's `0xFEEDC0DE` constant (PULP `vendor/pulp-platform/axi/upstream/src/axi_lite_mailbox.sv` line 370, inherited through `axi_lite_mailbox_unit.sv`) with an OKAY response — not a FIFO entry and not an error — and leaves the transmit occupancy unchanged, re-compared against the golden afterwards.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Deferred*: the read half of the threshold pair. `RIRQT` and
`STATUS.read_level_above` need the receive FIFO filled from the peer side,
which this aperture cannot do, so the golden predicts `read_level_above` as
constant false and no vehicle grades it. Data round-trip readback is deferred
for the same reason. Both need the external inbound master — see
<<known-limitations>>.

'''''

[[sep_irq_simultaneous_fanin_no_alias_test]]
===== `sep_irq_simultaneous_fanin_no_alias_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: Mailbox, IRQ, PIC

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), `+skip_fuse_sense`, 3 seeds

*Source Module*: `sep_irq_simultaneous_fanin_no_alias_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Objective*: Simultaneous IP-IRQ fan-in into the aggregator: one-hot map, no alias, RW1C clear.

*Test Coverage*: Simultaneous IP-IRQ fan-in: one-hot map, no alias, RW1C clear.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Fire a single source and require exactly one aggregate bit.
. Fire several together; the `[8:33]` region must equal exactly the driven mask.
. Re-sample the region raw and require every bit in it to be resolved.
. Write-one-to-clear each source and require the region to return to zero.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-NONVAC
|A single source lights exactly one aggregate bit — the real per-bit map proof.
|CHK-FANIN-MAP
|Several sources asserted together light exactly their own bits, compared as an exact equality against the driven mask, and each source's own IP interrupt-state register reads 1. The per-IP read is independent of the aggregate.
|CHK-ANTI-ALIAS
|Every bit of `sep_internal_interrupts[8:33]` is resolved — neither X nor Z — and the resolved region equals exactly the driven set. The X half is the part the other checkers cannot reach: the region polls read through a helper that maps unknown bits to zero per bit, so an undriven aggregator leg is indistinguishable from a quiet source there. This checker re-samples the probe without that mapping and names the offending bit indices on failure.
|CHK-CLEAR
|All driven sources clear on write-one-to-clear and the region returns to zero.
|===
*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Resolution limit*: the region poll matches on *some* sample, so a transient
smear that clears before a later sample would pass. The checker wording reads as
an always-property; it is not one.

*Deferred*: CPU-ISR delivery of the simultaneous set, and the full 32-source
cross-product. One seed asserts one subset of at least two of the four
cross-IP sources; the `[8:33]` contract is identical for every subset, but no
single run covers every combination.

'''''

[[sep_pic_irq_source_map_delivery_test]]
===== `sep_pic_irq_source_map_delivery_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: Mailbox, IRQ, PIC

*Run mode*: `cpu`, firmware `pic_irq_source_map_test`, `+skip_fuse_sense`

*Source Module*: `sep_pic_irq_source_map_delivery_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, CPU FW

*Objective*: Prove the PIC delivers a seeded subset of interrupt sources to the CPU with one-hot claim and RW1C clear (MUST trio every seed).

*Test Coverage*: PIC delivers a seeded source subset with one-hot claim and RW1C.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Arm the MUST PIC sources plus the seeded extras and wait a quiet window with no ISR.
. Deliver OTBN done to the CPU; complete when the source de-asserts.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-NONVAC
|No spurious interrupt service before any trigger, over a quiet window with every selected source's interrupt enable already armed. The default vector-table handler increments `sep_dummy_int_count`; a non-zero count fails this checker, so an unregistered source cannot be swallowed silently.
|CHK-DUMMY
|After the source walk the same dummy-handler count is still 0, so an unregistered source did not fire during delivery either.
|CHK-DELIVER
|Each source's interrupt reaches the CPU and its handler runs. This also *carries the source-to-PIC-id map*, but by the claim id rather than by which handler ran: the firmware registers ONE shared service routine for every non-mailbox source, so the identity of the running handler distinguishes nothing among them. The routine reads the claim id out of `meihap` and increments the per-source delivery counter selected by that id, and the test waits on that specific counter. A wrong id therefore resolves to no selected source, or to a different one, and the wait expires either way.
|CHK-ONEHOT
|Triggering one source does not fire another *selected* source. Every selected PIC source stays interrupt-enabled throughout, including after the mailbox ISR (WIRQT is raised past occupancy rather than masking IRQEN). Sources outside the per-seed selection are not observed, so this is not a claim about the full aggregator.
|CHK-IP-RW1C
|Event-type sources and the mailbox clear by write-one-to-clear of `INTR_STATE` / IRQS. DMA is Status-type: `INTR_STATE` is read-only, and the ISR writes INTR_TEST=0 (and W1C `STATUS`). The witness is the IP state bit re-read as 0 inside the handler.
|CHK-PIC-COMPLETE
|No source re-fires after its handler cleared it — the claim completed, no storm. The quiet window runs with every selected source still interrupt-enabled.
|CHK-RANDCFG
|Firmware consumed the patched MUST trio plus two seed-selected extras: the logged `SCENARIO` line must match the Python config exactly, which is what rules out an inert patch running only the compiled-in MUST trio. The firmware's own line here reports only that its parameters resolved — the compiled default satisfies that — so the host-side needle owns this contract.
|CHK-FW-REPORTED
|Every firmware checker above appears in the console, AND the per-source checkers appear once per selected source. The pass magic cannot show that a source was skipped or that a check was compiled out; a line count short of the selected-source count can. The pass verdict itself is asserted before any summary line is logged, so a kept log cannot carry a PASS summary under a failing verdict.
|===
*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Randomization*: RANDCFG — the MUST trio (mailbox, OTBN done, HMAC done) walks
every seed; two extras are drawn per seed from a five-member pool, so one run
exercises two of the five and three go untouched. With three seeds a run covers
at most six draws and is not guaranteed to reach all five. The map contract is
identical for every draw; which extras are graded is seed-dependent and is not
tallied.

*Not claimed*: "wake on wait-for-interrupt, not by polling". The wait helper loops
on a `wfi` and a counter, so a `wfi` implemented as a no-op degenerates to a spin
poll and yields the same pass.

'''''

[[sep_spi_ot_dma_tx_test]]
===== `sep_spi_ot_dma_tx_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: SPI and DMA

*Run mode*: `cpu`, firmware `spi_ot_dma_tx_test`, 3 seeds

*Source Module*: `sep_spi_ot_dma_tx_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, ocah_spi_vip, CPU FW

*Objective*: Drives SRAM through the secure DMA into the OpenTitan SPI host transmit FIFO and on to the flash BFM, then reads the flash back and compares against the cocotb-seeded random source data. After the walk, a TXDATA write past `STATUS.TXFULL` must latch exclusive `ERROR_STATUS.OVERFLOW` and the host must run again after recovery.

*Test Coverage*: DMA SRAM to SPI TX to flash, readback compare, and TX overflow recovery.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Walk the seeded length and trigger cells: DMA SRAM into the OpenTitan SPI TX FIFO, page-program the flash BFM, read the flash back and compare to the SRAM source; W1C-clear DMA done.
. Write TXDATA past `STATUS.TXFULL`; expect exclusive `ERROR_STATUS.OVERFLOW`; `CTRL.SW_RST` plus write-one-to-clear; a flash status read must complete.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-TRIGGER
|The transmit watermark tracks FIFO occupancy across the threshold, with the exact polarity asserted and the observed values logged. `EVENT_ENABLE.TXWM` is programmed.
|CHK-DMA-TX
|The flash content equals the SRAM source, compared against cocotb-seeded random data.
|CHK-DMA-DONE
|The DMA done bit clears on write-one-to-clear and reads back clear. Handshake mode does not raise `STATUS.chunk_done` (RTL: chunk_done is the inverse of cfg_handshake_en); that status is `sep_dma_basic_test`.
|CHK-SPI-IDLE
|The SPI host returns to idle with a clean error status.
|CHK-NONVAC
|The flash readback holds content that is not the erased 0xFF pattern, so the content compare above is not erased-against-erased. Scanned on the value the flash returned, not on the staged source, which the config forces non-erased and could never fail.
|CHK-RAND-REP
|The cocotb golden walked the deterministic length and trigger cells, requiring a literal floor of write-enable and page-program transactions on the bus and a per-case address and data match.
|CHK-MULTICHUNK
|Every PAGE PROGRAM the device received carries more *payload* than one 16-byte DMA chunk, so the transmit-watermark refill loop iterated. Counted from the bus, not from the scenario table, and counting payload only: adding the four opcode and address header bytes would let a transfer that delivered exactly one chunk and stopped clear a 16-byte bar.
|CHK-ERR-OVERFLOW
|A TXDATA write past `STATUS.TXFULL` latches exactly `ERROR_STATUS.OVERFLOW`; `CTRL.SW_RST` plus write-one-to-clear then lets a flash status read complete.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

'''''

[[sep_spi_ot_flash_cmd_rand_test]]
===== `sep_spi_ot_flash_cmd_rand_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: SPI and DMA

*Run mode*: `cpu`, firmware `spi_ot_flash_cmd_test`, 3 seeds

*Source Module*: `sep_spi_ot_flash_cmd_rand_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, ocah_spi_vip, CPU FW

*Objective*: JEDEC, WREN/RDSR, WRDI, page program, FAST_READ, and sector erase against the flash BFM, with a neighbour-sector intact check, write-protect and status-register breadth, and the three host `ERROR_STATUS` classes with recovery.

*Test Coverage*: JEDEC, program, read, erase, write-protect, and host error-status recovery.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. READ ID, then prove WEL set by WREN and cleared by WRDI.
. Program, FAST_READ, and sector-erase with neighbour intact.
. After the erase: prove WEL consumed; a page program with WEL clear must not land; opcode `0x35` returns the seeded status-register-2 value.
. `ERROR_STATUS` is zero after that sequence. Then inject underflow, reserved `CMD.SPEED`, and an out-of-range chip-select, each exclusive and recovered; READ ID works again.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-PROGRAM / CHK-READ
|A page program followed by a read returns the exact random pattern at the exact random address.
|CHK-ERASE
|A sector erase leaves the programmed region reading all-ones and leaves the adjacent 4 KiB neighbour sector intact. The region is proven non-erased earlier in the walk, by the page program and read-back pair that `CHK-PROGRAM`/`CHK-READ` covers, so the all-ones result is a change of state rather than a region that was never written.
|CHK-NO-ERROR
|The SPI host error status is zero after the sequence.
|CHK-JEDEC
|READ ID returns the BFM JEDEC identity.
|CHK-RDSR
|RDSR sees WEL set after WREN. A controller timeout is a distinct failure, not a status byte that looks like WEL.
|CHK-WRDI
|WRDI runs on a WEL that RDSR just proved set, then RDSR sees WEL clear.
|CHK-FAST-READ
|FAST_READ returns the programmed pattern.
|CHK-WEL-AUTOCLR
|The erase consumes the write-enable latch. The latch is state of the SPI flash device model, not of SEP: what this grades is that the controller drove a well-formed erase the device accepted and retired, and it fails if the controller leaves the device armed. It is not RTL coverage of a SEP register, and takes no feature credit as such.
|CHK-WP-PP
|A page program issued with the write-enable latch clear does not land. The firmware reads the sector back over SPI and sees all-ones; the golden then reads the device model's stored array directly, off the SPI read path, so a program that landed while the read path returned a stuck all-ones value fails. The model records no payload on that transaction. The model drains to chip-select-high without decoding the address phase when the latch is clear, so this cannot separate a device refusal from a truncated command.
|CHK-RDSR2
|Opcode `0x35` returns the seeded non-zero status-register-2 value. A decode that folds `0x35` onto `0x05` returns the write-enable SR1 encoding (`0x02`) and fails. A receive path that returns all-zero also fails. The firmware does not re-read SR1 on this step; the fail cases are the two wrong bytes, not a live SR1 compare.
|CHK-ERR-UNDERFLOW
|Reading `RXDATA` with the receive FIFO empty latches exactly `ERROR_STATUS.UNDERFLOW`, then write-one-to-clear releases it.
|CHK-ERR-CMDINVAL
|A command with the reserved speed encoding latches exactly `ERROR_STATUS.CMDINVAL`, then write-one-to-clear releases it.
|CHK-ERR-CSIDINVAL
|A segment whose chip-select is outside `NUM_CS` latches exactly `ERROR_STATUS.CSIDINVAL`, then write-one-to-clear releases it.
|CHK-ERR-RECOVER
|READ ID completes with the BFM JEDEC identity after the three injections, so the host was released rather than left disabled.
|CHK-RAND-REP
|The seed varies the programmed address, word count and payload. The opcode sequence is fixed, and the BFM golden requires that exact observed order.
|===
*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Scope*: the transaction-order golden requires the BFM-visible opcode sequence
(JEDEC, WREN, RDSR, WRDI, program/read, FAST_READ, neighbour program/read, erase,
primary and neighbour read, post-erase status, the ignored write-protect program,
RDSR2, and the recovery READ ID). The three `ERROR_STATUS` injections put nothing
on the bus: the host disables its core the cycle the error latches. Dual and quad
modes stay infrastructure-gated and are not exercised. They are BFM-gated, not a
feature-scorecard hole.

'''''

[[sep_spi_ot_host_csr_irq_rand_test]]
===== `sep_spi_ot_host_csr_irq_rand_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: Mailbox, IRQ, PIC

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), `+skip_fuse_sense`, 3 seeds

*Source Module*: `sep_spi_ot_host_csr_irq_rand_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Objective*: OpenTitan SPI host CSR reset, R/W, interrupt test, and error-status W1C.

*Test Coverage*: SPI host CSR reset, R/W, interrupt test, and error-status W1C.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Read the eight control registers at documented reset with STATUS idle.
. Walk each read/write register: write, read back, then drive its unimplemented bits and require them to read zero without disturbing the writable ones.
. Drive the interrupt test register with the enable clear and then set.
. Induce each of the six error conditions in turn and write-one-to-clear it.
. Fill the transmit FIFO across the programmed watermark.
. Measure the enabled command drain, then hold the same command off with the core disabled for a multiple of that measurement.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-RESET
|Eight control registers read their documented reset values and the status register reads idle. Five of the eight expect zero, which a tied-off decode would also return — mitigated within this entry by the writability, interrupt and error checks below, each of which drives a register non-zero.
|CHK-REG-RW
|Each read/write register accepts a write and reads it back exactly. Each register that HAS unimplemented bits additionally has those bits driven to one and confirmed to read back zero, with the writable bits undisturbed. The probe pattern is derived from the register's implemented mask, not from the walk mask: `CTRL.SW_RST` is implemented and deliberately outside the walk, and driving it would soft-reset the core mid-walk. `CSID` implements all 32 bits and so has no unimplemented half to probe.
|CHK-NONVAC
|EVERY walked register's readback differs from its own observed pre-write value. Where a seeded value happens to equal what the register already holds, the test flips the lowest writable bit instead, so no register on any seed can satisfy this by writing back what was already there.
|CHK-INTR
|The interrupt test register drives interrupt status, gated by the enable — mask proven in both directions — and clearing the source deasserts it.
|CHK-ERR-W1C
|Six error conditions (UNDERFLOW, CMDINVAL, CSIDINVAL, OVERFLOW, CMDBUSY, ACCESSINVAL) are each induced by their own trigger, observed set, and cleared by write-one-to-clear, with a clean precondition asserted first. Each trigger must set its OWN bit and no other: the clean precondition makes exclusivity checkable, and without it a device that raised every error bit on any stimulus would satisfy all six legs. These are the six bits the generated register block defines; none is left unprovoked.
|CHK-WATERMARK
|`STATUS.TXWM` follows the RTL polarity (set while TXQD is below the watermark): set while empty and at occupancy `wm-1`, clear at the exact watermark and after filling past it.
|CHK-ENABLE
|Clearing the enable holds the queued command off; setting it drains the transmit FIFO. The negative window is not a constant: the test first measures how many polls the same one-word command takes to drain with the core ENABLED, then requires the disabled command to survive a fixed multiple of that measurement, with `TXQD` still 1 and `TXEMPTY` clear at the end of it. A device that ignored the enable but merely executed slowly would pass a hand-picked window; it cannot pass a calibrated one.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

'''''

[[sep_sram_datapath_breadth_test]]
===== `sep_sram_datapath_breadth_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: Memory, boot, reset, WDT, clock

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), `+skip_fuse_sense`

*Source Module*: `sep_sram_datapath_breadth_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Objective*: SRAM strobe, pattern, sequential, and partial-write breadth on the 64-bit port.

*Test Coverage*: Every contiguous WSTRB mask, the 64-bit pattern set, sequential distinct words, and the base plus last-valid SRAM word.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Walk every contiguous WSTRB mask against a Python strobe model.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-WSTRB
|Every contiguous byte-strobe mask changes only its own byte lanes, with neighbours preserved — compared against a Python strobe model. A strobe-ignoring memory would zero the neighbours and fail, because unselected lanes carry zero data.
|CHK-PATTERN
|A set of 64-bit data patterns — all-ones alternating, walking one, walking zero — reads back exactly.
|CHK-SEQ
|Consecutive single-beat 64-bit words hold distinct per-address values, which is the real address-decode proof in this entry.
|CHK-BOUNDARY
|The base address and the last valid SRAM word both read and write exactly. The extent is the generated map, not a doc literal: `OCH_SEP_TOP_SEP_SRAM_BASE_ADDR` = `0x10000000` and `OCH_SEP_TOP_SEP_SRAM_SIZE` = `0x40000` in `hw/sys/sep/regs/gen/sv/sep_addrmap_pkg.sv`, matching SRAM_N_ENTRIES = 256 * 1024 / 8 in `hw/top/sep_ip_integration.sv`, so the top valid 64-bit word is `0x1003fff8`. One-past-top decode-error is `sep_axi_map_refuse_test`, not this row: that address is the ROM base.
|CHK-NONVAC
|Two addresses are written with complementary patterns and both are read back and value-checked, so a read path returning the same value for both fails. Requiring an *unwritten* neighbour to differ from a non-zero pattern is not claimed — that difference is already guaranteed by the pattern constructor; see <<audit>>.
|===
*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Infrastructure gate*: non-contiguous byte strobes are not checked — the AXI
extension derives the strobe from address and length — and are recorded as
deferred rather than covered.

'''''

[[sep_dma_basic_test]]
===== `sep_dma_basic_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: SPI and DMA

*Run mode*: `cpu`, firmware `dma_basic_test`, 3 seeds

*Source Module*: `sep_dma_basic_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, CPU FW

*Objective*: Secure-DMA control and copy breadth over the CPU LSU: documented reset values, the configuration lock reading locked while the engine is observed busy, the range-valid gate and its rw0c lock each with a positive control, FIXED/INCR/WRAP against a per-mode golden, 1/2/4-byte widths, `STATUS.done` RW1C, one invalid-opcode error with recovery, the address and inverted-range refusals, a DMA-issued host-path command-integrity inject, and a fabric DECERR dest that raises `host_path_err` with the pin low. The mode and width walk is directed.

*Test Coverage*: Secure-DMA locks, address modes, widths, errors, and host-path integrity.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Read DMA config registers at documented reset, then prove CFG_REGWEN lock while BUSY.
. While the range registers are still writable: `RANGE_VALID=0` raises `range_valid_error`, and an enabled range whose limit sits below its base raises exclusive `base_limit_error` with `STATUS.done` clear. Then lock `RANGE_REGWEN`.
. Walk INCR / FIXED / WRAP × 1B/2B/4B; consume the seed-patched SCENARIO line.
. Provoke one invalid opcode, then four exclusive address-alignment refusals; a clean copy after each error class proves the engine recovers.
. With `dma_host_intg_inject_i` high, one legal SRAM copy raises exclusive `host_path_err` and aggregator bit 41. `DMA_BUS_ERR_CLEAR` returns STATUS and bit 41 to 0. A legal copy with the pin low then matches the source snapshot.
. With the pin low, one copy whose destination is the first word past the DMA CSR window raises exclusive `host_path_err` from the fabric DECERR. CLEAR and a legal recovery copy follow.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-RESET
|The DMA configuration registers read their documented reset values.
|CHK-CFG-REGWEN
|The model check in this entry: the configuration lock reads locked while the engine is busy — with busy actually observed, failing loudly if the copy finished too fast — and the *same* write is rejected while locked and lands while unlocked.
|CHK-RANGE-REGWEN
|The range-valid gate produces the documented error, an enabled range whose limit sits below its base raises `base_limit_error` *exclusively* and sets no `STATUS.done`, and the range base register rejects a write once locked. A positive control precedes the lock — a non-reset value is written and proven to land first — so "unchanged after the lock" cannot pass identically for a read-only register or a missing decode.
|CHK-COPY-MODE
|Increment, fixed-source, fixed-destination and wrap addressing each produce their expected image against an independent local snapshot of the source fill, with a sentinel neighbour check per iteration.
|CHK-WIDTH
|Each transfer-width encoding is written *and read back*, and the copy is byte-exact. Without the read-back the three iterations were indistinguishable — 16 bytes is a whole multiple of 1, 2 and 4, so a register stuck at its reset produced identical output.
|CHK-DONE-RW1C
|The done bit is observed set and then cleared to zero.
|CHK-ERR-OPCODE
|An invalid opcode raises exactly the opcode error — asserted *exclusively*, so no other error bit may be set — with the done bit required clear, then cleared by write-one-to-clear. The recovery copy after the error matches the independent source snapshot.
|CHK-ERR-ADDR
|The address validation the engine applies before it moves a byte. Four refused descriptors, each asserting its `ERROR_CODE` bit *exclusively*: a 4-byte transfer whose source is not 4-byte aligned raises `src_addr_error`, the same on the destination raises `dst_addr_error`, a 2-byte transfer on an odd source raises `src_addr_error`, and a 2-byte transfer on an odd destination raises `dst_addr_error`. None may set `STATUS.done`, and a clean copy after the four proves the engine recovers. The inverted-range leg sits in `CHK-RANGE-REGWEN`, which must run before the one-way range lock closes.
|CHK-HOSTINTG
|A DMA-issued host command under `dma_host_intg_inject_i` raises exactly `DMA_BUS_ERR_STATUS.host_path_err`. Aggregator bit 41 is 1 and bit 40 stays 0. `DMA_BUS_ERR_CLEAR` returns STATUS and bit 41 to 0. A legal copy with the pin low then matches the independent source snapshot. Each leg also requires the transfer to terminate, so a wedged engine cannot pass on a latch raised from its first command.
|CHK-HOSTFABRIC
|A DMA transfer whose destination is the first word past the DMA CSR window, with the inject pin low, raises exactly `host_path_err`. `DMA_BUS_ERR_CLEAR` returns STATUS to 0. The CLEAR register is a write-only single-pulse field that always reads 0, so its read value is not graded. A legal copy then matches the independent source snapshot.
|CHK-RAND-REP
|Mode and width cells walk every seed; src/dst/length/fill come from the run seed.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Not claimed*: lockstep punch-through. The register path (`reg_path_err`) is not
exercised by this leaf at all; the unmapped-window term is proven on the
`sep_irq_ip_to_aggregator_test` card. Persistence across a CPU-only reset, and
the rule that a fault arriving in the clear cycle still latches — both stated in
the register description — are unproven here.

'''''

[[sep_boot_rom_lsu_read_test]]
===== `sep_boot_rom_lsu_read_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: Memory, boot, reset, WDT, clock

*Run mode*: `cpu`, firmware `rom_lsu_read_test`

*Source Module*: `sep_boot_rom_lsu_read_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, CPU FW

*Objective*: LSU reads of the Boot ROM match the loaded image; stores to ROM are ignored.

*Test Coverage*: LSU reads of the boot ROM match the image; stores to ROM are ignored.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. LSU-read ten half-words of the loaded Boot ROM image.
. Store to ROM; content must stay the image literal and the CPU must not trap.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-ROM-READ
|Load-store reads of ten half-words across five distinct 64-bit ROM words match the loaded image, against hard-coded literals. Nine of the ten expectations are non-zero, so a stuck-at-zero read path fails.
|CHK-ROM-BOUNDARY
|The last valid ROM word reads back exactly. One-past-top decode-error is `sep_axi_map_refuse_test`, not this row.
|CHK-ROM-WRITE-IGNORED
|A store to ROM leaves the content unchanged, compared against the *image literal* rather than against a pre-write read of the same address, and the CPU does not trap. The store's AXI response is not sampled on this firmware path.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

'''''

[[sep_warm_cold_reset_scratch_test]]
===== `sep_warm_cold_reset_scratch_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: Memory, boot, reset, WDT, clock

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), `+skip_fuse_sense`

*Source Module*: `sep_warm_cold_reset_scratch_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Objective*: Warm vs cold scratch: warm bank clears, cold bank retains, both writable after release.

*Test Coverage*: Warm bank clears, cold bank retains, both writable after release.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Write both scratch banks, then pulse warm reset.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-NONVAC
|Both scratch banks hold written values before any reset, so the retain and clear checks below are observations rather than reset-value reads.
|CHK-WARM-RST
|A warm reset source drops the CPU reset observable (assert then release). Isolation of the system-reset observable is not this row: `wdt_rst_ni_i` has no fan-out to it.
|CHK-WARM-RECOVER
|Both banks are writable again after the reset releases.
|CHK-COLD-REINIT
|A cold reset clears the cold bank too.
|CHK-WARM-CLEAR / CHK-WARM-RETAIN
|The warm-reset scratch bank clears while the cold bank retains its value across the same event — the pair that makes the domain split real.
|CHK-BANK-ALIAS
|Each of the sixteen implemented registers holds its own pattern, so an index-to-index or bank-to-bank alias fails.
|CHK-WARM-BANK
|A warm reset clears every warm register and leaves every cold register at its pattern.
|CHK-COLD-BANK
|A cold reset clears every register of both banks. Both banks are re-armed with non-reset values immediately before the cold reset, so no assertion in the sweep compares a zero against a register the earlier warm reset had already cleared.
|===
*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Independence limit*: the front-door and back-door values are not independent —
the probe is assigned from the same flops the register read returns — so their
agreement catches a read-mux fault, not an independent confirmation that the
reset landed.

'''''

[[sep_cpu_dbg_reset_independence_test]]
===== `sep_cpu_dbg_reset_independence_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: Memory, boot, reset, WDT, clock

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), `+skip_fuse_sense`

*Source Module*: `sep_cpu_dbg_reset_independence_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Objective*: the CPU and system reset observables are released at rest, and the
CPU observable is live under a real reset source. `dbg_rstb_i` isolation is not
claimed: the pin has no netlist path to either observable in this build.

*Test Coverage*: CPU and system reset observables at rest; debug-reset independence.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Both reset observables released at rest with `dbg_rstb_i` high.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-BASELINE
|Both reset observables are released at rest.
|CHK-LIVE
|A real reset source drops the CPU reset observable and then restores it, so the observable is live rather than stuck at one. This is what keeps the entry worth running.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*No scorecard row, deliberately.* The denominator rule is that the architecture
documents define the feature set. `dbg_rstb_i` is named in them — a port-table
line in `hw/sys/sep/doc/port_table.adoc` and a sentence in the integrator guide
describing a separate reset for the EL2 CPU debug module — but neither states a
behavioural contract, and nothing requires isolation from the system reset. A feature the specification does not define is not a
row, so this entry earns none and its absence is not an oversight. The two
checkers above are worth running as a reset-tree liveness guard; they are not a
feature credit. Closing the isolation claim needs both a run mode where
`dbg_rstb_i` reaches `sep_cpu` and a specification statement to check it against.

'''''

[[sep_wdt_aon_timer_internals_test]]
===== `sep_wdt_aon_timer_internals_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: Memory, boot, reset, WDT, clock

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), `+skip_fuse_sense`, 3 seeds

*Source Module*: `sep_wdt_aon_timer_internals_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Objective*: Wakeup and watchdog tick, threshold expiry, wakeup cause and its RW1C, the watchdog pet, and the REGWEN lock on the AON timer. Bark and bite are not claimed here: `sep_nmi_sanity_test` and `sep_reset_wdt_sanity_test` own those legs, and no checker in this entry observes either.

*Test Coverage*: AON timer tick, threshold, wakeup cause RW1C, pet, and REGWEN.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Expire the wakeup threshold and W1C-clear the interrupt.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-WKUP-COUNT
|The wakeup counter advances on its own tick, and `INTR_STATE.wkup_expired` stays 0 under the high threshold for that window.
|CHK-WKUP-EXPIRE
|The wakeup threshold expiry sets its interrupt, which then clears on write-one-to-clear.
|CHK-WKUP-CAUSE
|The wakeup cause register is still set after COUNT is reset, then write-0 clears it.
|CHK-WDOG-PET
|Petting the watchdog resets its counter. The observation window must run long enough that the pre-pet count exceeds the clock-crossing tolerance by a wide margin, and the post-pet value must be clearly below it. A tolerance wider than the count the window reaches passes when the pet is ignored — see <<audit>>.
|CHK-NONVAC
|A pre-lock threshold write lands, so the lock check below is not reading a reset value.
|CHK-REGWEN-LOCK
|Once locked, both `WDOG_BARK_THOLD` and `WDOG_BITE_THOLD` reject writes.
|CHK-WKUP-PRESCALE
|`WKUP_CTRL.prescaler` divides the wakeup count rate. The measurement window is sized from the programmed divisor so the divided run must advance (a frozen counter fails the lower bound) and must stay at or below the divisor bound (an ignored prescaler fails the upper bound). Both bounds come from the divisor, not from the undivided measurement.
|CHK-INTR-TEST
|`INTR_TEST` forces `INTR_STATE.wkup_expired` with the wakeup counter off, then write-one-to-clear releases it.
|CHK-REGWEN-SCOPE
|With the watchdog lock closed, `WKUP_THOLD_LO` and `WDOG_COUNT` still accept writes. The lock covers the watchdog configuration only. `WDOG_COUNT` is free-running and `WDOG_CTRL` is itself lock-covered, so the write is proven by LOWERING the counter, which free running cannot do: the counter first climbs past a literal floor, and the write to zero must then read back below the value it held. Raising the counter instead would be satisfied by free running alone on a blocked write, and a target near the seeded bark threshold would trip a bark.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

'''''

[[sep_otbn_compute_rand_test]]
===== `sep_otbn_compute_rand_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: Crypto and entropy

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), `+skip_fuse_sense +esrc_noise_force`

*Source Module*: `sep_otbn_compute_rand_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, entropy golden (strict CHK1..CHK4 plus `CHK5_otbn_urnd` in observe mode)

*Objective*: Loads a tiny RV32I program into OTBN IMEM, stages two seeded DMEM operands, issues EXECUTE, and checks `ERR_BITS==0` plus the DMEM result against an independent Python golden, while the strict entropy scoreboard runs against OTBN's live URND draw.

*Test Coverage*: OTBN ADD/XOR/AND vs an independent golden, with URND observed.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Bring up `no_cpu`, park AES/HMAC/KMAC, bring up entropy for OTBN URND wipe.
. Walk ADD, XOR, AND against the independent golden.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-ERRBITS
|Every compute cell retires with `ERR_BITS==0`.
|CHK-DMEM
|Every compute cell's DMEM result equals the Python golden for that op and those operands. The result word is pre-seeded with the golden's exact complement, so a program that never stores its result fails here rather than passing on a seed whose golden happens to be zero.
|CHK1..CHK4 / CHK5_otbn_urnd
|This entry also runs the strict entropy scoreboard against the chained golden, with `CHK5_otbn_urnd` in observe mode, because OTBN draws real URND entropy while it computes.
|CHK-RANDCFG
|ADD, XOR, and AND ran in one invocation. Operands come from the run seed.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

'''''

[[sep_clock_gate_control_test]]
===== `sep_clock_gate_control_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: Memory, boot, reset, WDT, clock

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), `+skip_fuse_sense`

*Source Module*: `sep_clock_gate_control_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Objective*: `CLOCK_GATE_CTRL` implements one bit (`pka_cg_enable[0:0]`); RTL sinks it into an unused net.

*Test Coverage*: `CLOCK_GATE_CTRL` storage and reserved-bit RAZWI; no live per-IP gate.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Write `pka_cg_enable` 1 then 0 and read it back.
. Read AES / HMAC / OTBN / SW_DEBUG CSRs with the bit 0 and 1.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-STORAGE
|The implemented bit writes and reads back in both directions.
|CHK-STUB-CONST
|AES STATUS, HMAC INTR_STATE, OTBN STATUS and SW_DEBUG read the *same value* with `pka_cg_enable` set and clear, not merely OKAY on both: the first read of each witness is kept and the second, taken with the bit flipped, must match it. A witness whose value moved with the enable would answer OKAY either way, so completing the access is not the contract. Bound: these CSRs are constant in this build, so the compare catches an enable that perturbs them and cannot by itself distinguish "the bit gates nothing" from "these witnesses never change".
|CHK-RSVD-RAZWI
|An all-ones write to both 32-bit halves of `CLOCK_GATE_CTRL` reads back only the implemented bit. An unimplemented bit that stores, or an alias onto the upper half, fails here.
|===
*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Scope limit*: the implemented contract is the designer placeholder, not a live
per-IP clock gate.

'''''

[[sep_efuse_program_lock_matrix_test]]
===== `sep_efuse_program_lock_matrix_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: eFuse, lifecycle, SEC_DIS, locked-field

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), real fuse sense

*Source Module*: `sep_efuse_program_lock_matrix_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Objective*: Program × lock on every legal spare field in one invocation.

*Test Coverage*: Program then lock on every legal spare field in one invocation.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Walk `SPARE0` through `SPARE7` in order. For each spare, program one seed-selected bit while unlocked; OTP readback and resense shadow match the independent image golden.
. Program that spare's own `LOCKS_SPARE` bit, then reject a second bit (`PROGRAM_ERR`). OTP and resense golden keep the rejected bit 0.
. Spare `k+1` is programmed only after spare `k` is locked, so a lock bit wired past its own slot fails the next spare's unlocked-program cell.
. The seed selects only the bit offsets inside each spare. Slot coverage does not depend on reseed.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-UNLOCK-PROGRAM
|An unlocked spare bit programs and reads back in OTP and after resense. Logged once per spare.
|CHK-LOCK-REJECT
|A write-locked spare rejects a later program; OTP and the post-resense shadow match the independent golden (lock bit set, rejected bit 0). Logged once per spare.
|CHK-SPARE-LOCK-SLOTS
|All eight spares ran both cells in one invocation. A lock of spare `k` must not block spare `k+1`.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

'''''

[[sep_locked_field_access_irq_path_test]]
===== `sep_locked_field_access_irq_path_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: Mailbox, IRQ, PIC

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, real fuse sense (no `+skip_fuse_sense`), `LC_PROD`

*Source Module*: `sep_locked_field_access_irq_path_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Objective*: Stage the spare field's lock bits in the OTP image (same spare-slot geometry as <<sep_efuse_program_lock_matrix_test>>), sense at `LC_PROD`, then access the *shadow* aperture.

*Test Coverage*: Locked-field write refuse and the matching interrupt path.

The shared config draws four distinct spares and stages `SIP_DIS` /
`SYS_DIS` at 0. That is this leaf's seed-to-image map.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Stage write-lock and read-lock on two seed-selected spare fields, plus an unlocked spare as contrast, in one OTP image, and sense at `LC_PROD`.
. Access the shadow aperture for each of the three, watching the interrupt probe for the duration of each access beat.
. This is not an OTP program: the program-lock vehicle already owns `PROGRAM_ERR`.
. An unlocked spare in the same image is the negative control.
. Walk write-lock then read-lock every seed — they are two different branches in `efuse_shadow_reg_access_control`.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-UNLOCKED
|Unlocked spare write/read never raises `[33]`; readback matches the write.
|CHK-WRITE-LOCK
|Bit [33] during the write, BRESP OKAY, following read equals pre-write, bit [33] low after retire.
|CHK-READ-LOCK
|Bit [33] during the read, RDATA sentinel, OKAY, bit [33] low after retire.
|===
*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Datapath facts that decide the checkers*: the lock decision is made in
`efuse_shadow_reg_access_control` (`hw/ip/efuse/rtl/`) from the sensed `LOCKS` /
`LOCKS_SPARE` vector: a write to a write-locked field or a read of a read-locked
field is answered with pslverr low, prdata matching the sentinel, and
`locked_field_access_interrupt_o` high, which aggregates into
`sep_internal_interrupts[33]`. Consequently:

* *The AXI write has no sentinel on BRESP* — APB `prdata` is not a
  write-response field. The write-lock denial proof is `[33]` high during the
  write, BRESP OKAY, then a read of the same field (write-lock does not block
  reads) returning the pre-write value. Do not expect SLVERR; JTAG deny is a
  different path (DECERR plus the same sentinel).
* *A read-lock has no following legal read* — `LOCKS` are OTP W1S, so the field
  stays read-locked. The read itself is the denial proof: RDATA=`0xbadcab1e`,
  OKAY, `[33]` high during the beat.
* *The interrupt is combinational and pulse-only* — there is no sticky status bit
  and no `W1C` on this path, so the clear contract is deassertion after the
  access retires. `sep_internal_interrupts_probe_o` must be watched continuously
  across the beat; a sample taken after the access completes is always 0.

*Anti-vacuity*: the locked and unlocked accesses are the same driver, the same
aperture and the same run, differing only in the lock bits of the field, so a
probe stuck low fails the assert leg and a probe stuck high fails the unlocked
leg. The deassert leg is credited only after the assert leg has been observed
high. The denial conjunct (unchanged write-lock readback / sentinel on read-lock)
attributes `[33]` to the locked path.

*Not claimed*: end-to-end delivery to the CPU — PIC claim, masking and ISR entry
— which needs `cpu` mode and a firmware image. This entry is
generate-and-deassert on both lock flavours of one seed-selected spare; the
`SECURE_TM_LOCK` fields are graded by <<sep_efuse_secure_tm_write_lock_test>>.

'''''

[[sep_efuse_secure_tm_write_lock_test]]
===== `sep_efuse_secure_tm_write_lock_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: Mailbox, IRQ, PIC

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, real fuse sense (no `+skip_fuse_sense`), `LC_PROD`

*Source Module*: `sep_locked_field_access_irq_path_test` (`+secure_tm_lock`)

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Objective*: Prove the field map's `SECURE_TM_LOCK` term refuses a shadow write while the latched TEST_EN strap is high, and that the identical write lands with the strap low.

*Test Coverage*: The `secure_tm` arm of the shadow-write lock, on the five apertures the lifecycle chapter names: `LOCKS`, `LOCKS_SPARE`, `LC_STATE`, `SIP_DIS` and `SYS_DIS`.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Sense at `LC_PROD` with the TEST_EN strap low.
. Write a seeded payload to `LOCKS`, `LOCKS_SPARE`, `LC_STATE`, `SIP_DIS` and `SYS_DIS` and read each back: the strap-low half, where every write must land.
. Raise `test_en_strap_i` and re-sense, so the strap is latched into `secure_tm`. The re-sense reloads every shadow word from the OTP image, so each field's pre-write value in the half below is the sensed value again, not what the half above wrote.
. Write the same payload to the same five fields, watching the interrupt probe across each beat, and read each back.
. Write a `SECURE_TM_UNLOCK` spare with the strap still up.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-SECTM-CONTROL
|With the strap low, the readback is the OR-merge of the pre-write value and the payload, the value actually moves, and `[33]` stays low.
|CHK-SECTM-BLOCK
|With the strap high, the stored value is unchanged, `[33]` pulses during the beat and is low after it retires, and the response is OKAY rather than SLVERR.
|CHK-SECTM-CONTRAST
|A `SECURE_TM_UNLOCK` spare still takes a write with the strap high, and its readback equals what was written.
|===
*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Datapath facts that decide the checkers*: the write-lock term is
`secure_tm_i & lock[3]` in `efuse_shadow_reg_access_control`
(`hw/ip/efuse/rtl/efuse_shadow_reg_access_control.sv`), where `lock` is the
static per-field entry of the compile-time field map in
`hw/sys/sep/rtl/efuse/sep_efuse_pkg.sv`, not a fuse bit. `LOCKS` and
`LOCKS_SPARE` are one map entry, so a refusal on either is the same lock.
A refused write is terminated in the access-control block itself, so it never
reaches the shadow storage and the response carries no error. `LC_STATE` is
written in bytes [31:8], which OR-merge as ordinary shadow bytes and leave the
lifecycle nibble alone.

*Anti-vacuity*: each field takes the same address and the same payload in both
conditions within one run, so a write path that never worked would fail the
strap-low half, and a lock that never engaged would fail the strap-high half.
The strap-low half additionally asserts that the value moved, so a payload that
sets no new bit fails as a test bug rather than passing as a refusal. The
`SECURE_TM_UNLOCK` contrast separates the lock from a bus that stopped
accepting writes when the strap went up.

*Not claimed*: the JTAG and TRoot writers of the same registers — this entry
drives the frontdoor only. Nor is this a walk of every `SECURE_TM_LOCK` entry
in the compiled field map: the map also marks `RMA_CHIPLET_TOKEN_DIGEST` and
`RMA_SIP_TOKEN_DIGEST`, each carrying a source note that the lifecycle chapter
does not list them. The five apertures here are the set the chapter names, and
which list is normative is an open question for the design owner.

'''''

[[sep_lcc_demote_feat_ctrl_matrix_test]]
===== `sep_lcc_demote_feat_ctrl_matrix_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: eFuse, lifecycle, SEC_DIS, locked-field

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), real fuse sense

*Source Module*: `sep_lcc_demote_feat_ctrl_matrix_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Objective*: Owns the demote product, including the demote lock.

*Test Coverage*: Demote matrix vs the feature-control golden, including debug-disable bits.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Walk TEST_DEV and PROD at DIS=0 through the four demote cells.
. W1S `sep_fuse_dbg` / `smc_fuse_dbg` and check PROD with both demotes set, so those bits close while Case 2 and Case 3 stay open.
. W1S a seed-pinned DIS pair and walk the four demote cells at PROD.
. Every cell's inbound probe is OKAY iff `sep_debug=1`, else DECERR.
. Every cell's `dbg_disable` vector matches the DTP gating ladder for that same `FEAT_CTRL`.
. Every cell's `sep_fuse_dft_disable` / `smc_fuse_dft_disable` ports match the compose of that same `FEAT_CTRL`.
. Lock one seed-selected `DEMOTE` at 0; a later demote write is ignored.
. `rst_ni` releases the lock; the same demote write then lands.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-FEAT-CTRL
|`FEAT_CTRL` equals the spec-derived golden for every walked cell.
|CHK-LIVE-GATE
|External access of the LC shadow is OKAY when `sep_debug=1` and DECERR when `sep_debug=0`. Not CSR storage alone.
|CHK-RAND-REP
|Thirteen demote cells ran in one invocation (eight DIS=0, one fuse-dbg compose, four pinned DIS).
|CHK-DEMOTE-LOCK
|With `lock` set and `demote` still 0, a later demote write is ignored and `feat_ctrl` stays at the undemoted golden.
|CHK-DEMOTE-LOCK-RESET
|After `rst_ni` the lock is 0 and the same demote write lands.
|CHK-DBG-DISABLE
|All eleven `dbg_disable` bits match the DTP gating ladder for the same `FEAT_CTRL` the cell just verified. `dft_secure` is Case 3 and `dfd` is Case 1.
|CHK-FUSE-DFT-DIS
|`sep_fuse_dft_disable` equals Case 3 AND `sep_fuse_dbg`, inverted. `smc_fuse_dft_disable` equals Case 2 AND `smc_fuse_dbg`, inverted. The compose cell keeps Case 2 and Case 3 open while bits 2 and 3 are closed, so a golden that ignores those bits fails there.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

'''''

[[sep_efuse_rma_token_rand_test]]
===== `sep_efuse_rma_token_rand_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: eFuse, lifecycle, SEC_DIS, locked-field

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), real fuse sense

*Source Module*: `sep_efuse_rma_token_rand_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Objective*: Standalone RMA token matrix plus the token-comparator redundancy fault path.

*Test Coverage*: RMA token compare, match, mismatch, and lifecycle update.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Start in PROD; LC shadow and `FEAT_CTRL` match the independent golden.
. Wrong SIP then CHIPLET token: no match code, program `PROGRAM_ERR`, LC unchanged after resense.
. Legal SIP then CHIPLET token: match code, LC advances, shadow and `FEAT_CTRL` match the independent golden after resense.
. Common-mode invert of all three RMA_SIP comparators on a valid token: legal mismatch code, no sticky bit, IRQ low.
. Common-mode invert of all three RMA_SIP comparators on a wrong token: legal match code, no sticky bit, IRQ low (fail-open hole).
. Collapse instance 0, then two-instance disagree: match status `6'b111111`, SIP sticky bit, `sep_internal_interrupts[39]`. Valid retry plus write-0 leave the bit and IRQ asserted.
. A cold-reset re-sense clears `TOKEN_MATCH_FAULT` and releases the interrupt.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-LC-FEAT
|LC shadow and LCC `FEAT_CTRL` match independent goldens after each resense.
|CHK-MISMATCH
|A wrong token does not update LC.
|CHK-MATCH
|A legal token updates LC (PROD → `RMA_SIP_1` → `RMA_CHIP_1`).
|CHK-RANDCFG
|SIP and CHIPLET both walked match and mismatch; tokens from the seed.
|CHK-COMMON-MODE
|All three comparators flipping a match to a legal mismatch does not set a sticky bit or raise `sep_internal_interrupts[39]`.
|CHK-COMMON-MODE-MATCH
|All three comparators flipping a mismatch to a legal match does not set a sticky bit or raise `sep_internal_interrupts[39]`.
|CHK-COLLAPSE
|Inject `3'b001` forces `6'b111111` and the SIP sticky bit. That encoding also makes instance 0 disagree with the others, so this check does not isolate the collapse detector.
|CHK-DISAGREE
|Two instances that disagree force `6'b111111` and keep the sticky bit.
|CHK-ERROR-CODE
|A redundancy fault writes `6'b111111` on every match-status bit.
|CHK-STICKY
|A valid-token retry and a write-0 leave `TOKEN_MATCH_FAULT` and irq39 asserted (`sw=r` until reset).
|CHK-IRQ-39
|`sep_internal_interrupts[39]` (PIC source 40) asserts with the sticky bit.
|CHK-WHICH-TOKEN
|Collapse on CHIPLET and SEC_DISABLE sets bits 8 and 16 (OR with SIP bit 0).
|CHK-JTAG-FAULT
|After `RMA_CHIP_1`, a JTAG read of `TOKEN_MATCH_FAULT` matches the AXI read. PROD/RMA_SIP JTAG token-block allow is `sep_efuse_jtag_axil_el2_cpu_mux_test`, not this check.
|CHK-FAULT-RESET
|A cold-reset re-sense clears `TOKEN_MATCH_FAULT` and releases `sep_internal_interrupts[39]`. The earlier sticky checks cannot tell a resettable latch from one that never clears. After the two zeros the detector is re-armed once, so the phase ends on a zero-to-one transition.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

'''''

[[sep_efuse_token_match_fault_pic_test]]
===== `sep_efuse_token_match_fault_pic_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: Mailbox, IRQ, PIC

*Run mode*: `cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`

*Source Module*: `sep_efuse_token_match_fault_pic_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, CPU FW

*Objective*: Closes the PIC story the no_cpu RMA vehicle cannot prove: EL2 claims source 40, reads the SEC_DISABLE sticky bit, and masks `meie[40]` so the level-high line does not re-enter.

*Test Coverage*: Token-match fault to PIC source 40; mask quiets the request.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Firmware arms PIC source 40, publishes READY on the cold-scratch register, then presents a SEC_DISABLE token so a compare is in flight.
. Host injects a differential-pair collapse on the SEC_DISABLE comparator once READY is observed.
. The service routine records the claim id and the fault register, then masks `meie[40]`.
. Firmware holds a quiet window and requires the source to still be requesting throughout it.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-PIC-CLAIM
|ISR `meihap` claim id is 40.
|CHK-PIC-FAULT
|`TOKEN_MATCH_FAULT` secure-disable bit is set.
|CHK-PIC-MASK
|After `meie[40]` is masked the service routine does not re-enter, AND the source is still asserting its request throughout the quiet window. `TOKEN_MATCH_FAULT` is software-read-only and clears only on reset, so the line cannot drop by itself; without the still-requesting leg a quiet window would also be satisfied by a request that went away on its own, or by a sticky latch that never set. The check is additionally gated on an interrupt having been delivered at all, so it cannot report a mask result on a run where nothing arrived.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Not claimed*: the other two comparators. This entry is the CPU/PIC half only —
the three-comparator breadth (`RMA_SIP`, `RMA_CHIPLET`, `SEC_DISABLE`), the
collapse / disagree / common-mode inject matrix, the software-read-only
behaviour and the JTAG read are graded on
<<sep_efuse_rma_token_rand_test>>, and are not re-credited here.

'''''

[[sep_fabric_output_remap_datapath_proxy_test]]
===== `sep_fabric_output_remap_datapath_proxy_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: Fabric and remap

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), `+skip_fuse_sense`

*Source Module*: `sep_fabric_output_remap_datapath_proxy_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Randomization*: RANDCFG — the seed selects one bank, one remap region and one filter entry; the checkers and the addresses they compare follow from that choice. Coverage is one region of the bank per run, so the entry runs at a single seed and the card claims one cell, not a sweep.

*Objective*: Live remap plus outbound-filter drop.

*Test Coverage*: Live outbound / AP remap datapath, not CSR storage alone.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Program one region to `0x8000_0000` and allow-list the remapped beat. CPU-LSU read of the window is OKAY.
. A second region, left at offset 0, returns DECERR.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-REMAP-TRANSLATE
|The programmed window completes OKAY at the remapped target. Identity would miss the allow window.
|CHK-FILTER-DROP
|A beat outside the remapped allow set is DECERR.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

'''''

[[sep_esrc_ht_watermark_arming_test]]
===== `sep_esrc_ht_watermark_arming_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: Crypto and entropy

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`, `+esrc_noise_force`

*Source Module*: `sep_esrc_ht_watermark_arming_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, entropy golden

*Randomization*: RANDCFG — all five `HT_WATERMARK_NUM` encodings walk every
seed; the illegal selector and the low mode allowed to fall come from the run
seed.

*Objective*: Health-test watermark arming: each supported selector arms to `0x0000` high or `0xFFFF` low on `MODULE_ENABLE` 0->1, a selector write leaves `HT_WATERMARK` at the entry value, and an illegal selector resolves to `REPCNT_HI`. Scope: the ESRC → DRBG → EDN datapath row owns CHK1–CHK4 and stays where it is.

*Test Coverage*: Health-test watermark arming and the matching status.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Every supported selector arms on MODULE_ENABLE 0→1.
. Changing the selector does not re-arm. An unsupported value becomes `REPCNT_HI`.
. A low watermark falls once `HEALTH_TEST_CTRL.ENABLE` is allowed.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-ARM-MEN
|Each supported selector arms to `0x0000` (high) or `0xFFFF` (low) on MODULE_ENABLE 0→1.
|CHK-NO-REARM
|A selector write leaves `HT_WATERMARK` at the entry value.
|CHK-UNSUPPORTED
|An illegal selector resolves to `REPCNT_HI` and arms `0x0000`.
|CHK-LOW-FALL
|After arming a low mode, enabling health tests drops `HT_WATERMARK` below
`0xFFFF`. Both `APT_LO` and `MARKOV_LO` take this path on every seed: they drive
separate event counters in `entropy_source.sv`, so choosing one per seed would
leave the other permanently unproven.
|CHK-RANDCFG
|The number of selectors whose DUT readback matched its documented arm value
equals the size of `enum WATERMARK_TEST`, read from `entropy_source.rdl` at
import. The bound comes from the RDL rather than from the walk generator, so a
skipped mode fails here. The same invocation covers the seed-selected illegal
selector and both low-mode falls.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Not claimed*: the shared TRNG reset path. It clears the selector and the
watermark together; no vehicle asserts that pair today.

'''''

[[sep_reg_bit_bash_rand_test]]
===== `sep_reg_bit_bash_rand_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: Fabric and remap

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`

*Source Module*: `sep_reg_bit_bash_rand_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Randomization*: RANDCFG — three seeds (`reseed = 3`). The seed varies the block order, the complement-vs-ones order and every touch value, never which registers are swept: the cell set is identical on every seed, so coverage is not seed-dependent. Reseeding is what exposes a register that accepts one mask-legal value and refuses another. The reset walk is the inventory after reasoned skips. Full-mask write-lands is 19 registers; 32 inbound START/END use the wrap model.

*Objective*: `env/sep_reg_meta.py` exports `addr`, `reset`, `mask` (software-usable field bits) and `mask_all` (storage, reserved included) from the generated SystemRDL.

*Test Coverage*: Seeded CSR bit-bash across the LSU-reachable register map.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Not a UVM RAL model.
. `env/sep_reg_meta.py` exports `addr`, `reset`, `mask` (software-usable field bits) and `mask_all` (storage, reserved included) from the generated SystemRDL. `iter_register_walk` counts OFFSET symbols that lack DEFAULT/struct (`nometa`) instead of dropping them silently.
. `SepRegBitBashCfg` is the single source of truth for both the walk and the skip reasons. ENTROPY_SOURCE registers that hardware advances while the noise source runs are excluded from the reset walk by `sep_reg_meta.reg_hw_updating()`: every register whose fields all declare `sw = r` in the generated JSON model, minus the constants named in `_CONSTANT_RO`. That set is 21 registers, of which 13 are tallied under `hw-owned; read value is not the POR value` -- the other 8 are claimed first by the `STATUS` and `RDATA` name suffixes. `sw = r` alone is not the criterion: COMPONENT_ID is software-read-only but hardwired (`entropy_source.rdl` `hw = na`, no storage in `entropy_source_reg.sv`), so it is opted back in and stays in the walk. The dead-space store compare uses the same accessor, so the two checks cannot drift apart.
. Write-lands is the anti-vacuity control: a complement write must move exactly the software-usable mask.
. Inbound-filter START/END stay in the write sweep under the full export mask; readback is checked against the `allow_burst=0` 8-byte granule (`hw/common/axi/axi_filter/doc/index.adoc`, `filter_ctrl.rdl`) with the peer at reset (`END_ADDR` reset `0x7`): START rounds down and END rounds up when they share a beat. The 4 KB granule is `CHK-PAGE-WIDEN` on `sep_fabric_inbound_filter_rule_matrix_test`.
. The inventory is seed-stable: export 1024, inventory 865, nometa 159, reset 481 after 384 reasoned skips -- the `reset_skip=[...]` tally in the walk line accounts for all 384. Write-lands is 19 full-mask registers (8 scratch-cold + 8 scratch-warm + 3 `SEP_CPU_CTRL`) plus 32 inbound START/END under the wrap model. Storage touch is every admitted side-effect-free register (130 of 865). `TOUCH_BLOCKS` is the anti-vacuity list (AES/HMAC/KMAC/OTBN/DMA/SPI/WDT and both mailboxes); the sweep fails if any of those nine IPs stops offering a candidate.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-RESET
|Every reset-eligible register reads its exported reset value.
|CHK-WRITE-LANDS
|A complement write moves exactly the software-usable mask bits: 18 registers over the full mask, plus 32 inbound START/END over ``mask[31:3]`` whose ``[2:0]`` are pinned by the same-beat wrap model instead.
|CHK-RO
|Bits outside the mask stay unchanged across the write bash. Counted only where the register has out-of-mask bits: 48 of the 50 have an all-ones mask, so the compare cannot fail there and is not claimed. Falsifiable on 2 (`PKA_CTRL`, `SEP_NMI_VEC`).
|CHK-RESERVED
|Reserved bits (`mask_all` minus `mask`) read back 0. Counted only where the register has a non-zero reserved field: 1 of the 50 (`SEP_NMI_VEC`, reserved `0x1`).
|CHK-BLOCK-TOUCH
|Every side-effect-free export RW storage register — 130 of the 865-register inventory, not one per IP: write a seed-derived ``x``, assert ``(readback & mask) == (x & mask)``, restore reset. Shadowed names take a dual write. The gate is the per-register safety predicate AND the side-effect predicate; the second is required, because the first admits registers whose write drops live beats. `TOUCH_BLOCKS` is the anti-vacuity list, so the sweep fails if any of those nine IPs stops offering a candidate. Excluded registers each carry a tallied reason and the sweep accounts for all 865. Not storage: hardware-owned status and state-machine observability words, the CSRNG reseed counters, an identity register, two read ports, `ENTROPY_SOURCE.FIPS_LOCK` (a sticky lock is never a storage touch), and two selectors that refuse a random mask-legal value — `HT_WATERMARK_NUM`, whose unsupported encodings sanitize, and `NOISE_OBS_CTRL`, whose mask contains a write-only singlepulse field.
|CHK-RANDCFG
|The whole seed-built configuration ran in one invocation: the reset, write and storage-touch walks each covered every register the cfg named. `TOUCH_BLOCKS` is the anti-vacuity list, not the third walk. A completeness control over the sweep itself, not evidence about the DUT -- it fails when a walk silently drops work, not when a register misbehaves. The walk sizes (`export`, `inventory`, `nometa`) are logged with it so a shrunken inventory is visible rather than inferred from a PASS.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

'''''

[[sep_axi_order_sweep_test]]
===== `sep_axi_order_sweep_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: Fabric and remap

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`

*Source Module*: `sep_axi_order_sweep_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Randomization*: RANDCFG — every (register x ordering) cell runs on every seed, and every (ordering x size) cell with it. The seed varies the staged data, the channel separation and the response backpressure, never which cell runs.

*Objective*: AXI write address and write data are independent channels with no ordering requirement between them (IHI 0022 A3.3), so AW-first, W-first and same-cycle are all legal stimulus and each must leave the written value in the addressed register without displacing its neighbour. The ordering is driven by holding the trailing channel until the leading one asserts VALID, and the ordering each cell actually presented is read off the bus and asserted against what was requested. Every block behind the crossbar terminates AXI at its own register adapter, so the contract has to hold at each adapter rather than at one scratch word. The sweep configures all three orderings at every write-safe register and crosses them with the 1/2/4-byte sizes on the scratch words, where a narrow write has no side effect. The narrow cells compare the whole software-usable word against the AXI lane rule; `sep_axi_strobe_window_test` owns the same rule at default timing.

*Test Coverage*: LSU-splice AXI burst order and response sweep.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Take the register inventory and the exclusion reasons from `sep_reg_bit_bash_seq`: `write_reason() is None` for the no-side-effect blocks, plus `touch_reason() is None` restricted to `TOUCH_BLOCKS` for plain RW storage inside the other major IPs. A second list would let two sweeps disagree about which registers are safe to write.
. Drop the blocks where the access itself would decide the result. `WDT_TIMER` is the one today: aon_timer runs on a clock 1000 times slower than the system clock, so the AXI timeout spans about ten of its edges and whether a round trip fits depends on the phase the access arrives on. A probe there measures CDC latency against the timeout rather than channel delivery. `sep_reg_bit_bash_rand_test` covers that block's storage.
. Prime the register with the complement under its write mask at default timing, and read it back, so a write that never lands reads as the prime rather than looking plausible.
. Apply the cell's timing profile through the VIP master (`AxiTimingProfile`, `ocah_axi_master_config`) to hold AW or W back, write the staged value, and read it back at default timing so the compare tests the write and not the read.
. Read a second register in the same block, write it back with one software-usable bit flipped at default timing, read it again, then re-read the swept register. Deriving the value from the current contents keeps a write that never arrived from reading back correct.
. A shadowed register commits on the second identical write. The profile arms a one-shot release, so it shapes the first write only and the committing second write goes out same-cycle. The ordering under test is the one the register sees first; the commit itself is not an ordered access.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-ORDER-LAND
|The written value is in the register after the ordering write. An adapter that latches a pending-write flag on AW accept and reads that flag in the same cycle the W arrives answers OKAY while dropping the data, so the compare is on the readback, never on the response. Every cell must produce a data compare; a cell that could not run is named with its reason, and the count then fails the test rather than reading as a full sweep.
|CHK-ORDER-DISPLACE
|The next write in the block lands on the register it addresses, and the swept register does not change when its neighbour is written. A pending flag left set by the ordering commits the following W handshake to the wrong target, which CHK-ORDER-LAND cannot see: it reads the register that was written, not the neighbour that was corrupted. The follow-on value is derived from what the neighbour currently holds and flips one software-usable bit, so a write that never arrived cannot read back correct. A refused follow-on write or readback fails this checker rather than being recorded as a skip. A cell in a single-register block offers no neighbour to write, so the required witness count is the number of cells that have one.
|CHK-ORDER-XSIZE
|All nine (ordering x size) cells produced a data compare, so the write lands at every transfer size and not only at the bus width.
|CHK-ORDER-STIM
|Each cell presented the ordering it requested, read off the AW/W valid assertions rather than taken from the configured profile. Asserted per cell. The slave's handshake is logged separately and is allowed to coincide: a slave that holds WREADY until AW answers a W-first presentation same-cycle, which is its own contract, not a stimulus failure.
|CHK-COVERAGE
|The cell count is asserted against a floor and against the cells the config presented, then the per-ordering tally, the blocks reached, the (ordering x size) grid and the witness count are logged. Every skipped register is counted under its reason on the configuration line the test logs first. A silent skip would let a shrinking sweep keep reporting PASS.
|CHK-RANDCFG
|The cell count for the seed is logged against the configured total, so a shrunken sweep is visible.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

'''''

[[sep_axi_order_sweep_m_axi_test]]
===== `sep_axi_order_sweep_m_axi_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: Fabric and remap

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`

*Source Module*: `sep_axi_order_sweep_m_axi_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Randomization*: RANDCFG -- every (register x ordering) cell runs on every seed. The seed varies the staged data, the channel separation and the response backpressure, never which cell runs.

*Objective*: The same ordering contract as `sep_axi_order_sweep_test`, driven from the SMN inbound master instead of the CPU-LSU splice. Both masters reach the same eleven IP block adapters, so an adapter that mishandles a channel ordering has to be caught from either. This path also grants a W-first handshake: the CPU-LSU register adapters hold WREADY until AW, so a W-first presentation is accepted same-cycle there, while the inbound path accepts W before AW.

*Test Coverage*: SMN-inbound AXI burst order and response sweep.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Assert the cell floor when the walk completes, and require the compare count to equal the cells the config presented, so a walk shrunk by a map or routing change fails rather than reporting a clean pass over fewer cells.
. Program the inbound filter from the CPU-LSU side: the filter denies by default, so five allow windows are opened over the swept extent. The window bounds follow `traffic_filter.sv`, which compares `addr[AW-1:3]` inclusively on an 8-byte granule.
. Walk the cells exactly as the CPU-LSU sweep does.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-ORDER-LAND
|The written value is in the register after the ordering write, compared on the readback rather than on the response, so an adapter that answers OKAY while dropping the data fails. Every cell must produce a data compare; a cell that could not run is named with its reason and the count then fails the test.
|CHK-ORDER-DISPLACE
