|The next write in the block lands on the register it addresses and the swept register does not change when its neighbour is written, so a pending flag left set by the ordering cannot commit the following W handshake to the wrong target. The follow-on value is derived from what the neighbour currently holds with one software-usable bit flipped. The required witness count is the number of cells whose block offers a neighbour.
|CHK-ORDER-XSIZE
|All nine (ordering x size) cells produced a data compare, so the write lands at every transfer size and not only at the bus width.
|CHK-ORDER-STIM
|Each cell presented the ordering it requested, read off the AW/W valid assertions rather than taken from the configured profile. On this path a W-first presentation is accepted before AW, which is the inbound adapter's own contract and is logged rather than treated as a stimulus failure — the opposite of the CPU-LSU register adapters, which hold WREADY until AW and answer W-first same-cycle.
|CHK-COVERAGE
|The cell count is asserted against a floor and against the cells the config presented, then the per-ordering tally, the blocks reached, the (ordering x size) grid and the witness count are logged. Every skipped register is counted under its reason on the configuration line the test logs first, including the sixteen `INBOUND_FILTER_CTRL` blocks this path cannot sweep. A silent skip would let a shrinking sweep keep reporting PASS.
|CHK-RANDCFG
|The cell count for the seed is logged against the configured total, so a shrunken sweep is visible.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Scope*: the sixteen `INBOUND_FILTER_CTRL` blocks are not swept here and are counted in the skip tally. Those CSRs are the filter the access is passing through, so writing one mid-walk would reprogram the path under the test and measure filter behaviour rather than adapter ordering. They are swept in full on `sep_axi_order_sweep_test`, whose bus has no inbound filter. No allow window covers the filter CSR bank, so the ownership hole `sep_fabric_inbound_filter_rule_matrix_test` asserts stays shut.

[[sep_axi_strobe_window_test]]
===== `sep_axi_strobe_window_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: Fabric and remap

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`

*Source Module*: `sep_axi_strobe_window_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Randomization*: RANDCFG — the size and lane axes are walked exhaustively rather than seeded, so every AxSIZE at every legal lane offset runs on every seed and no run can issue zero narrow writes. The seed varies the data.

*Objective*: A write narrower than the bus beat must change only the byte lanes its strobe selects (IHI 0022 A3.4.3), and a memory window must return the data that was written to it. A stage that widens the strobe, or writes the whole beat, changes bytes the master never asked for; only a data compare sees it, because the response is OKAY either way.

*Test Coverage*: Write-strobe windows on the LSU splice.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Prime a scratch word with a known value.
. Issue a write narrower than the bus beat at each legal lane offset for each AxSIZE, and read the whole word back.
. Compare against the AXI lane rule: the addressed lanes take the new data, every other lane keeps the primed value.
. Write and read back the OTBN IMEM and DMEM windows.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-STROBE
|Each narrow write changed only its own byte lanes, at every AxSIZE and every legal offset. The line reports the count, so a run that issued no narrow write cannot read as a pass. The compare is the addressed 32-bit word: the generated SEP C headers contain no 4-byte-adjacent register pair, so a 64-bit beat has no neighbour CSR in bytes 4–7 and a widened strobe has no second register to corrupt.
|CHK-WINDOW-DATA
|Each memory window returned the value written to it. A window behind an SRAM-style TileLink adapter applies the mask on reads, unlike a register behind a register adapter which ignores it, so a bridge driving that read mask from the write strobe returns zeros with a correct response. A window not writable in this configuration is named with its reason. At least one window must have been verified: a walk where none was writable proves nothing and fails.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

'''''

[[sep_drbg_axil_adapter_port_arbitration_test]]
===== `sep_drbg_axil_adapter_port_arbitration_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: Fabric and remap

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`

*Source Module*: `sep_drbg_axil_adapter_port_arbitration_test`

*BFM / Model*: TB-instantiated `drbg_axil64_lane_adapter` (`u_tbadp_vehicle` in `tb/tb_top.sv`), driven pin-level from cocotb

*Randomization*: none. The three orderings are fixed and all three run on every seed; the contract is a cycle alignment, not a data pattern.

*Objective*: AW, W and AR are independent AXI channels (IHI 0022 A3.3), and a master may not deassert a VALID before its handshake completes (A3.2.1). `drbg_axil64_lane_adapter` computes, in `StIdle`, `aw_ready` and `w_ready` gated on `!ar_valid` and `ar_ready` gated on `!aw_valid && !w_valid`. A presentation that puts AR alongside a write therefore leaves every ready low with no channel able to go first and neither side able to withdraw, so the port is held until reset. This leaf presents all three legal orderings at the port and requires both accesses to retire under each.

*Test Coverage*: All three legal AW/W/AR orderings at the TB-owned adapter port.

*Scope*: the MODULE's arbitration, not the SEP integration. The DUT's own lane adapters sit behind the crossbar, which delivers W one cycle after AW and re-serializes every write to that order whatever a master presents -- `CHK-CONCURRENT-CAL` on the two fabric-driven leaves measures the gap at both ends and reports it as fabric-introduced. A same-cycle AW/W/AR presentation and a W-before-AW presentation are therefore not producible at a DUT adapter port from the fabric side, which is why this vehicle exists. It is a second instance of the same RTL module with its own reset and no fabric in front of it: every legal ordering is presentable to the cycle, and a wedged cell is cleared without resetting the DUT, so the three verdicts are independent. It claims nothing about the integration; the two leaves that follow own that half.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Read the live CSRNG lane and require OKAY before the vehicle is touched (`CHK-PORT-ANCHOR`).
. On the vehicle, retire a lone write, a lone read, and an AW-then-W gap with no AR, each answering OKAY (`CHK-PORT-CONTROL`).
. Present each of the three legal AW/W/AR orderings at the vehicle port. Confirm the port presented the named ordering (`CHK-PORT-STIM`) and that both accesses retire in bound (`CHK-PORT-PROGRESS`). Reset the vehicle between cells.

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,4"]
|===
|Checker |Contract

|CHK-PORT-ANCHOR
|A plain read of the live CSRNG lane answers OKAY before the vehicle is touched. The vehicle is a TB-owned instance, so without this the leaf could report a pass against a DUT that never left reset; it also puts a transaction through the AXI agent, which the scoreboard requires as positive evidence before it will allow a pass at all.

|CHK-PORT-CONTROL
|Three legs, all required to retire AND to answer OKAY, before any overlap cell runs. A lone write and a lone read show the vehicle and the AXI-Lite-32 responder behind the adapter are alive at all -- without them a broken driver would report every ordering as stalled and imitate the defect exactly. A third leg drives AW and then W four cycles later with no AR, the same separation the `aw-then-ar` and `w-then-ar` cells use, so the separation itself is excluded as the cause of their stall by observation rather than by reading the design under test. The response code carries the other half: an access the adapter rejects as unsupported is answered SLVERR straight out of `StIdle` with nothing forwarded and retires just as promptly, so OKAY is what shows the forwarding leg is alive, and OKAY at the 64-bit port cannot be produced without the downstream handshake.

|CHK-PORT-STIM
|The ordering the port actually presented, read off `tbadp_chan_o` rather than taken from the offsets requested. A cell whose presentation does not match is a stimulus failure and is reported as one, never scored as coverage -- a cell that did not overlap says nothing about the arbitration in either direction.

|CHK-PORT-PROGRESS
|Both accesses retire: BVALID and RVALID are both seen within a bounded cycle count. The stall this bounds is a stable, legal `1'b0` on all three readys, which no X-check and no protocol assertion can see, so the check also records the longest run of held-valids-with-every-ready-low, which is the stall signature itself.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Parked on an RTL fix*: `CHK-PORT-PROGRESS` fails on all three orderings while `CHK-PORT-CONTROL` passes. The lone write retires with `aw`/`w` handshaking on the first cycle and BVALID at cycle 3 carrying OKAY; the lone read retires with RVALID at cycle 3, also OKAY; the gapped write handshakes AW at cycle 0 and W at cycle 4 and retires with BVALID at cycle 7, OKAY; all three show zero stalled cycles. The response code is part of the control, not decoration: an access the adapter rejects as unsupported is answered SLVERR straight out of `StIdle` with nothing forwarded, and retires just as promptly, so a control that watched only the valid would accept a dead forwarding leg. The cycle count matters for the same reason -- a forwarded write cannot answer before the downstream leg has taken it. The three overlap cells then hold the port with every ready low for the whole bound -- the same-cycle cell with no handshake at all, the AW-first cell after AW alone has handshaked, and the W-first cell after W alone has handshaked. Those last two are the partially-committed states the arbitration analysis predicts. Reported to the open issue that raised concurrent AW/W/AR arbitration on this adapter. Recorded as a real failure and deliberately not converted to an expected failure.

*Not claimed*: the SEP integration. That a fabric master can drive this adapter into the stall is proven separately, and only for the one ordering the fabric can present.

'''''

[[sep_drbg_axil_concurrent_csrng_aw_then_ar_test]]
===== `sep_drbg_axil_concurrent_csrng_aw_then_ar_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: Fabric and remap

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`

*Source Module*: `sep_drbg_axil_concurrent_csrng_aw_then_ar_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Randomization*: RANDCFG -- the seed varies the staged register value only. The channel placement is measured per build, not drawn, so a seed replays the cell without pinning it to a latency the fabric may have changed.

*Objective*: The same arbitration contract as <<sep_drbg_axil_adapter_port_arbitration_test>>, but on the DUT's own CSRNG lane adapter and reached the way software reaches it. The two lane adapters sit on the CSRNG and EDN control buses (`drbg.sv` `u_csrng_axil_adapter` / `u_edn_axil_adapter`, fed from `sep_crypto.sv` `csrng_axil` / `edn_axil`), which is the path every SEP-level CSRNG and EDN register access already takes, so the stall is reachable by ordinary CSR traffic rather than by a corner the fabric cannot present. The cell writes the block's `INTR_ENABLE` and reads its `INTR_STATE` concurrently, so both accesses are behind the same adapter.

*Test Coverage*: One AW-then-AR overlap at the DUT CSRNG lane adapter.

*Scope*: one lane, one ordering, one leaf. A stalled adapter cannot be recovered inside a simulation -- the port stays held and the master stays stuck on it -- so a second cell after a wedge would measure the wedge rather than itself.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Measure the CSRNG `INTR_ENABLE` writable width, then a lone write and a lone read, and place the AW-then-AR overlap from the measured crossbar latencies (`CHK-CONCURRENT-CAL`).
. Present that overlap on `INTR_ENABLE` / `INTR_STATE`. Confirm the CSRNG adapter port (`drbg_csrng_axil_chan_o`) saw the named ordering (`CHK-CONCURRENT-STIM`).
. Require both accesses to retire and the write to land (`CHK-CONCURRENT-LAND`).

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,4"]
|===
|Checker |Contract

|CHK-CONCURRENT-CAL
|The register's writable width is measured, not assumed: all-ones is written and read back, and the seed's pattern is narrowed to the bits that stick. The two blocks implement different interrupt counts, so a hardcoded width is wrong for one of them and a non-existent bit reads back 0 -- indistinguishable from a dropped write. A lone write and a lone read, neither of which can deadlock, then measure when AW, W and AR arrive at the adapter port through the crossbar. The overlap is then placed from those measured latencies: on broken RTL the first overlap is terminal, so the placement cannot be retried and must not be guessed, and a placement the measured latency cannot reach is reported unreachable rather than fired blind. The same measurement records the AW-to-W gap at the master and at the port and states which side introduced it -- master 0 cycles, port 1 cycle, so the stagger is the fabric's. That is also the evidence that plain untimed traffic already puts AW and AR in the same cycle whenever a store and a load are in flight together.

|CHK-CONCURRENT-STIM
|The ordering the ADAPTER PORT presented, read off `drbg_csrng_axil_chan_o`, not the ordering requested and not the presentation at `s_axi`. The crossbar restaggers the channels, so a presentation at the TB master is not evidence of one at the arbitration point. The leaf is covered only when that presentation is the named ordering. A different overlap, or a cell that missed its overlap, is reported unreachable and does not count as this leaf.

|CHK-CONCURRENT-LAND
|Both accesses retire and the write lands. The stall is a stable legal `1'b0` on all three readys, so the check is a bounded timeout plus a data compare against a complement prime; the prime makes a dropped write read back as the prime rather than as a plausible value. The check also records the longest run of held-valids-with-every-ready-low.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Parked on an RTL fix*: `CHK-CONCURRENT-LAND` fails. With AW presented and AR arriving while W is still outstanding, the CSRNG lane adapter port holds its valids for the whole bound with `aw_ready`, `w_ready` and `ar_ready` all low and no channel ever handshaking. The stall is permanent: nothing in the adapter can clear it, since neither side may withdraw a VALID and no ready ever rises. That a following access to the same lane also fails to retire was observed while developing the vehicle and is not re-asserted by this leaf, whose cell ends at the wedge. Reported to the open issue that raised this. Recorded as a real failure and deliberately not converted to an expected failure.

*Not claimed*: the other two orderings in that analysis. A true same-cycle AW+W+AR presentation and a W-before-AW presentation are not reachable at a DUT adapter port from a SEP master, for the reason `CHK-CONCURRENT-CAL` measures. They are graded on <<sep_drbg_axil_adapter_port_arbitration_test>> instead, at the module's port. `CHK-CONCURRENT-CAL` logs the measured arrivals, so a fabric change that closes the stagger would make them placeable here and visible.

'''''

[[sep_drbg_axil_concurrent_edn_aw_then_ar_test]]
===== `sep_drbg_axil_concurrent_edn_aw_then_ar_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: Fabric and remap

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`

*Source Module*: `sep_drbg_axil_concurrent_edn_aw_then_ar_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Randomization*: RANDCFG -- as <<sep_drbg_axil_concurrent_csrng_aw_then_ar_test>>.

*Objective*: The same contract on the second lane adapter, the EDN control path. `drbg.sv` instantiates `drbg_axil64_lane_adapter` twice, so the two lanes are separate hardware and a defect in one instance is not evidence about the other. The checks are those of <<sep_drbg_axil_concurrent_csrng_aw_then_ar_test>>, with the probe on `drbg_edn_axil_chan_o` and the access pair on the EDN block's own registers.

*Test Coverage*: One AW-then-AR overlap at the DUT EDN lane adapter.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Measure the EDN `INTR_ENABLE` writable width, then a lone write and a lone read, and place the AW-then-AR overlap from the measured crossbar latencies (`CHK-CONCURRENT-CAL`).
. Present that overlap on the EDN block's own registers. Confirm the EDN adapter port (`drbg_edn_axil_chan_o`) saw the named ordering (`CHK-CONCURRENT-STIM`).
. Require both accesses to retire and the write to land (`CHK-CONCURRENT-LAND`).

*Checkers*:

[cols="1,4"]
|===
|Checker |Contract

|CHK-CONCURRENT-CAL
|Same contract as <<sep_drbg_axil_concurrent_csrng_aw_then_ar_test>>, on the EDN interrupt registers. The writable width is measured on this block; the lone write and lone read measure arrival at `drbg_edn_axil_chan_o`.

|CHK-CONCURRENT-STIM
|The ordering the EDN adapter port presented, read off `drbg_edn_axil_chan_o`, not the ordering requested and not the presentation at `s_axi`.

|CHK-CONCURRENT-LAND
|Both accesses retire and the write lands. The stall signature is the same held-valids-with-every-ready-low bound as the CSRNG leaf.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Parked on an RTL fix*: `CHK-CONCURRENT-LAND` fails identically -- the EDN lane adapter port holds its valids to the bound with all three readys low. That both instances fail the same way, and that the TB-instantiated third instance does too, is what shows the defect is in the module rather than in one integration.


[[sep_axi_partial_lane_read_test]]
===== `sep_axi_partial_lane_read_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: Fabric and remap

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`

*Source Module*: `sep_axi_partial_lane_read_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Testlist*: `system.toml` (`reseed = 3`), also in `all.toml` / `cpu_stub`

*Randomization*: RANDCFG — `SepAxiPartialLaneReadCfg` is the single source of truth for stimulus and goldens. Required cells walk every seed: all 36 contiguous (offset, length) byte-run slices of a 64-bit word (`8+7+…+1`), all 14 aligned narrow-ARSIZE cells (AxSIZE 0/1/2 at every legal lane offset: `8+4+2`), and the all-zeros / all-ones CSR patterns. The seed varies walk order, SRAM address, the 64-bit word, and extra CSR patterns only.

*Objective*: A read narrower than the 64-bit CPU-LSU beat only obligates the responder to drive the addressed byte lanes: a 32-bit CSR endpoint leaves RDATA[63:32] undriven, and a narrow-ARSIZE beat leaves every non-addressed lane undriven. The VIP master samples the full beat and extracts the addressed bytes, so the value a test receives must be a pure function of the addressed lanes — exact against an independent golden and identical on re-read — regardless of what the undriven lanes carry. `sep_axi_strobe_window_test` owns the write-side lane rule; here the write is trusted and the read extraction is on trial. The contract is pinned through the public VIP read path alone, with no cocotb or cocotbext-axi global state involved.

*Test Coverage*: Partial-lane reads on the LSU splice.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Log the seed, word, addresses and cell counts, and hold the walked counts against the 8-byte-beat floors (36 slices, 14 ARSIZE cells) plus the two required CSR extremes.
. Write a pattern to the 32-bit `SW_DEBUG` CSR, read it back twice as 4-byte reads with a checked read of the non-zero RO `SEP_LOCAL_BASE_ADDR` register between them; repeat for the all-zeros, all-ones and seed-random patterns.
. Prime a seed-random 64-bit SRAM word and its neighbour with the complement value.
. Read every contiguous (offset, length) byte run of the primed word, re-reading each slice after a checked full-width read of the complement neighbour.
. Read the primed word at every aligned narrow-ARSIZE cell (AxSIZE 0/1/2 at each legal lane offset), re-reading each cell after the same complement-neighbour disturb.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-RANDCFG
|Walked slice and ARSIZE counts equal the combinatorial floors (`36`, `14`), and the CSR walk starts with all-zeros then all-ones. Seed, word, addresses and counts are logged, so a shrunken walk fails rather than reporting PASS over fewer cells.
|CHK-CSR-LANE
|Each pattern reads back exactly and identically on the repeat, through a beat whose upper four lanes the CSR endpoint never drives. All-zeros always runs next to all-ones and random patterns on the same register, so a dead write path or a zero-filled read cannot pass the set.
|CHK-SRAM-SLICE
|Every byte-run slice equals the Python slice of the primed word, and re-reads it exactly after the disturbing read. An extraction that starts at the wrong byte, takes the wrong length, or depends on leftover channel payloads fails the compare; the disturbing read is itself compared against the complement word, so the disturbance is proven to have happened.
|CHK-ARSIZE
|Every aligned AxSIZE-0/1/2 cell returns exactly the addressed lanes of the primed word, and re-reads them identically after the complement-neighbour disturb, on beats where the responder legitimately drives nothing else.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Not claimed*: write-side strobe masking (`sep_axi_strobe_window_test`); AxSIZE=3 (that is the slice walk's full-width baseline).

'''''

[[sep_axi_map_refuse_test]]
===== `sep_axi_map_refuse_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: Fabric and remap

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`

*Source Module*: `sep_axi_map_refuse_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Randomization*: RANDCFG — every directed anchor that falls outside all crossbar routes runs on every seed, plus seed-selected addresses from the reserved rows. Anchors and rows the crossbar routes are skipped with a counted reason, and a row that could not yield its full quota is reported in the per-row shortfall line.

*Objective*: Rows marked `_RSV_` in `hw/sys/sep/doc/memory_map.adoc` allocate nothing, so an access there must not answer OKAY. The refusal expectation is taken from the map, never from the RTL — asking the decoder what the decoder should do would pass on any decoder. `env/sep_axi_decode_map.py` parses the crossbar `AddrMap` for two distinct purposes: it reports every span the fabric routes that the map calls reserved, and it filters those spans out of the probe set. The filter is deliberate. Whether the fabric must refuse a routed-but-reserved span is an open specification question, and the scoreboard has no outcome-unknown mode, so driving one would assert the open question by the back door. The asserted contract therefore covers the reserved addresses no decode rule reaches; the rest are enumerated and logged. Scope is the gaps BETWEEN block windows; `sep_fabric_deadspace_decode_test` walks the dead tail INSIDE one, and neither subsumes the other.

*Test Coverage*: Unmapped / refuse addresses complete with the specified error response.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Parse the allocation table in `hw/sys/sep/doc/memory_map.adoc`; a row marked `_RSV_`, and any address in no row at all, allocates nothing.
. Parse the crossbar `AddrMap` and report every span the fabric routes that the map calls reserved. Two rules state their bounds as `och_sep_top_addrmap_pkg` symbols rather than hex literals and are resolved against the generated package, so no rule falls out of the cross-check silently.
. Read and write the surviving anchors and the seed-selected reserved addresses, and report the per-row shortfall where a row yielded fewer probes than its quota.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-MAP-REFUSE
|Every probed address is refused. The map does not mandate which flavour, so DECERR and SLVERR both satisfy it and the line reports the split. The probe set is the map-reserved addresses that no crossbar decode rule covers; addresses the crossbar routes are excluded before the walk with a counted reason and are not asserted on. Spans the crossbar routes that the map calls reserved are enumerated statically and logged as MAP-AUDIT, so the disagreement between the map and the RTL is visible without either side being asserted.
|CHK-NONVAC
|The DECERR beats the monitor observed on the bus equal the count the master reported, so the refusals are evidence from the pins and not only from the driver.
|CHK-OKAY
|A known-mapped CSR on the same bus returns OKAY and its generated reset value, read before any refusal probe. Without it a wedged or dead bus would refuse everything and read as a clean pass. The address and the expected value come from the generated SystemRDL export, and the register's reset is non-zero so the compare cannot pass against a tied-off decode.
|CHK-RANDCFG
|The probe and anchor counts for the seed are logged, so a shrunken walk is visible.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

'''''

[[sep_fabric_deadspace_decode_test]]
===== `sep_fabric_deadspace_decode_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: Fabric and remap

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`

*Source Module*: `sep_fabric_deadspace_decode_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Randomization*: RANDCFG — the twelve directed wrap offsets on every seed, plus two seed-selected dead offsets per block window.

*Objective*: Negative control on intra-block dead space. Each block owns an address window and populates only part of it; an access past the allocated size owns no register and must be refused. Window ends come from `hw/sys/sep/doc/memory_map.adoc`, which states the rule: within an aperture only the unit's register extent responds and the remainder returns DECERR. Landing on a live register instead is a hard failure. A later beat of a burst that ends past the window's extent must be refused on the same terms as a single beat.

*Test Coverage*: Dead-space decode refuses with zero data.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Each block owns an address window and populates only part of it.
. An access past the allocated size owns no register and must be refused.
. Window ends come from `hw/sys/sep/doc/memory_map.adoc`, which states the rule: within an aperture only the unit's register extent responds, the remainder returns DECERR, and an access there never reaches the unit.
. A refusal that still writes the register is a failure, so every probe reads back the window's live registers as well as the response.
. A later beat of a burst that ends past the extent is held to the same rule as a single beat.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-WINDOW-LIVE
|Each of the ten watched windows (secure_dma, wdt_timer, aes, otbn, csrng, edn, entropy_src, km_mailbox, lifecycle, spi_controller) has a non-empty allocated-register snapshot, so the no-alias compare can fail. The line reports readable and armed separately: the store compare drops hardware-updating addresses, so armed is the falsifiable count.
|CHK-DEADSPACE-REFUSE
|Every probed address past the allocated size is refused (not OKAY), including the directed wrap anchors (KM `0x10920ff8`, DMA `0x108008a8`). A single accepted probe fails the checker; there is no tolerated count.
|CHK-DEADSPACE-NO-ALIAS
|No allocated register in that window changes across a probe, the directed wrap anchors included, and a read of a dead offset never returns a live register's value. The alias compare is not gated on the response: `memory_map.adoc` says an access to the reserved remainder never reaches the unit, so a refused read that still hands back live data has reached it. A single aliased probe fails the checker.
|CHK-DEADSPACE-BURST
|A burst that ends past the window's allocated extent and answers OKAY, where the same address is refused as a single beat, fails. A beat inside the extent is also compared against the value the single-beat path reads. A burst that times out fails the audit rather than passing. A window whose dead space is 4KB-aligned, or that has no live words before it, carries no legal burst into it: it is named as not auditable rather than counted as a pass, and a run where no window was auditable fails.
|CHK-DEADSPACE-BEAT
|Each beat of the burst carries its own `RRESP`, kept in order by the bus monitor, so the refusal can be attributed to a beat rather than to the burst. No beat past a block extent may answer OKAY. The rule is asserted in that direction only: refusing the whole burst is legal AXI and stricter than the specification, so a window that refuses every beat is recorded, not failed. A second assertion requires at least one window that serves its in-extent beats and refuses only the tail — without one, the checker shows that nothing past an extent is served but not that the fabric can tell the beats apart. A captured vector whose length does not match the beats issued is treated as no evidence, since unrelated traffic on the bus would lengthen it. Read bursts only; see <<known-limitations>> for why writes cannot be.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

'''''

[[sep_efuse_set_only_monotonicity_test]]
===== `sep_efuse_set_only_monotonicity_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: eFuse, lifecycle, SEC_DIS, locked-field

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`; omit `+skip_fuse_sense` so the shadow bank is live

*Source Module*: `sep_efuse_set_only_monotonicity_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Objective*: Proves the eFuse shadow-register write path OR-merges new data with the stored value, so a bit that is already set cannot be cleared by a write of zero, while a genuinely writable field still overwrites. Randomization is RANDCFG — every legal set-only field is walked every seed, word index and bit patterns come from the run seed, and the OR-merge contract itself is directed.

*Test Coverage*: eFuse set-only fields never clear a burned bit.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Scope is the shadow-register write path that OR-merges new data with the stored value.
. The walked fields are metal `WRITE_SET_ONLY` in `sep_efuse_pkg::EfuseFieldMap`: `BL1_VERSION`, `BL2_VERSION`, `CHIPLET_PUBK_REVOKE`, `REQUIRED_SIGNERS`, `REQUIRED_ALGS`.
. That is the field map, not the `hw/sys/sep/doc/periphs.adoc` SW-writable column (`REQUIRED_SIGNERS` is `true` there; firmware may update the shadow per manifest).
. A write of zero cannot clear a set bit.
. Program × lock is a different mechanism; never program `LC_STATE`.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-SENSE
|A known 1 is sensed into each legal set-only word.
|CHK-CLEAR-REJECT
|A frontdoor write of 0 leaves that bit 1.
|CHK-SET-STICKS
|A write that sets a previously-0 bit sticks; readback is the OR.
|CHK-OR-MERGE
|A later write of 0 cannot clear the OR.
|CHK-WRITABLE-CONTRAST
|A WRITE_UNLOCK spare still overwrites, so the OR is field-local.
|CHK-RANDCFG
|Seven set-only fields walked in one invocation, including `SIP_DIS` and `SYS_DIS` -- the Class-1 members, whose shadow OR-merge takes the `class1_shadow_storage_idx` arm of `efuse_shadow_regs.sv` that the non-Class-1 fields never reach. Both are pinned to their function-group word so the walk does not OR `sep_debug`, `chiplet_dbg` or `sip_debug` into `FEAT_CTRL`. This test drives the CPU-LSU master, which has no inbound filter.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

'''''

[[sep_esrc_fips_lock_test]]
===== `sep_esrc_fips_lock_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: Crypto and entropy

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`

*Source Module*: `sep_esrc_fips_lock_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, entropy golden

*Randomization*: RANDCFG — every certified-configuration class every seed; pre-lock value and rejected poke come from the run seed.

*Objective*: The FIPS lock makes ESRC configuration write-once: a pre-lock write moves the field off reset, the same field rejects a poke after `LOCK=1`, write-0 cannot clear the lock, and `rst_ni` is the only way back, with the BIW observe enable still writable under the lock. The advisory RCT/APT cutoffs derived from `MIN_ENTROPY_H` are graded against an SP 800-90B oracle. Scope: health-test *quality* stays Phase 3.

*Test Coverage*: FIPS lock and the matching entropy-source status.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. One of two vehicles on the ESRC lock-and-alert scorecard row.
. Health-test *quality* stays Phase 3.
. `FIPS_LOCK.LOCK` is write-1-only.
. Observe FIFOs stay writable under lock.
. Walks every field `entropy_source.sv` marks `swwel = fips_lock`, one target
per register, except `CTRL.MODULE_ENABLE`.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-PRE-LOCK
|A pre-lock write moves the field off reset.
|CHK-POST-LOCK
|The same field rejects a poke after `LOCK=1`.
|CHK-LOCK-SET
|`FIPS_LOCK.LOCK` reads 1 after the arming write.
|CHK-LOCK-W1S
|Write-0 leaves `FIPS_LOCK.LOCK=1`.
|CHK-CTRL-RSVD
|Writing reserved `CTRL[0]` before the lock leaves `CTRL` unchanged, where the
other `CTRL` fields are demonstrably writable so an unchanged register isolates
bit 0. Repeated after the lock, the same write leaves `FIPS_LOCK.LOCK=1`.
|CHK-RST-NI
|`rst_ni` clears `FIPS_LOCK.LOCK` to 0.
|CHK-POST-UNLOCK
|After `rst_ni` the released target reads its register reset, then takes the
poke that the lock rejected. The target is chosen so its poke differs from the
reset, otherwise the reset value alone would satisfy the readback.
|CHK-OBS-ENABLE-WRITABLE
|`BIW_OBS_CTRL.RAW_ENABLE` stays writable under lock. This is the control bit
only. `BIW_OBS_STATUS` and `BIW_OBS_RDATA` are not read, so the observe FIFO
itself is not claimed live.
|CHK-REC-THRESH
|`RECOMMENDED_THRESHOLDS.RCT_LIMIT` tracks `MIN_ENTROPY_H` across a sweep of
eight Q4.4 points, compared against SP 800-90B 4.4.1 `C = 1 + ceil(20/H)`
re-derived in the test. The oracle is the standard, not
`entropy_source_rec_thresh_lut.sv`, which the checker never reads. `APT_LIMIT` is
an exact binomial cutoff with no closed form; what is claimed for it is that it
stays inside the 1024-sample window and falls as the assessed min-entropy rises.
|CHK-RANDCFG
|26 locked registers walked in one invocation, covering 34 of the 35 fields
`entropy_source.sv` marks `swwel = fips_lock`. Every register that locks two
fields moves both off reset in the pre-lock write, so a field inside the compare
mask is never left unwritten.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Not claimed*: `CTRL.MODULE_ENABLE`. It is locked too, but clearing it stops the
block for the rest of the walk, so its lock belongs to the reset-recovery
vehicle.

'''''

[[sep_esrc_alert_delivery_test]]
===== `sep_esrc_alert_delivery_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: Crypto and entropy

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`, `+esrc_noise_force`

*Source Module*: `sep_esrc_alert_delivery_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, entropy golden

*Randomization*: RAND-NONE — directed persistent-failure trip.

*Objective*: A short window plus stuck forced noise trips `ALERT_THRESHOLD`,
and the alert bookkeeping the RDL defines is read at that trip. An opening
`INTR_TEST` sweep, before the trip, drives every interrupt source the RDL defines.

*Test Coverage*: Entropy-source alerts reach status and the aggregator.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Second vehicle on the same scorecard row.
. From the clean pre-trip state, pulse each `INTR_TEST` source on its own.
. A short window plus stuck forced noise trips `ALERT_THRESHOLD`.
. Read the alert counters and the health-test latch at the trip.
. Health-test *quality* stays Phase 3.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-NO-ALERT
|ALERT, PERSISTENT_FAILURE, and PIC source 16 are 0 before the trip.
|CHK-INTR-TEST
|Each `INTR_TEST` source `ENTROPY_SOURCE.fields("INTR_TEST")` names sets only
its own `INTR_STATUS` bit, raises PIC source 16, and clears on W1C. Swept
before the trip: `INTR_STATUS` bits are re-driven from their live cause, so a
source whose real condition is latched cannot clear. The expected count is
that field list, not a literal.
|CHK-ALERT
|`MAIN_SM_STATUS.ALERT` asserts after the threshold.
|CHK-PERSISTENT-FAILURE
|`INTR_STATUS.PERSISTENT_FAILURE` asserts. Note it is the OR of the AlertHang
trip, a main-FSM error and a counter-integrity error; CHK-SM-ERR-ZERO is what
makes this one attributable.
|CHK-PIC-16
|`sep_internal_interrupts[15]` (PIC source 16) asserts. Only PERSISTENT_FAILURE
is enabled for the trip, so this adds the aggregate slot wiring on top of
CHK-PERSISTENT-FAILURE rather than an independent cause.
|CHK-SM-ERR-ZERO
|`MAIN_SM_STATUS.ERR` stays 0 across the trip, so PERSISTENT_FAILURE is
attributable to the alert threshold.
|CHK-ANY-FAIL-WINDOWS
|`ALERT_SUMMARY_FAIL_COUNTS.ANY_FAIL_COUNT` sits inside `[ALERT_THRESHOLD,
ALERT_THRESHOLD + 8]` at the trip and stays at or below the summed per-lane fail
pulses. `ALERT_THRESHOLD` is 3: at 1 the alert comparator already implies the
lower bound, and no denomination is distinguishable at a count of one. The upper
bound against the per-lane pulse total is what rejects a counter denominated in
events rather than in failing *windows*.
|CHK-FAIL-ATTRIB
|`REPCNT_TOTAL_FAILS` is non-zero, attributing the trip to the repetition test
under stuck noise with `REPETITION_LIMIT` = 5, and each lane's 32-bit total
covers its 4-bit `ALERT_FAIL_COUNTS` counter. The two take the same fail pulse
but carry different widths and different clears, so the compare is a
DUT-to-DUT contract. The reverse does not hold and is not claimed:
`alert_cntrs_clr` also fires on a passing window.
|CHK-HT-STATUS
|`HEALTH_TEST_STATUS.HEALTH_STATUS` latches a failing test while the alert is
asserted, and is W1C. No reserved bit latches: `entropy_source.rdl` allocates
repetition [0], APT [3], Markov high [4] and Markov low [5], leaving [1:2] and
[6:7] reserved.
|CHK-GEN-HEALTH
|All twelve `GENERATOR_n_HEALTH_STATUS` registers are read at the trip. At least
one latches a failing lane, and none latches a reserved bit. These twelve
registers had no DV coverage before.
|CHK-W1C
|After leaving AlertHang, W1C clears both bits and the PIC line.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Not claimed*: `es_cntr_err` -> `MAIN_SM_STATUS.ERR`. This is the health-test
counter-redundancy countermeasure: `prim_count` holds each count twice, up and
down, and reports an error only when `cnt_q[0] + cnt_q[1]` stops equalling the
counter maximum. No stimulus can break that invariant — both copies take the same
enable and the same step — so the path is reachable by a physical fault and by
nothing else. Proving it therefore requires fault injection, which is Phase 3 —
see the holding note in <<phase-3>>.
CHK-SM-ERR-ZERO proves the bit is 0 on the alert path, which is what makes
PERSISTENT_FAILURE attributable to the threshold trip; it does not claim the
error path works.

'''''

[[sep_sec_dis_override_test]]
===== `sep_sec_dis_override_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: eFuse, lifecycle, SEC_DIS, locked-field

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`; omit `+skip_fuse_sense`

*Source Module*: `sep_sec_dis_override_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Randomization*: RAND-NONE — directed match / mismatch / restore.

*Objective*: A match is reachable because `tb_top` binds `SEP_SEC_DISABLE_TOKEN` to SHA-256 of the all-zero token. A match before sense-done must not release the sense-gated reset (`hw/ip/efuse/rtl/efuse_interface_controller.sv`, issue 1462).

*Test Coverage*: Security-disable match, mismatch, restore, and sense-gated reset hold.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. A match is reachable because `tb_top` binds `SEP_SEC_DISABLE_TOKEN` to SHA-256 of the all-zero token.
. The comparator hashes the written `SEC_DISABLE_TOKEN_I` and compares it to the metal expected digest reconstructed from `SEP_SEC_DISABLE_TOKEN` (`hw/ip/efuse/rtl/efuse_token_processing.sv`, `hw/sys/sep/doc/token_processing.adoc`).
. After `release_no_cpu_reset`, write the all-zero token before `sep_fuse_sense_done_o`. Match must assert, and `sep_cpu_reset_n_o` must stay 0 while sense is still open (`ext_boot_seq_done_i` is already 1, so sense is the only held term).
. Wait for sense-done. Token `1` mismatches.
. On a later match, `sec_dis` asserts and `FEAT_CTRL` follows feat_ctrl_expected(..., sec_dis=1).

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-DIGEST-BIND
|Independent SHA-256 of the all-zero token equals the TB-bound digest.
|CHK-SENSE-GATED-RESET
|A match with `sep_fuse_sense_done_o=0` and `ext_boot_seq_done_i=1` leaves `sep_cpu_reset_n_o` and `dbg_sep_reset_n_o` at 0. `reset_n` carries no SEC_DIS term; the release is a separate DTP `sep_reset_n` TDR step.
|CHK-MISMATCH
|Written token=1 returns `SEC_DISABLE_TOKEN_MATCH=0x2a`.
|CHK-NO-OVERRIDE
|After the mismatch, `sec_dis` stays 0; `FEAT_CTRL` equals the fail-closed PROD golden.
|CHK-MATCH
|Written all-zero token returns `SEC_DISABLE_TOKEN_MATCH=0x15`.
|CHK-OVERRIDE
|After the match, sec_dis=1 and `FEAT_CTRL` equals the override golden (all ones).
|CHK-DEACTIVATE
|A later token=1 returns mismatch again.
|CHK-RESTORE
|After that mismatch, `sec_dis=0` and `FEAT_CTRL` is fail-closed PROD again.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

'''''

[[sep_fabric_local_alias_datapath_test]]
===== `sep_fabric_local_alias_datapath_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: Fabric and remap

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`

*Source Module*: `sep_fabric_local_alias_datapath_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Randomization*: RANDCFG — region and source page from the run seed; the dest is `CLOCK_GATE_CTRL` so the offset is determined and non-vacuous.

*Objective*: Scope is the sixteen programmable local-master regions ahead of the system-peripherals routing demux (`hw/sys/sep/doc/fabric.adoc`, `axi_alias_remap`).

*Test Coverage*: Local-alias remap datapath, not the CSR bank alone.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Distinct from the CPU high-alias window (`sep_cpu_ifu_lsu_alias_remap_matrix_test`).
. Scope is the sixteen programmable local-master regions ahead of the system-peripherals routing demux (`hw/sys/sep/doc/fabric.adoc`, `axi_alias_remap`).
. CSR programming stays on `sep_fabric_remap_filter_csr_bank_test`.
. A programmed filter-bank page beat returns the marker written to `CLOCK_GATE_CTRL`.
. Local-master `REGION_ATTRS` has `valid` and `cacheable`.
. Cacheable overrides AXI `cache` on a hit; this vehicle does not observe that field.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-OFFSET
|A programmed region's live beat equals input plus the programmed offset (data matches identity `CLOCK_GATE_CTRL`).
|CHK-VALID-GATE
|`region_valid` gates the remap. The same window is reprogrammed with bounds and offset live and only the enable clear, and the beat must then read the value at the address the master issued rather than the remapped destination. Programming the window first is what separates a working gate from an address that was never in range, and the vacuity guard on `CHK-OFFSET` has already shown the two values differ. The cacheable attribute is not checked: `axi_alias_remap` drives the outbound cache bits from the region on a hit and this bench has no observable on them.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

'''''

[[sep_km_key_policy_vault_test]]
===== `sep_km_key_policy_vault_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: Key Manager vault

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`, `+km_rom_hex=km_rom_vault.parhex`

*Source Module*: `sep_km_key_policy_vault_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, KM path / golden

*Randomization*: RANDCFG — slot, SRAM lock region and the seal/free slot pair from the run seed; extent endpoints 0 and 63 and the lock/W1C sequence are directed.

*Objective*: The key/policy vault refuses what it should: a store to a locked SRAM region is dropped with the readback unchanged, the matching `SRAM_WRITE_LOCK_VIOLATION` bit sets, `IRQ_STATUS` raises AXI_SLVERR or AXI_DECERR, and the status bit clears W1C. The seal walk is here too: the same erase must retire a sealed slot and free an unsealed one.

*Test Coverage*: Vault lock, seal, retire, erase, and SRAM write-lock on the KM CPU bus.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Dedicated `km_rom_vault` on the KM CPU bus, not `rom_main`.
. The seal walk lives here rather than on the command set because no mailbox command seals a slot: over that surface an erase always frees, so the retire path has no vehicle there.
. PicoRV32 is RV32E (`x0`–`x15` only).
. A legal slot in 0..63 takes `CTRL.lock_write`; a store past `KM_KPV_SIZE` (`0x13108`, still inside the 8 KB KPV window) raises AXI SLVERR or DECERR; a write to a write-locked SRAM region (never region 0 or 15) is dropped and raises `SRAM_WRITE_LOCK_VIOLATION` plus `IRQ_STATUS.SRAM_WRITE_LOCK_ERR`, then both W1C-clear to 0.
. [cols="1,2,2,1",options="header"]

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-SLOT
|`CTRL.lock_write` sticks on slots 0, the seed slot, and 63.
|CHK-LOCKWR
|A key-data write to a write-locked slot raises AXI SLVERR. This is the LOCK path, not the decode path CHK-EXTENT grades: a legal key-entry address on a locked slot. The sticky error bits are cleared and confirmed clean first, so an earlier fault cannot satisfy it.
|CHK-LOCKUSE
|A key-data read of a `lock_use` slot raises SLVERR and returns zero data. Both halves are required: the refusal alone would not show the data path is also forced.
|CHK-EXTENT
|Store to `0x13108` sets `IRQ_STATUS` AXI_SLVERR or AXI_DECERR.
|CHK-DROP
|Write to the locked SRAM region is dropped (readback unchanged).
|CHK-VIOL
|`SRAM_WRITE_LOCK_VIOLATION` reads EXACTLY the locked region's bit. Compared whole rather than masked, so a decoder that set every region's bit fails.
|CHK-IRQ
|`IRQ_STATUS.SRAM_WRITE_LOCK_ERR` is set and no ROM/SRAM parity or ROM-write error is raised alongside it.
|CHK-W1C
|Violation and IRQ bits read back 0 after W1C.
|CHK-SEAL
|One `CTRL` write on the seed's seal slot sets `seal` and raises `lock_write` with it.
|CHK-RETIRE
|Erasing that sealed slot leaves it retired: `lock_write` held and `lock_use` gained.
|CHK-RETSTICK
|A SECOND erase of the retired slot leaves it retired. Retirement is stated to hold until warm reset, so it is graded as a sticky property rather than a one-shot transition.
|CHK-FREE
|An unsealed slot is marked with `lock_write`, and the erase clears `CTRL` entirely, so the slot is reusable. The mark matters: the reset value is already clear, so without it a no-op erase would pass. The contrast is the point: the same erase, one sealed and one not.
|CHK-ERASEDATA
|The erase overwrites the stored key words, not just the CTRL bookkeeping. Two words are preloaded before the slot is marked; after the erase both differ from the preload AND from each other, so a fill that never ran or that is stuck at one value fails.
|CHK-IRQSET
|`IRQ_SET.DRBG_ERR_SET` raises `IRQ_STATUS.DRBG_ERR`, and W1C clears it. This grades the software-inject path and the status plumbing ONLY -- it is not coverage of the DRBG fault source, and must not be recorded as such.
|CHK-RANDCFG
|The ROM echoes an eight-bit fold of the WHOLE config word, so all four seeded operands are proven received. Echoing only the slot would let a ROM that ignored the region or either seal-walk slot produce an identical result word.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Aperture*: the vault is on the KM CPU bus, so the ROM evaluates these
contracts and the host reads one summary word from KM SRAM. That indirection is
real and is stated here rather than left implied: the host observes a result
word, not the individual registers. What keeps it from being a firmware
self-report is the seeded configuration -- the slot, region and slot pair are
chosen by the host, and the result word carries a fold of the whole config word
the ROM received, so a firmware that reported success unconditionally would
still have to have consumed every one of the host's operands.

'''''

==== Key Manager vault vehicles

[[sep_km_command_set_rand_test]]
===== `sep_km_command_set_rand_test`

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, real fuse-sense, `+esrc_noise_force`,
`+km_rom_hex=rom_main.rom.parhex`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: Key Manager vault

*Source Module*: `sep_km_command_set_rand_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, KM path / golden

*Objective*: the KM mailbox command set is this vehicle's surface — generate,
transfer, revoke-or-shred, and illegal-to-error. The sideload KATs prove only
that a consumer can consume a key; none of them exercises the command interface
that produces, moves and destroys one.

*Test Coverage*: Generate, transfer, revoke-or-shred, and illegal-to-error on the KM mailbox.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Stage a valid PROD OTP image and sense it for real: the KM firmware reads OTP
at boot. Park OTBN, KMAC and HMAC, leave AES released as the consume witness, so
a transferred key can only land where the test says it lands.
. Bring entropy up strict, then release the KM and wait for `RESP_KM_READY`.
. Generate a key for AES twice; check the handle and the echoed argument fields.
. Load a known key, transfer it to AES, and encrypt: the ciphertext is compared
against an independent AES-256-ECB golden.
. Transfer the same handle to an engine outside its permitted set.
. Shred the AES key, then load and transfer a second, distinct known key and
encrypt again.
. Revoke the generated handle, then transfer on it.
. Revoke the reserved null handle; walk seeded undefined command IDs; send a
defined command with the wrong payload length. Probe with `CMD_STAT` after each
refusal.

*Randomization*: RAND-REP — generate / transfer / revoke-or-shred /
illegal-to-error every seed. RANDCFG for the generated key size, the
out-of-policy destination, and the undefined command IDs, which always include
the four IDs immediately outside the defined runs.

*Scope*: sideload KATs stay consume proofs; this vehicle owns the mailbox command
set. The plan left open whether the mailbox PROTOCOL folds in here or splits
out. It splits out: see `sep_km_mailbox_protocol_rand_test`. The seal
walk is on `sep_km_key_policy_vault_test`, not here: no mailbox command seals a
slot, so over this surface an erase always frees.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK0
|`rom_main` boots on real entropy and announces `RESP_KM_READY`.
|CHK-GEN
|`CMD_KEY_GENERATE` returns success and a non-null handle, and its return argument echoes the requested size and destination mask.
|CHK-UNIQ
|A second generate returns a different handle.
|CHK-XFER
|`CMD_KEY_TRANSFER` of a known loaded key succeeds and the AES ciphertext equals the independent golden.
|CHK-DEST
|A transfer to an engine outside the key's permitted set is refused with the failure code, not an argument-validation code.
|CHK-SHRED
|`CMD_ENGINE_SHRED` succeeds with its destination echoed, and the engine re-keys: a second distinct key transferred afterwards encrypts to its own golden and no longer to the first key's.
|CHK-REVOKE
|`CMD_KEY_REVOKE` succeeds with the handle echoed.
|CHK-CLOSED
|A transfer on the revoked handle is refused with the failure code: revoke fails closed.
|CHK-NULL
|Revoking the reserved null handle is refused with the invalid-argument code.
|CHK-ILLEGAL
|Every seeded undefined command ID returns the invalid-command code.
|CHK-LEN
|A defined command carrying the wrong payload length returns the invalid-length code.
|CHK-FRAME
|The three framing rejections, each with the value it carries: a corrupt header CRC-8 returns RC_HEADER_CRC with the CRC the KM computed, a wrong sequence number returns RC_CMD_NOSEQ with the number it expected, and a corrupt payload CRC-32C returns RC_PAYLOAD_CRC. The sequence-counter side effects differ between the three and are handled in the driver.
|CHK-GONE
|A shredded engine refuses to start. After a final `CMD_ENGINE_SHRED` the AES produces no output within a bounded window, which is the only frontdoor evidence the shred reached the key: the engine key registers are write-only. Runs last, because an engine parked waiting for a key stays parked.
|CHK-ALIVE
|After every refusal `CMD_STAT` succeeds and reports no latched recoverable error. The second half is what gives CHK-DEST and CHK-CLOSED their meaning: while a recoverable fault is pending the KM refuses every key command with the same failure code those two expect, before it looks at the arguments at all, so a faulted KM would otherwise satisfy both without the policy they name being exercised.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Anti-vacuity*: every frame is built with a correct header CRC-8, a correct
declared length and a correct payload CRC-32C, so a refusal is a verdict on the
command ID or its arguments and never on framing. The refusal checkers assert
the exact documented code rather than "not success", so a rejection for the
wrong reason does not read as a pass. The two known keys are checked to encrypt
the plaintext differently before either is used, so CHK-SHRED's
before-and-after comparison cannot be satisfied by two identical goldens.

*Not claimed*: the DRBG fault SOURCE. The organic timeout and AXI-Stream error
conditions cannot be provoked from the SEP fabric, and at SEP level such a fault
is unrecoverable -- the KM emits an unsolicited fault response and halts -- so it
is neither a readable status nor a return code from here. The interrupt plumbing
for that same bit IS reachable and is graded on the vault vehicle's CHK-IRQSET,
via the architected `IRQ_SET.DRBG_ERR_SET` inject; the two must not be conflated,
and CHK-IRQSET is not coverage of the source.

The mailbox is a transport here, not the subject. Its register surface -- depth,
full, the four overflow/underflow stickies, `IRQ_STATUS`, and `SEP_CTRL`'s
response modes and flush -- is owned by `sep_km_mailbox_protocol_rand_test`.

'''''

[[sep_km_mailbox_protocol_rand_test]]
===== `sep_km_mailbox_protocol_rand_test`

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`, KM held in `SW_RESET_N` (reset default).
No `+km_rom_hex`: this leaf grades the SEP-side registers, not a command stream.

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: Key Manager vault

*Source Module*: `sep_km_mailbox_protocol_rand_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Objective*: the SEP-side KM mailbox as a REGISTER surface rather than a
transport. `STATUS` depth and full/empty, the two SEP-reachable sticky error
bits, `IRQ_STATUS` / `IRQ_ENABLE` gating onto aggregator `[14]`, both
`SEP_CTRL` response modes, and flush.

*Test Coverage*: SEP-side KM mailbox registers: depth, stickies, IRQ, response modes, flush.

*Why it is its own entry*: a transport only ever touches what a well-formed
exchange needs. `sep_km_command_set_rand_test` reaches 4 of the 7 SEP-side
registers and 2 of the 12 `STATUS` bits. Reaching the rest means reading an
empty FIFO and overrunning a full one, and both corrupt a frame in flight.

*Why KM is held*: the SEP-facing mailbox lives on cold reset, so the FIFOs and
SEP registers stay alive while the KM CPU cannot consume inbound words or push
outbound ones. Fill, overflow and underflow are then deterministic, and
firmware cannot flush or fault the mailbox under the host. A `SEP_CTRL` write
after a deliberate underflow is a register-block access, independent of the
FIFO error path, and must stay OKAY. The write is a 32-bit beat: a 64-bit
beat at `SEP_CTRL` (offset 0x18) spans past the 0x1C mailbox window and the
fabric refuses it. Negative FIFO accesses are marked `expect_error` so the
environment scoreboard does not fail them as real errors.

*Randomization*: RANDCFG. The 16 inbound fill words and the overflow poke come
from the run seed. Every discrete cell (idle, underflow SLVERR, underflow
OKAY-mode, fill, overflow SLVERR, overflow OKAY-mode, IRQ enable/gate/W1C,
flush) walks in one invocation.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Bring up no_cpu with fuse sense skipped. Require `SW_RESET_N` bit 0 clear.
. Compare reset `STATUS` and `IRQ_STATUS` against the empty golden.
. Read empty `SEP_READ_DATA`; require SLVERR, data 0, and `outbound_underflow`
  latched in both `STATUS` and `IRQ_STATUS`.
. Write `SEP_CTRL` immediately after that underflow; require OKAY and readback.
. Set `outbound_underflow_resp`; the same empty read must be OKAY with the
  stickies still set. Write-1-to-clear both copies.
. Write 16 seeded words; after each write, `STATUS` depth/full/empty and
  `IRQ_STATUS.inbound_write_space_avail` must match the golden.
. A 17th write must SLVERR, latch `inbound_overflow`, and leave depth 16.
. Set `inbound_overflow_resp`; the next overrun must be OKAY, drop the word,
  and leave the sticky set.
. Enable the two error IRQs; aggregator `[14]` must rise. Clear the enable;
  the pin must drop while `IRQ_STATUS` stays. Write-1-to-clear both stickies.
. Set flush. Both FIFOs empty, `flush` self-clears, and the overflow sticky
  survives until write-1-to-clear.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-HELD
|`SW_RESET_N` bit 0 reads 0. Without this the fill/overflow goldens are a race against firmware.
|CHK-IDLE
|Reset `STATUS` and `IRQ_STATUS` match the empty golden; aggregator `[14]` is 0.
|CHK-UFL
|Empty `SEP_READ_DATA` returns SLVERR and 0, and `outbound_underflow` latches in both `STATUS` and `IRQ_STATUS`.
|CHK-CTRL
|A 32-bit `SEP_CTRL` write immediately after that underflow is OKAY and reads back. The register is not the FIFO error path. A 64-bit beat at offset 0x18 spans past the 0x1C window and is refused.
|CHK-UFL-RESP
|`SEP_CTRL.outbound_underflow_resp` turns the same empty read into OKAY; the sticky bits stay set.
|CHK-UFL-W1C
|Write-1-to-clear drops both sticky copies.
|CHK-FILL
|16 seeded writes fill inbound. After each write, `STATUS` depth/full/empty and `IRQ_STATUS.inbound_write_space_avail` match the golden.
|CHK-OVR
|A 17th write returns SLVERR, latches `inbound_overflow`, and leaves depth 16.
|CHK-OVR-RESP
|`SEP_CTRL.inbound_overflow_resp` turns the next overrun into OKAY; the sticky bit stays; depth stays 16 (the word is dropped).
|CHK-IRQ
|Enabling the two error IRQs raises aggregator `[14]`. Clearing the enable drops the pin while `IRQ_STATUS` stays. Write-1-to-clear drops both.
|CHK-FLUSH
|`CTRL.FLUSH` empties both FIFOs, self-clears, and does not clear the sticky error bits.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Accepted scope deltas*: `inbound_underflow`, `outbound_overflow`, and
`flushed_by_km` need the KM CPU as the peer. `STATUS.inbound_separator`
updates only when the KM pops the inbound FIFO, and `STATUS.outbound_separator`
only when the SEP pops a KM-pushed word, so both stay on the KM IP mailbox
vehicles. `outbound_read_data_avail` stays 0 for the same reason.

'''''

[[sep_km_crc_pcpi_kat_test]]
===== `sep_km_crc_pcpi_kat_test`

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`, `+km_rom_hex=km_rom_crc.parhex`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: Key Manager vault

*Source Module*: `sep_km_crc_pcpi_kat_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, KM path / golden

*Objective*: each mode of the KM CRC engine produces the value an independently
written host-side model produces. The engine has no register interface, so the
proof has to come from KM firmware issuing the PCPI custom-0 instruction.

*Test Coverage*: KM CRC PCPI modes vs an independent host golden.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Release the KM on a dedicated service ROM. The ROM applies one CRC instruction
per request — mode, chaining state, data operand — and returns the raw result.
It decides nothing.
. Walk the seeded operand set through all three modes and compare each result
against the host-side golden.
. Send one word through the word instruction and the same four bytes through
four chained byte instructions.
. Send one state and byte through both polynomial families.
. Repeat each byte-mode operand with garbage in the upper three bytes.

*Randomization*: RANDCFG — the vector set, from the run seed.

*Aperture*: the CRC engine has no register interface at all. It is a PicoRV32
PCPI co-processor reached by a custom-0 instruction, so the only honest
activation is KM firmware, in the ROM pattern the vault test established. The
assembler has no mnemonic for the instruction, so the image emits encoded words;
the KM core is built without the upper register file, so the encodings target
two registers in the lower half and cannot name one the core does not have.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-WORD
|Every seeded CRC-32C word vector equals the independent golden.
|CHK-BYTE
|Every seeded CRC-32C byte vector equals the independent golden.
|CHK-ROHC
|Every seeded CRC-8/ROHC vector equals the independent golden.
|CHK-XCHAIN
|One word instruction equals four chained byte instructions from the same start state. Hardware on both sides, so it holds independently of the golden.
|CHK-POLY
|The two polynomial families return different results for the same state and byte, compared over the low byte so their differing result widths cannot satisfy it. The operand is screened for this: the two agree in the low byte for roughly one input in 256, which is correct hardware, so it is excluded from the draw rather than tolerated by a looser check.
|CHK-NARROW
|CRC-8/ROHC leaves the upper twenty-four result bits clear.
|CHK-UPPER
|Both byte modes ignore the operand's upper twenty-four bits.
|CHK-MOVE
|No result equals its own input state.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Anti-vacuity*: the golden is anchored to published check values for both
algorithms and to a byte-order self-test, all asserted at import, so a
transcription error fails before a run rather than agreeing with an equally
wrong DUT. The operand draw rejects an all-zero state with an all-zero byte,
which every reflected CRC maps to zero and which therefore passes against a dead
engine; words whose four bytes are not all distinct, which cannot separate
little-endian consumption from big-endian; and operands sitting on a fixed point
of their mode, which the engine correctly maps back onto their own input state.
A fixed point is legal hardware behaviour, not a defect -- in CRC-8/ROHC one
byte in 256 is a fixed point for any given state -- so it is screened out of the
draw with the independent golden rather than tolerated by CHK-MOVE, which would
otherwise be unfalsifiable for that vector. CHK-MOVE then fails a co-processor
that hands back its first operand, and CHK-XCHAIN and CHK-POLY need no golden at
all. All three modes walk in this vehicle; do not split them into siblings.

*Not claimed*: the illegal-encoding trap. An unrecognised instruction in this
family produces no handshake and traps the core, which would end the service
loop; the KM firmware's own regression owns that leg.

'''''

[[sep_km_kpv_scrambler_test]]
===== `sep_km_kpv_scrambler_test`

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`,
`+km_rom_hex=km_rom_kpv_scrambler.parhex`

*Phase*: 2

*Bucket*: Key Manager vault

*Source Module*: `sep_km_kpv_scrambler_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, KM path / golden

*Objective*: the key-policy vault scrambler transforms both the stored word and
the address mapping while enabled. Pass-through while disabled is the
observation aperture this test relies on rather than a contract it asserts --
it is what makes the raw stored words readable frontdoor, and a vault that did
not pass through would fail the round-trip and the count instead.

*Test Coverage*: Vault scrambler enable, key transform, and sticky lock.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Release the KM on a dedicated observation ROM and post two scrambler keys and
three logical key-entry indices.
. With the scrambler off, zero all 1024 key-entry words. That reaches a known
starting state through a legitimate frontdoor, without a wipe and without
relying on power-up values.
. Program the first key, enable the scrambler, write one plaintext at each of
the three logical indices, then disable and scan the whole file.
. Repeat with the second key at the first index only.
. Re-enable and read the first index back.
. Take the sticky lock last, then read the key register and the first index
again.

*Randomization*: RANDCFG — two scrambler keys and three logical indices, drawn
distinct, from the run seed.

*Aperture*: the vault is reachable only from the KM side, so this needs its own
ROM image, not `rom_main` — that image programs the key, enables the scrambler
and takes the sticky lock during boot, so the enable toggle is already gone
before the host can act. No hierarchical peek is needed: with the scrambler
disabled the vault addresses its register file with the logical index and passes
data through unmodified, so firmware can write plaintext with scrambling on,
disable it, and read the raw stored words back through a legitimate frontdoor.
The lock is taken last because it is sticky. The ROM reports raw observations
and the host holds every verdict.

*Precondition*: `km_kpv_regfile` has no reset, so power-up is undefined and a
whole-vault "exactly N non-zero" scan is not a contract until the file is
known-zero. The zero fill is *setup*, not a wipe security checker.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-COUNT
|After three writes through the enabled scrambler, exactly three of the 1024 words differ from the zeroed setup.
|CHK-DATA
|No stored word equals any plaintext written.
|CHK-ADDR
|The stored words do not all sit at their own logical index: the address mapping is tweaked, not identity.
|CHK-KEYED
|The same plaintext at the same logical index under a different key stores a different word, so the transform is keyed rather than a fixed obfuscation. The reported index is range-checked and the value required non-zero first, so a second-key pass whose write never landed fails here instead of being read as evidence of keying.
|CHK-RT
|With the scrambler re-enabled, the logical index reads back the exact plaintext.
|CHK-KEYRD
|Before the lock, the key register reads back the key that was written. This is the control for CHK-LOCK: without it a write-only or permanently-zero register would satisfy the zero-after-lock check for free.
|CHK-LOCK
|After the sticky lock the same register reads zero.
|CHK-LOCKRT
|The round-trip still recovers the plaintext after the lock, so the hardware kept the key it was using.
|CHK-SWWEL
|The lock's DOCUMENTED contract: after it, a write of a different key and a write clearing ENABLE are both refused -- the round-trip still returns the plaintext and ENABLE still reads set. CHK-LOCK grades behaviour the specification does not state; this grades the behaviour it does.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Anti-vacuity*: "exactly three" is the load-bearing quantifier, not "at least
one". A write that never landed gives zero differences and fails; a read path
stuck at zero gives zero and fails; a register file left dirty gives more than
three and fails. A bare "the stored word differs from the plaintext" check would
pass in the first two cases, and under a simulator that defaults uninitialised
memory to zero it would also pass on a file that was never written. Round-trip
is a required conjunct, so a scrambler that destroys data cannot pass by looking
scrambled. The plaintext is chosen with mixed nibbles because the substitution
is nibble-wise and a uniform value would weaken the difference signal.

CHK-ADDR is deliberately "not all of them" rather than "each of them". The
address transform is a bijection on the 1024 indices, and a bijection may have
fixed points, so a single index mapping to itself is legal; an identity mapping
across three seeded indices is not.

CHK-LOCK asserts RTL behaviour the architecture document does not state. The
document requires only that a locked key cannot be modified; the hardware
additionally drives the key register to zero once the lock commits, while the
scrambler keeps using a shadow copy. CHK-LOCKRT is what makes the pair
meaningful: the key is unreadable and still in use. Recorded here because a
checker that outruns its specification should say so rather than read as
contracted behaviour.

*Not claimed*: the eraser's use of the shared scrambler, the seal and retire
contracts owned by the vault test, and the wipe security contract.

'''''
