[[known-limitations]]
[[phase-3]]
=== Known Limitations

This table holds two different kinds of item, and the distinction matters when
reading a row: a *hardware* gap or spec-vs-RTL divergence, where the RTL or the
specification is expected to change; and a *firmware* constraint, where the
hardware is correct as built and software must program it correctly. A firmware
constraint is not an open defect.

[cols="2,4",options="header"]
|===
|Topic |Contract

|`drbg_axil64_lane_adapter` concurrent AW/W/AR arbitration (hardware)
|The adapter arbitrates its write channels against `ar_valid` and its read channel against `aw_valid`/`w_valid`. In `StIdle` it computes `aw_ready = !aw_pending_q && !ar_valid`, `w_ready = !w_pending_q && !ar_valid` and `ar_ready = !aw_pending_q && !w_pending_q && !aw_valid && !w_valid`, so a presentation that puts AR alongside a write leaves every ready low. No channel can go first, no pending bit is set, the state does not advance, and AXI forbids either side deasserting a VALID before its handshake completes (IHI 0022 A3.2.1), so the port is held until reset. Three vehicles reproduce it: the two DUT lane adapters over the SEP fabric (<<sep_drbg_axil_concurrent_csrng_aw_then_ar_test>>, <<sep_drbg_axil_concurrent_edn_aw_then_ar_test>>) and a TB-instantiated third instance driven at its port (<<sep_drbg_axil_adapter_port_arbitration_test>>), which reaches all three legal orderings and passes a lone-write / lone-read control first.

The stall is invisible to the checks the module already carries: the `ASSERT_KNOWN` checks at the bottom of the file confirm the ready signals are not X, and a stalled-but-stable `1'b0` satisfies that. It needs no exotic alignment either -- `CHK-CONCURRENT-CAL` measures plain untimed traffic arriving with AW and AR in the same cycle, so any store and load in flight together can reach it.

Two of the three orderings are not reachable at a DUT adapter port from a SEP
master, and the cause is the fabric rather than the stimulus. Two independent
lines of evidence agree.

Structurally, `axi_demux_simple.sv` gates W forwarding on the AW handshake --
`w_select_valid = w_cnt_up | (|w_open)` at line 312, consumed at line 497 --
so W can never be forwarded to a downstream port before its own AW has been
accepted. A W-before-AW presentation therefore cannot exist at the adapter
port.

The same-cycle AW+W gap is empirical, not a spill-parameter argument.
`sep_crypto_axi_interconnect` sets both `SpillAw` and `SpillW` to 1, so it
does not explain a one-cycle stagger. `CHK-CONCURRENT-CAL` measures master
gap 0 / port gap 1 on both DUT lanes. A write-only sweep of thirteen master
timing profiles (write-only, so no cell can deadlock) produced port AW-to-W
gaps of 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 7 and 9 cycles and never 0. `aw_delay=N` shifts AW
and W together and leaves the gap at 1, because the profile releases the
trailing write channel off the leading one rather than placing either
absolutely; `w_delay=N` only widens the gap. A master-side profile can
delay a channel, never advance one, so the minimum reachable gap is the
fabric's own.

Both orderings are graded on the port-driven vehicle instead, which is why that
vehicle exists rather than being a convenience. All three leaves are red and are deliberately not converted to expected failures. Reported to the open issue that raised the concurrent arbitration question; the RTL fix is owned there, not here.

|Token-comparator collapse inject (`3'b001`)
|`tb_top` mode `3'b001` forces instance 0 to a collapsed pair and leaves instances 1 and 2 as a legal match. The fault output is the OR of collapse and disagree, so `CHK-COLLAPSE` does not prove the collapse detector exists. Isolating that term needs all three pairs alike and `compute_comparison_vld_i=1`. SEC_DISABLE's valid is the sticky digest valid, not the RMA `digest_vld && fuse_sense_done` term, so an all-alike force with SEC valid still 0 is expected not to raise that rail. The inject stays at `3'b001` until a kept run isolates the term.

|Inbound CFG allow-list (firmware constraint)
|Hardware does not hard-block allow-listing the inbound-filter CFG window, outbound CFG, the remap banks, or `SEP_GLOBAL_BASE_ADDR` / `SEP_REGION_SIZE`, and is correct as built: the filter applies the rules it is given. Firmware must keep those CSRs outside every allow window — allow-listing them is a programming error, not a hardware defect, and nothing is expected to change in the RTL. The suite proves ownership under correct programming (`CHK-OWNERSHIP`).

|Routed-but-reserved spans (specification)
|`sep_axi_map_refuse_test` drives only reserved addresses the crossbar does not route. An address the crossbar routes but `memory_map.adoc` marks `_RSV_` is counted as a skip and reported by `MAP-AUDIT`, not asserted: whether the fabric must refuse a routed reserved span is an open specification question, and `expect_error` would settle it by the back door. A fabric that answers OKAY there therefore passes this vehicle.

|Unpopulated hole inside `sep_cpu_ctrl` (specification)
|`sep_cpu_ctrl.rdl` places `SEP_FUSE_SENSE_STATUS` at `0x150` (64-bit) and `SEP_SW_DEBUG` at `0x178`, and declares nothing between, so `0x10A3_0158`-`0x10A3_0177` is eight words no one assigned. It is a *hole*, not a declared reserved region: the RDL marks it neither `reserved` nor a register, and no document names a required response for it.

*Spec*: `hw/sys/sep/doc/memory_map.adoc` says that within an aperture "only the unit's register extent responds; the remainder is reserved and returns DECERR", and that such an address "cannot alias onto one" because reads and writes "never reach the unit". The aperture rows are per unit -- this block sits inside `0x10A0_0000`-`0x10A3_FFFF` (SYS) -- so whether that sentence reaches a gap *inside* one unit's own register file, as against the space between units, is the open question.

*RTL*: the generated block cannot produce DECERR for any address. `hw/sys/sep/regs/gen/sv/blocks/sep_cpu_ctrl_reg.sv` computes `is_valid_addr = '1; // No valid address check`, never uses it, and ties `decoded_err`, `cpuif_wr_err` and `readback_err` to `'0`; the readback mux defaults to `'0`. The request does reach the block, which acknowledges it -- so both halves of the spec sentence are unmet as built.

*DV*: `sep_address_map_test` `CHK-CPU-CTRL-HOLE` grades only what holds under either ruling -- the access retires, and it does not alias `SEP_SW_DEBUG` (with a control write first proving that register writable, since `SEP_FUSE_SENSE_STATUS` is `sw = r` and no AXI write can move it). The observed response is reported as `MAP-AUDIT` and is not asserted, for the same reason `sep_axi_map_refuse_test` reports rather than arming `expect_error`: asserting either answer settles the question by the back door. A `SLVERR`/`DECERR` assertion added in the branch that became this revision did exactly that and could not pass against this RTL.

Owner decision needed: if the sentence governs intra-block holes, the fix is a generated address check or explicit `reserved` declarations in the RDL; if it does not, `memory_map.adoc` should say so, since it currently reads as universal.

|Dead-space per-beat refusal on writes (AXI)
|Read bursts are proven per beat: the bus monitor keeps each beat's `RRESP` in order, so `CHK-DEADSPACE-BEAT` can name the beat that answered. Write bursts cannot be, and no tooling will change it — AXI returns one `BRESP` for a whole write burst, so a write burst that refused only its tail is indistinguishable from one that refused every beat. The write contract stays at burst granularity: the burst is refused and no live register moves.

|Mailbox 32-bit read of a 64-bit entry (specification, unproven)
|`fabric.adoc` says each 64-bit input word is mapped to two 32-bit words the SEP host can access. That is the consume path: a 32-bit host reads the low word and the high word of an incoming message. This aperture cannot fill RX, so pop-per-read and high-half loss are not graded here. RTL pops the whole 64-bit entry on any successful `READ_DATA` access (`mbox_r_pop_o`), so a 4-byte read would consume the message and drop the other half; that consequence has no DUT log. `CHK-SUBWORD-PUSH` is the write-side contract only (a 4-byte `WRITE_DATA` beat is a complete push). Closing the spec claim needs a peer-path vehicle that places a known 64-bit value in RX and issues two 4-byte `READ_DATA` beats: occupancy must drop by one on the first, and the second must not return the high half of the same entry.

|Mailbox read threshold and data round-trip (testbench reach)
|`RIRQT` and `STATUS.read_level_above` are the read half of the mailbox threshold pair, and a data round-trip readback needs a value in the receive FIFO. Both require the peer side to write the inbound aperture, which the SEP-host aperture `sep_axil_mailbox_iface_rand_test` drives cannot do; the golden therefore predicts `read_level_above` as constant false. The hardware is not suspected — the gap is testbench reach. Closing it needs the external inbound master at the inbound aperture and belongs in its own peer-path vehicle, not in the TX-path rep.

|Entropy pool pointer fault, PIC source 39 (hardware not built)
|`sep_internal_interrupts[38]` was given to the entropy pool FIFO's pointer-integrity error so that enabling the check later does not renumber the map. The check is not built in the current configuration, so the source is tied to zero and can never assert, and no vehicle grades it. `interrupts.adoc` documents the same. When it is built it is level-sensitive with no write-one-to-clear, so firmware must mask the source or scrub the pool rather than acknowledge it, and a vehicle will need a pool scrub to clear it.

|Dead-space refusal flavour (specification)
|`memory_map.adoc` names DECERR for the reserved remainder inside an aperture. `spi_controller` refuses a dead-space write with SLVERR. The access is refused either way, which is the contract `sep_fabric_deadspace_decode_test` asserts, so the flavour is reported as `DEADSPACE-FLAVOUR` for the design owner and is not failed. Which error responses are permitted is the open question.

|OSS hygiene exclusions
|No licensed/proprietary IP in the build. Bender targets stay `sep` / `sep_el2` / `sep_wrapper` only.

|Verilator covergroups
|SystemVerilog covergroups are not available under Verilator. Feature coverage is the iconic scorecard; VCS Phase 1 bins are in link:SEP_FCOV.adoc[SEP_FCOV]. RTL code coverage is collected separately and thresholded in Phase 3.

|ROM-FW ownership
|Boot-ROM firmware contracts are out of this VPLAN (separate owner / `rom_fw` class).

|Signed-off observation probes
|A small set of TB observation/inject ports is documented with the DV house rules. New backdoors need explicit sign-off.
|Behavioural OTP array
|The array behind the fuse-command interface is a simulation-only behavioural bank, not an OTP macro. Two properties the eFuse programming entries appear to establish are properties of that model rather than of hardware: write-one-to-set additivity is OR-only by construction, so a bank that overwrote instead of OR-ing cannot be represented and no-clobber is not a failable contract; and persistence across cold reset comes from the model having no reset on its storage. What is proven against real logic is that each program reached its addressed bit through the real programming path, and that the sense FSM re-reads the array after reset with the shadow tracking it.

|JTAG MMR token-block access
|`hw/sys/sep/doc/periphs.adoc` allows the MMR token block and denies the shadow, the program/read interface, and the SHIM. No checker claims DECERR on the MMR token block. Inbound ownership under correct programming (CFG, outbound CFG, remap banks, and region CSRs outside every allow window) is owned by `sep_fabric_inbound_filter_rule_matrix_test` `CHK-OWNERSHIP`.
|RTL assertions are backend-dependent
|What participates depends on the backend. Under Verilator, `prim_assert.sv`, `abr_prim_assert.sv` and `ocah_assert.svh` all gate their macros on `VERILATOR`, so design and vendored-IP SVA expand to nothing, and the SEP Verilator targets pass no `--assert`, so the two-state `ocah_axi_sva` rules are dropped as well. Under VCS those macros are live. The shared native profile also defines `COMMON_CELLS_ASSERTS_OFF`, so the PULP `common_cells` inline assertions are off on every backend. Where a checker proves a property, that checker is the whole of the proof on Verilator; no assertion layer sits underneath it there.

|Entropy-pool interrupt numbering (spec)
|`hw/sys/sep/doc/crypto.adoc` numbers the two entropy-pool interrupts one lower than the canonical table in `hw/sys/sep/doc/interrupts.adoc`. PIC source IDs are 1-based, so aggregation index 36/37 is PIC source 37/38; firmware following `crypto.adoc` would enable and mask the wrong two sources. No checker asserts this today; do not XFAIL it.

|CPU reset-vector TDR (spec)
|`hw/sys/sep/doc/cpu.adoc` documents a CPU reset-vector TDR selected by a JTAG opcode. The vendored tap implements no such register — the vector reaches the CPU through a direct wrapper input. The testbench shifts that opcode on every CPU-boot run and the sequence is inert: stimulus that reads as coverage and cannot fail. Remove it or give the claim an owner.

|Scratch bank register count (spec vs RTL)
|`hw/sys/sep/doc/cpu.adoc` requires "two sets of 16 x 32-bit scratch register banks". `hw/sys/sep/regs/blocks/sep_scratch/sep_scratch.rdl` declares `SCRATCH[8]`, so each bank implements eight registers, not sixteen. The reset-domain behaviour of the banks that exist is proven; the count is not, and no test asserts it. This row is the record of the difference, not a claimed contract. Do not XFAIL.

|AP/STEE output-remap attributes and pass-through (spec)
|`hw/sys/sep/doc/fabric.adoc` gives the AP/STEE remappers a cacheable attribute and says unprogrammed regions are transparent pass-throughs. `hw/common/axi/output_remap/rtl/output_remap.sv` has neither a valid bit nor a cacheable bit and rewrites the address unconditionally, so an unprogrammed region zeroes the upper address bits instead of passing through. The vehicle on this bank is `sep_fabric_output_remap_datapath_proxy_test`, which proves a programmed window translates and that a beat outside it is refused; the pass-through behaviour of an unprogrammed region has no checker, so this row is the record of the gap and not a claimed contract. Do not XFAIL.

This row is about the AP/STEE bank only. The local-master aliaser is a different block: `hw/common/axi/axi_alias_remap/rtl/axi_alias_remap.sv` carries `region_valid` per region, drives the outbound cache bits from the region on a hit, and passes an address through unchanged when no region hits. `sep_fabric_local_alias_datapath_test` covers the enable with `CHK-VALID-GATE`.

|Class 2 scan-root count (spec)
|`hw/sys/sep/doc/attack_countermeasures.adoc` states "exactly 10" Class 2 scan roots. The RTL instantiates an eleventh on the entropy-pool EDN leg that is absent from the list, and nothing lints the manifest against the RTL. That manifest is the sole input deciding what is never scan-dumpable.

|`sec_dis` is not forced
|No entry forces `sec_dis`. The LC→`feat_ctrl` stitch stays an end-to-end path; a forced override is not an acceptable substitute for it.

|Checkers sharing one pass line
|Three firmware tests evaluate several conditions and print a single pass line, so their rows do not fail independently of each other: `sep_spi_ot_dma_rx_test` (3 of 3 checkers), `sep_dma_cpu_contention_test` (5 of 5), `sep_mailbox_plic_test` (4 of 4). Firmware counters still gate the verdict.

|Lifecycle codes with no owner
|Closed for the encodings, narrowed for the mechanism. `sep_efuse_lcc_lc_state_stitch_test` walks four of the seven named codes on the OTP-program path (`LC_TEST_DEV`, `LC_PROD`, `LC_RMA_SIP_1`, `LC_RMA_CHIP_1`); the `LC_STATE` write-1-to-set leaves add `LC_RMA_SIP_0`, `LC_RMA_CHIP_0` and `LC_PROD_END` as sensed starting states with a `FEAT_CTRL` claim on each, plus two codes outside the named set. What remains unowned is the *pairing*: `LC_RMA_SIP_0`, `LC_RMA_CHIP_0` and `LC_PROD_END` are sensed but never reached by programming a fuse bit, because the OTP image is a t=0 deposit and a fuse survives reset — from a given image only a W1S superset is reachable in one leaf.

|Contracts not claimed (no vehicle in `all`)
|KM emergency wipe (`KM_WIPE_CTRL.wipe_state`); CPU-firmware AES SRAM round-trip; AES CTR DECRYPT; ABR sign/verify, ML-KEM-1024, and key-vault seed; entropy-pool drain-under-fill / stall-duration; isolate drain-before-reset on the four accelerator domains (the shared TRNG drain is proven); locked-field CPU PIC/ISR; ESRC health-test quality and `prim_count` redundancy fault inject; inbound-filter negative product; non-identity inbound global remap; eleven-output `dbg_disable` gate test; named negative matrices (`sep_cpu_lsu_*_matrix_test`, `sep_fabric_gap_matrix_test`, `sep_irq_extended_connectivity_test`, `sep_efuse_uvm_permission_matrix_test`); KMAC sideload fail-closed + recovery (`ErrKeyNotValid` / `CMD.err_processed`).

|===
